> **Roadmap step 4, the register of readings (`docs/ROADMAP.md`, step 4: the sourcing stage; `docs/FINAL_AUDIT.md` H-6, H-17; owner
> decisions 125 (b), 128 (b), 130, 136).** The passages read for step 4's items: the hours (decision 125 (b)), the items step 2 and step
> 3 handed on, and the claims of the narrative statements (`narrative.js`'s `STATEMENT_CLAIMS` cite these entries by their ids, the
> backquoted names in the headings). Each entry: the source (a key of `appearance.js`'s `APPEARANCE_SOURCES` where the work is there; the
> others are described in the table below and are added to `APPEARANCE_SOURCES` when a claim cites them, as `test.js` requires), the locator, the passage as printed, a translation, the grade and label, what it settles and what it does not, the map's texts
> it bears on, and its status. 210 readings by the clusters' researchers and 63 counter-readings by their source skeptics (248 read on page images, 18 on page images with part of the passage or its locator on the text layer, 7 on a text layer only), 59 negatives, 26 sources not readable from here; 65 readings accepted with corrections, none rejected. Generated from the research workflow's structured results; each correction of a source
> skeptic is shown on its own line beside the researcher's value and named as the skeptic's (the researcher's value is never replaced); the
> clusters' proposals and the skeptics' reviews of them are in `proposals.md`.

# Roadmap step 4: the register of readings (Austerlitz, 2 December 1805)

- **Compiled:** 14 October 2026, by a research workflow: one researcher per cluster (C1 the three disputed hours; C2 the other hours; C3
  Drouet, I Corps and the Guard; C4 the Zuran, the chapel, the ice and the sun; C5 the strengths and the 6B data questions; C6 the
  language, Blasowitz and the places), each reading then checked by a source skeptic who downloaded its page images afresh, read them, and
  checked the quotation word for word, the locator, the translation, the grade and what the reading is said to settle, and who looked for
  counter-evidence on neighbouring pages and in the other sources (the counter-readings below). The exceptions are named on their status
  lines: the readings made on a text layer were checked on it, not on an image, and the counter-readings were checked by no other reader.
  **Every reader was a machine reader**: nothing here was read by a person, and decision 136's human second reading is not done by this
  register.
- **The page image is the reading.** The text layer (OCR) was used to find pages. A quotation read on the text layer alone says so on its
  quote line ("read on: text" or "OCR"); one read on page images with part of the passage or its locator on the text layer says "part on the
  text layer" there; `test.js` lets no "fact" or "attributed" claim rest on either.
- **Grades** (of the passage, for the use it is put to): **A** a document dated 1805 (an order, a report, a return, a bulletin), or an
  account printed in 1806 by an officer present; **B** a participant's memoir published decades later, an official history written from
  the archives, a report quoted in such a history, a return of another date used for 2 December, or an 1806 editor's note of unstated
  origin; **C** a later account not written from the archives, or a modern local history or web page whose sources are not stated.
  **Labels** (of a reading): fact (what the source says, as it says it), disputed (another source read here says otherwise; both kept),
  inference, uncertain; a label's first word is the label (`test.js` reads it), the rest the reader's gloss ("fact; disputed (...)").
- **Hours** are the sources' local hours, as given (decision 69: the clock's basis is not established). Russian "въ 8-мъ часу" is "in the
  eighth hour", between seven and eight; Russian dates are old style. "Au jour naissant" and "à la pointe du jour" are words, not hours.
- **Transcription:** as printed, with the source's own spelling, accents and capitals; pre-1918 Russian as printed; German Fraktur's long
  s as s; "[...]" an omission; "[sic]" not used (the spelling is the source's).
- **Not read here** (modern works in copyright, cited by the map and kept "cited, not read"): Duffy 1977, Chandler 1966, Smith 1998, Goetz
  2005, Castle 2002. Not reachable from here: Gallica, HathiTrust, Hungaricana, militera.lib.ru (each named where it mattered).
- **The step-2 register** (`docs/step2-evidence/readings.md`, §1-§13) is cited by section; where a cluster's researcher or skeptic read one
  of its pages again on the image, the entry here says so, which is a second reading by a machine reader (H-17's practice).

## C1: the three disputed hours (decision 125 (b)) and question 161

### C1.1 `mat1806.p101-106.disposition`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, Zusatz 7, p. 101 (n110) heading; p. 106 (n115) the hour; images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n110.jpg, https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n115.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 101: „Disposition zur Attaque auf die feindliche Position hinter Schlapanitz und Sokolnitz am 20. Novbr. 1805 a. St. (2 Decbr. n. St.)“ (editor: „die vom General Weyrotter *) entworfene Disposition zum Angriffe am 2ten December“). p. 106: „„Das Corps des Großfürsten Constantin Pawlowitz, kais. Hoh. besetzt bei Tages-Anbruche die Position hinter Blasowitz und Kruh [...] „Alle fünf Colonnen rücken um 7 Uhr aus, wobei zu beobachten, daß nach Besetzung eines Defilees, die Tete der nächsten Colonne erwartet werden muß und überhaupt nach der vorgeschriebenen Disposition zu agiren ist.“
- **translation**: "Disposition for the attack on the enemy position behind Schlapanitz and Sokolnitz, 20 Nov. 1805 O.S. (2 Dec. N.S.)" ... "The Grand Duke Constantine's corps occupies at daybreak the position behind Blasowitz and Kruh [...] All five columns march off at 7 o'clock, observing that after a defile is taken the head of the next column must be awaited, and in general acting according to the prescribed disposition."
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The order's hour: all five columns to march off at 7.
- **does not settle**: When they did march; the clock's basis.
- **bears on**: item 1: dok@1; EVENTS[#columns-move]; PHASES[0].events[1]; PHASES[1].events[1]; FORMATIONS.dok.track[1].act; SOURCE_NOTE.body[5]; lang@1; prz@1; kienmayer@1; EVENTS[#telnitz]; TOUR[3].x; ANALYSIS[#commitment].text.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 2 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept. Heading, editor's sentence, Constantine "bei Tages-Anbruche" and "Alle fünf Colonnen rücken um 7 Uhr aus [...]" read on the images, word for word. The order of 1805 printed in 1806: A. It settles the ordered hour only, as stated. Not read by a person.

### C1.2 `mat1806.p109.delivery`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, Zusätze 8-10, p. 109 (n118); images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n118.jpg.
- **locator, as the source skeptic gives it**: Zusatz 8 (end) and Zusatz 9, p. 109 (n118); Zusatz 8 begins p. 107 (n116)
- **quote** (read on: page image): „und daher bekamen die meisten Generale die Disposition für die auf den 2ten December festgesetzte Attake erst früh 7 Uhr dieses 2ten Decembers, einige sogar erst um 9 Uhr, um welche Zeit die Schlacht schon angegangen war, die Divisionärs und Brigadiers bekamen sie gar nicht.“ Zusatz 9: „Die Cavalerie-Colonne unter Fürst Liechtenstein stand am 1sten December und während der ganzen Nacht bei dem linken Flügel der combinirten Armee [...] Erst in der Morgendämmerung erhielt sie den Befehl, diese Position zu verlassen“
- **translation**: "and therefore most generals received the disposition for the attack fixed for 2 December only at 7 in the morning of that day, some only at 9, by which time the battle had already begun; the divisional and brigade commanders did not receive it at all." (Zusatz 9) "Liechtenstein's cavalry column stood on 1 December and all night by the left wing of the combined army [...] Only at dawn did it receive the order to leave this position"
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The editor's account: the disposition reached most generals only at 7; Liechtenstein's order to move at dawn.
- **does not settle**: Its source; a march hour.
- **bears on**: item 1: dok@1; EVENTS[#columns-move]; PHASES[0].events[1]; PHASES[1].events[1]; FORMATIONS.dok.track[1].act; SOURCE_NOTE.body[5]; EVENTS[#counter-march].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 1 freshly downloaded image: quotation exact, accept with corrections. Both quotations exact on p. 109. The delivery sentence closes Zusatz 8 (begun p. 107) and Liechtenstein's is Zusatz 9; Zusatz 10 on the same page is the Moniteur's remarks, not part of the reading. Zusatz 8 also has Weyrother dictating at 8 p.m. and presenting to Kutuzov 9-10 p.m. (p. 107, read: counter-reading materialien1806.p107.dictated-eight). B ok. Not read by a person.

### C1.3 `mat1806.p114-118.guardreport`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, Zusatz 13, p. 114 (n123), p. 118 (n127); images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n123.jpg, https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n127.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 114: „Ein officieller, ungedruckter Bericht verdient hier mit der nun folgenden Erzählung des Vfs. verglichen zu werden“ [...] „„Nach der Disposition formirte sich am 2ten December früh 7 Uhr die Armee in 4 Colonnen, um den rechten Flügel der feindlichen Armee anzugreifen.“ p. 118: „Noch war die Queue der Colonne vor dieser aufmarschirten Abtheilung nicht vorbei defilirt, als der Feind mit 2000 Mann Infanterie und einigen Abtheilungen Cavallerie nebst Geschütz aus dem Dorfe Pratzen vorrückte und die aufgestellten 2 Bataillons angriff, er wurde aber durch einige gut angebrachte Dechargen, die ihn in Unordnung brachten und die hierauf erfolgte Attake der 2 Escadrons Garde à Cheval, die ein ganzes Bataillon zusammen hieben, mit beträchtlichem Verluste zurückgeschlagen; bei welcher Gelegenheit die beiden Escadrons einen Adler (die einzige Trophäe des Tags) des 4ten Linien-Regiments eroberten. — Kaum waren die Garden auf der anbefohlnen Anhöhe von Krzenowitz aufmarschirt, als der Feind mit dreifacher Uebermacht gegen die Fronte und beide Flanken anrückte“
- **translation**: "An official, unprinted report deserves comparison here" ... "By the disposition the army formed on 2 December at 7 in the morning in 4 columns to attack the enemy's right wing." ... "The tail of the column had not yet filed past this deployed detachment when the enemy advanced from Pratzen village with 2,000 infantry [...] and attacked the 2 battalions posted there; but [...] the attack of the 2 squadrons of the Garde à Cheval, which cut down a whole battalion, drove him back with heavy loss; on this occasion the two squadrons took an eagle (the only trophy of the day) of the 4th line regiment. Hardly had the Guards formed on the ordered height of Krzenowitz when the enemy advanced with threefold superiority"
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: An official report of unstated author: the army formed at 7; the eagle taken by two Horse Guard squadrons while the Guard's column was still filing past, before it formed on the Krzenowitz height.
- **does not settle**: A clock hour for the eagle or the Guard's fight.
- **bears on**: item 1: dok@1; EVENTS[#columns-move]; PHASES[0].events[1]; PHASES[1].events[1]; FORMATIONS.dok.track[1].act; SOURCE_NOTE.body[5]; item 3: guard_cav@6; rg_cav@6; rg_inf@6; EVENTS[#guard-broken]; EVENTS[#guard-attack]; PHASES[6].events[0]; PHASES[6].events[1]; FORMATIONS.guard_cav.track[6].act; question 161; SOURCE_NOTE.body[5].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 2 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept. Quotations exact. B ok (official unprinted report of unstated author, quoted by the 1806 editor). The report places the eagle while the Guard column was still filing past toward its ordered height by Krzenowitz: the eagle as the Guard came into line, before it had formed. No clock; it bounds nothing in hours. Not read by a person.

### C1.4 `stut1806de.p49-52.telnitz`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, pp. 49, 51, 52 (n52, n54, n55); images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n52.jpg, https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n54.jpg, https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n55.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 49: „Um 7 Uhr Morgens setzte sich die combinirte Armee in Bewegung, und verließ die Anhöhen bey Pratzen, um auf ihre bestimmten Puncte vorzurücken. [...] Das Gefecht fing auf dem linken Flügel der Alliirten an. Das Corps des General Kienmayer [...] war dem Feinde am nächsten, und bestimmt durch das Defilee von Tellnitz zu brechen, um für die erste Colonne reine Bahn zu machen. [...] zwischen 7 und 8 Uhr rückten einige Escadronen Husaren vor, um den Feind zu recognosciren.“ p. 51: „Dieses Gefecht dauerte schon über eine Stunde, und noch sah man die Spitze der ersten Colonne nicht ankommen, mit welcher Herr von Buxhoevden marschirte.“ p. 52: „Endlich rückte Herr von Buxhoevden mit der ersten Colonne über Aujest hervor [...] Gegen 9 Uhr erhielt der Feind eine Verstärkung von 4000 Mann vom Corps des Marschall Davoust, welche von dem Kloster Reygern herbeyeilten.“
- **translation**: "At 7 in the morning the combined army set itself in motion and left the heights by Pratzen [...] The fighting began on the Allied left wing. Kienmayer's corps [...] was nearest the enemy and was to break through the Telnitz defile to clear the way for the first column [...] between 7 and 8 some squadrons of hussars came forward to reconnoitre." "This fight had already lasted over an hour, and still the head of the first column, with which Buxhowden marched, was not seen arriving." "At last Buxhowden came forward with the first column over Augezd [...] About 9 the enemy received a reinforcement of 4,000 men of Davout's corps"
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Stutterheim's sequence: the army moves at 7; Kienmayer's fight over an hour before the I Column's head appears over Augezd; Davout's 4,000 c. 9. (p. 49 re-read of step-2 §2.2.)
- **does not settle**: When the I Column left its camp exactly.
- **bears on**: item 1: dok@1; EVENTS[#columns-move]; PHASES[0].events[1]; PHASES[1].events[1]; FORMATIONS.dok.track[1].act; SOURCE_NOTE.body[5]; lang@1; prz@1; kienmayer@1; EVENTS[#telnitz]; TOUR[3].x; ANALYSIS[#commitment].text.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 3 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept. Every cut quotation exact ("Buxhoevden", "Aujest" as printed). A ok. "Um 7 Uhr" is the whole army's hour, as the researcher says, not the I Column's own. Not read by a person.

### C1.5 `stut1806de.p60-61.ninehours`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, pp. 60-61 (n63, n64); images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n63.jpg, https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n64.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 60: „Um den Colonnen des linken Flügels Zeit zum vorrücken zu lassen, erhielt der General-Lieutenant Kollowrath, welcher diese 4te Colonne commandirte, Befehl, sich erst gegen 8 Uhr in Marsch zu setzen. Das Gefecht bey Tellnitz war also schon angefangen, und der linke Flügel schon auf dem Marsche, als das Centrum sich formirte [...] Es war ungefähr 9 Uhr, und die dritte Colonne hatte eben die Anhöhe bey Pratzen verlassen, um der gemachten Disposition zu Folge nach Sokolnitz zu marschiren; die 4te Colonne langte auf dem Terrain an, welches der General Przybyszewsky die Nacht über besetzt hatte, als man plötzlich eine große Masse Französischer Infanterie in einer Vertiefung vorwärts von Pratzen bemerkte.“ p. 61: „Indessen eilte die Avantgarde der 4ten Colonne das Dorf Pratzen zu besetzen [...] und stellte eines ihrer Bataillone auf eine Anhöhe links vor dem Dorfe, wo sich noch das Ende der 3ten Colonne befand“
- **translation**: "To give the left-wing columns time to advance, Kollowrat [...] was ordered to set out only towards 8. The fight at Telnitz had thus already begun, and the left wing already on the march, when the centre formed [...] It was about 9, and the third column had just left the height by Pratzen to march to Sokolnitz; the 4th column reached the ground Przybyszewski had held overnight, when suddenly a great mass of French infantry was seen in a hollow in front of Pratzen." "[...] a height left of the village, where the tail of the 3rd column still was"
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: At about 9 the III Column had only just left the height by Pratzen, its tail still there; the 4th column ordered to march towards 8.

### C1.6 `stut1806de.p63.kamensky`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, p. 63 (n66); images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n66.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): „Der Feind entfaltete nach und nach seine Massen, stellte sich in mehreren Linien in Schlachtordnung, und marschirte rasch nach der Anhöhe, indem er seinen linken Flügel an die Kirche des Dorfs, und den rechten an den erhabensten Punct dieser Anhöhen lehnte. Dort formirte er einen Haken, um sich dem Ende der 3ten Colonne entgegen zu stellen. Es war die Brigade des General Kamensky, welche von dieser Colonne getrennt war, und welche auf der Anhöhe Front gemacht hatte, und den rechten Flügel des Marschall Soult bedrohte.“
- **translation**: "The enemy gradually unfolded his masses [...] and marched rapidly to the height, resting his left on the village church and his right on the highest point of these heights. There he formed a hook to face the tail of the 3rd column. It was General Kamensky's brigade, which was separated from this column, and which had made front on the height and threatened Marshal Soult's right wing."
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Sequence: after the French were seen c. 9 (p. 60), Kamensky's brigade had already made front on the height when Soult formed his hook; it precedes Jurczek's and Rottermund's attack (pp. 63-64) and the Fanagoria and 'Rhiasky' regiments' reinforcement on the commander-in-chief's order (p. 64, step-2 §2.6). Stutterheim numbers the column the 3rd.
- **does not settle**: A clock hour; who turned the brigade (no agent: 'Front gemacht hatte').

### C1.10 `md1844.p186-187.kamensky~C1.10`

- **id**: renamed (its reader gave the id `md1844.p186-187.kamensky`, which the step-2 register's entry of the same page has; renamed so each id is one reading; a mention of the bare id in a reader's text means this entry).
- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, pp. 185 (OCR), 186-187 (IIIF k=197, 198); second reading of step-2 §6.5; images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$197/full/1100,/0/default.jpg, https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$198/full/1100,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image, part on the text layer (see the source line)): p. 186: «Когда Французы опрокидывали нашихъ съ горъ, откуда не являлось возможности сбить ихъ, при разстройствѣ и малочисленности союзныхъ войскъ въ центрѣ, не имѣвшемъ резервовъ, Кутузовъ поѣхалъ къ лѣвому крылу четвертой колонны. Подымаясь на гору, онъ нашелъ тамъ бригаду Графа / Каменскаго 1-го, полки Фанагорійскій и Ряжскій, очутившіеся на горѣ по слѣдующему случаю: идучи въ замкѣ колонны Графа Ланжерона, къ Сокольницу, Графъ Каменскій увидѣлъ вправо Французовъ, входившихъ на Праценскія высоты. Извѣстя о томъ Графа Ланжерона, онъ остановилъ бригаду, построилъ ее отвѣсно противъ праваго крыла Французовъ, и атаковалъ, желая помочь 4-й колоннѣ. (*) Въ то мгновеніе, когда пріѣхалъ къ нему Кутузовъ съ Дежурнымъ Генераломъ, Княземъ Волконскимъ, бригада Графа Каменскаго была опрокинута.» Footnote: «(*) Донесеніе Графа Ланжерона Кутузову, отъ 2[3 or 5]-го Ноября, № 867.» (p. 185, OCR only: «Во время сихъ дѣйствій, продолжавшихся часа два»)
- **translation**: "When the French were throwing ours off the heights [...] Kutuzov rode to the left wing of the fourth column. Climbing the hill he found there Count Kamensky I's brigade, the Fanagoria and Ryazhsk regiments, which had come to be there as follows: marching at the rear of Langeron's column toward Sokolnitz, Kamensky saw on his right the French climbing onto the Pratzen heights. Having informed Langeron, he halted the brigade, formed it perpendicular against the French right and attacked, wishing to help the 4th column. At the moment Kutuzov arrived with the duty general, Prince Volkonsky, Kamensky's brigade had been overthrown." (p. 185: "during these actions, which lasted about two hours")
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact; disputed (who turned the brigade).
- **settles**: M-D's sequence: the turn on seeing the French climb; Kutuzov's arrival only after the 4th column was thrown off the heights.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: "Kutuzov's arrival only after the 4th column was thrown off the heights": the text says "Когда Французы опрокидывали нашихъ съ горъ" (imperfective: while the French were throwing ours off the heights), not after.
- **does not settle**: Any clock hour; the footnote's day (23 or 25).

### C1.11 `md1844.p194-196.guard`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, pp. 194-196 (IIIF k=205-207); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$205/full/1100,/0/default.jpg, https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$206/full/1100,/0/default.jpg, https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$207/full/1100,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 194: «Конная гвардія освободила пѣхоту отъ непріятельскихъ всадниковъ. Первые два эскадрона, подъ начальствомъ Полковника Оленина, ударили во флангъ Французамъ, обратили ихъ назадъ, и въ преслѣдованіи наскакали на батальонъ 4-го линейнаго полка, изрубили его и взяли знамя — единственный трофей Союзниковъ въ Аустерлицкомъ сраженіи. [...] Наши перешли въ безпорядкѣ за ручей. Въ ту минуту подоспѣли Кавалергарды и Лейбъ-казаки, получившіе при началѣ сраженія приказаніе поспѣшать на рысяхъ. [...] Атаки длились съ четверть часа, и стоили Кавалергардамъ убитыми и ранеными 15 офицеровъ, 200 рядовыхъ и 300 лошадей» p. 195: «Особенно пострадали 4-й и 5-й эскадроны [...] схваченные съ двухъ сторонъ конными гренадерами и егерями Наполеоновой гвардіи. [...] Командиръ его, Князь Рѣпнинъ, и нѣсколько офицеровъ, всѣ раненые, достались въ плѣнъ.» p. 196: «Атакою фланговъ нашихъ начинается второе дѣйствіе Аустерлицкаго сраженія. Былъ часъ по полудни.»
- **translation**: (p. 194) "The Horse Guard freed the infantry from the enemy horsemen. The first two squadrons under Colonel Olenin struck the French in the flank [...] rode onto a battalion of the 4th line regiment, cut it down and took the standard, the Allies' only trophy at Austerlitz. [...] Ours crossed the stream in disorder. At that moment the Chevalier Guards and the Life Cossacks came up [...] The attacks lasted about a quarter of an hour and cost the Chevalier Guards 15 officers, 200 men and 300 horses" (p. 195) "[...] caught from two sides by the horse grenadiers and chasseurs of Napoleon's guard [...] Prince Repnin and several officers, all wounded, were taken prisoner." (p. 196) "With the attack on our flanks the second act of the battle begins. It was one o'clock in the afternoon."
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: M-D: the eagle, then the Chevalier Guard's quarter-hour and Repnin's capture, all before the second act at one o'clock.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: M-D does not say the Guard's fight ended before one: "Былъ часъ по полудни" dates the beginning of the "second act" (Napoleon's attack on the flanks). "Before one" is an inference from the narrative's order and must be labelled so.
- **does not settle**: A clock hour for the Guard's attack itself (a bound only: before one).

### C1.13 `md1846.p261-262.chevaliers-one`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1846`, pp. 261-262 (n287, n288); images: https://archive.org/download/relationdelacam00dangoog/page/n287.jpg, https://archive.org/download/relationdelacam00dangoog/page/n288.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 261: «Ces attaques, qui ne durèrent pas plus d'un quart d'heure, coûtèrent aux chevaliers-gardes, en tués et blessés : 16 officiers, 200 cuirassiers et 300 chevaux; mais elles donnèrent à l'infanterie de la garde le temps de sortir des ravins. Les troisième et quatrième escadrons, qui chargèrent la cavalerie française pendant que cette dernière était aux prises avec le régiment de Séménowski, furent les plus maltraités. Pris en flanc et en front par les grenadiers à cheval et par les chasseurs-guides de Napoléon [...] Le commandant, le prince Repnine, ainsi que plusieurs officiers, tous blessés, furent faits prisonniers.» p. 262: «Le second acte du drame d'Austerlitz s'ouvrit par l'attaque de nos flancs. Il était une heure après midi»
- **translation**: "These attacks, which lasted no more than a quarter of an hour, cost the chevaliers-gardes 16 officers, 200 cuirassiers and 300 horses [...] The third and fourth squadrons [...] were worst handled [...] Prince Repnin [...] taken prisoner." - **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The 1846 translation agrees on the quarter of an hour and one o'clock; it differs from the 1844 original twice: 16 officers (1844: 15) and the third and fourth squadrons (1844: 4th and 5th).
- **does not settle**: A clock hour for the charge.

### C1.14 `rapp1823.p60-62.une-heure`

- **source**: `rapp1823`, pp. 60 (n79), 62 (n81); p. 61 OCR; images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugnralr00rappgoog/page/n79_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugnralr00rappgoog/page/n81_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image, part on the text layer (see the source line)): p. 60: «Nous arrivâmes à Austerlitz. Les Russes avaient des forces supérieures aux nôtres; ils avaient replié nos avant-gardes et nous croyaient déjà vaincus. L'action s'engagea; mais, au lieu de ces succès faciles que leur garde seule devait obtenir, ils trouvèrent partout une résistance opiniâtre. Il était déjà une heure, et la bataille était loin de se décider pour eux. Ils résolurent de tenter au centre un dernier effort. La garde impériale se déploya; infanterie, cavalerie, artillerie, marchèrent sur le pont sans que Napoléon aperçût ce mouvement, que lui dérobaient les accidents du terrain. Un feu de mousqueterie se fit bientôt entendre, c'était une brigade commandée par le général Schinner que les Russes enfonçaient. Napoléon m'ordonna de prendre les Mamelouks, deux escadrons de chasseurs, un de grenadiers de la garde, et de me porter en avant pour reconnaître l'état des choses.» p. 62: «Les canons, le bagage, le prince Repnin, étaient dans nos mains; malheureusement nous avions un bon nombre d'hommes hors de combat, le colonel Morland n'était plus, et j'avais moi-même un coup de pointe dans la tête.»
- **translation**: "It was already one o'clock, and the battle was far from being decided in their favour. They resolved to attempt a last effort in the centre. The [Russian] imperial guard deployed; infantry, cavalry, artillery marched on the bridge without Napoleon seeing the movement [...] Soon musketry was heard: it was a brigade under General Schinner that the Russians were breaking. Napoleon ordered me to take the Mamelukes, two squadrons of chasseurs, one of grenadiers of the guard, and to go forward to see how things stood." "The guns, the baggage, Prince Repnin were in our hands; [...] Colonel Morland was no more, and I had myself a sword-thrust in the head."
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: The charge's leader puts one o'clock BEFORE the Russian Guard's last effort: his charge and Repnin's capture after 13:00.
- **does not settle**: The clock's basis; the order of the eagle (not named on these pages).
- **bears on**: item 3: guard_cav@6; rg_cav@6; rg_inf@6; EVENTS[#guard-broken]; EVENTS[#guard-attack]; PHASES[6].events[0]; PHASES[6].events[1]; FORMATIONS.guard_cav.track[6].act; question 161; SOURCE_NOTE.body[5].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 9 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept with corrections. Quotations exact on pp. 60 and 62. B ok (posthumous: archive.org metadata gives creator "Rapp, Jean, comte, 1772-1821" and Bossange frères, 1823, "publiés par sa famille"). The title leaf was not found on n5-n13 (n8 the series title "Mémoires des contemporains", n9 the imprint verso, n11 a portrait): the src_new's "title leaf n6" is not confirmed by this reading; take title and imprint from the metadata and say so. Rapp's hour is narrative: "Il était déjà une heure" precedes the Allied resolve for a last effort, so in his telling his charge comes after one; it is not a stated clock for the charge. His brigade broken is "Schinner's"; no eagle on these pages. Not read by a person.

### C1.15 `corr11.9541.p451.guard-one`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9541 (30th Bulletin, dated 3 December 1805), p. 451 (n460); extends step-2 §1.5; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n460.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): «Le maréchal Soult s'ébranle au même instant, se dirige sur les hauteurs du village de Pratzen [...] Il n'y avait pas une heure que l'on se battait, et toute la gauche de l'ennemi était coupée. Sa droite se trouvait déjà arrivée à Austerlitz, quartier général des deux empereurs, qui durent faire marcher sur-le-champ la garde de l'empereur de Russie pour tâcher de rétablir la communication du centre avec la gauche. Un bataillon du 4e de ligne fut chargé par la garde impériale russe à cheval, et culbuté; mais l'Empereur n'était pas loin; il s'aperçut de ce mouvement, il ordonna au maréchal Bessières de se porter au secours de sa droite avec ses invincibles, et bientôt les deux gardes furent aux mains. Le succès ne pouvait être douteux : dans un moment la garde russe fut en déroute [...] A une heure après midi la victoire était décidée.»
- **translation**: "It was not an hour since the fighting began, and the whole enemy left was cut off. [...] the two emperors [...] had at once to send forward the Russian emperor's guard to restore the communication of the centre with the left. A battalion of the 4th of the line was charged by the Russian imperial horse guard and overthrown; but the Emperor [...] ordered Marshal Bessières to the help of his right with his invincibles, and soon the two guards were at grips. [...] in a moment the Russian guard was routed [...] At one o'clock in the afternoon the victory was decided."
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact; disputed against Rapp and Ségur.
- **settles**: The Bulletin's bound: the Guard fight was over before one o'clock, when 'the victory was decided'.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: does_not_settle says "pas une heure" measures only the time to cut the enemy left; but the same sentence chain sends the Russian Guard "sur-le-champ" after that first hour, so in the Bulletin's narrative the Guard was committed early (an inference from order, not a clock), not merely "before one". "A une heure après midi la victoire était décidée" follows Bernadotte, Lannes and the cuirassiers: a bound by order, not a clock for the Guard fight.
- **does not settle**: A clock hour for the Guard fight itself; 'pas une heure' measures the time to cut the enemy left, not the Guard's hour.

### C1.17 `schoenhals1873.p157-159.departure`

- **source**: `schoenhals1873`, pp. 157-159 (n161 foot, n162, n163); images: https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n161.jpg, https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n162.jpg, https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n163.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): pp. 157-158: „Die Stunde des Auf- / bruchs für alle Colonnen, mit Ausnahme Bagrations, war 7 Uhr Früh; doch sollte jede Colonne, ehe sie nach erzwungenem Defilé weiter rückte, das Vorbrechen der Spitze der linken abwarten“ p. 159: „Um halb 7 Uhr Früh setzte sich der linke Flügel des russisch-österreichischen Heeres nach der gegebenen Disposition in Bewegung. Kienmayer zog seine Vorposten ein [...] und rückte von Augezd gegen Tellnitz vor [...] Das Gefecht dauerte bereits eine Stunde; der Feind hatte noch bedeutende Reserven von der Division Legrand hinter dem Dorfe. - **translation**: "The hour of departure for all columns except Bagration's was 7 in the morning" ... [...] It was about 9 when Friant attacked the village anew"
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.

### C1.18 `schoenhals1873.p160.half-past-seven`

- **source**: `schoenhals1873`, p. 160 (n164); images: https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n164.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): „Unterdessen hatten auch um halb 8 Uhr die zweite und dritte Colonne ihre Lagerplätze verlassen. Erstere marschirte die Schlucht entlang, welche von der Höhe von Pratze gegen das Schloss von Sokolnitz führt [...] Während dessen erschien auch die Spitze der dritten, die durch Pratze, Kobelnitz rechts lassend, gezogen war, und rückte in Sokolnitz ein. [...] Wir werden aus dem Verlaufe der Schlacht sehen, dass die Höhen von Pratze bereits angegriffen waren, als die Spitze dieser Colonne bei Sokolnitz erschien [...] Jetzt zerstreute sich der Nebel und liess Napoleon die Lage der Dinge überblicken. In jenem Augenblicke, es mochte 9 Uhr Früh“
- **translation**: "Meanwhile at half past seven the second and third columns had also left their camping places. [...] the head of the third [...] entered Sokolnitz [...] the heights of Pratze were already under attack when the head of this column appeared at Sokolnitz [...] Now the fog dispersed [...] At that moment, it may have been 9 in the morning"
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: II and III Columns left camp at half past seven; the III Column's head reached Sokolnitz only after the Pratzen attack had begun.
- **does not settle**: The I Column's hour.

### C1.19 `schoenhals1873.p161.kamensky-langeron`

- **source**: `schoenhals1873`, p. 161 (n165); images: https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n165.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): „[es mochte 9 Uhr Früh] / sein, wo die drei ersten Colonnen bereits in dem Defilé verwickelt waren, und die beiden andern sich in Bewegung setzten [...] gab er den Befehl zum Angriff. [...] Die vierte Colonne [...] brach, um den drei ersten Colonnen Zeit zu lassen, etwa eine halbe Stunde später als diese auf. [...] Eben verliess der Nachzug der dritten Colonne die Höhe rechts vor Pratze, und die Spitze der vierten erreichte den verlassenen Lagerplatz derselben, als man das schnelle Hervorrücken zweier feindlicher Colonnen, aus den Divisionen Vandamme und St. Hilaire bestehend, jenseits Pratze bemerkte [...] Gegen die Brigade Kamenskoi, — welche General Langeron, der des Feindes Vordringen gegen die Höhe bemerkte, der vierten Colonne zu Hilfe sandte, — liess Soult die in Reserve gehaltene Brigade Levasseur [...] St. Hilaire's rechten Flügel unterstützen“ (signature line: „Österr. militär. ### C1.21 `schoenhals1873.p164-165.guard-noon`

- **source**: `schoenhals1873`, pp. 164-165 (n168, n169); images: https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n168.jpg, https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n169.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 164: „Der Feind hatte Blaziowitz angegriffen und das Garde-Jäger-Bataillon, welches es vertheidigte, herausgeworfen. Der Grossfürst rückte von der Höhe herab dem Feind entgegen [...] als in diesem entscheidenden Augenblicke die Reiterei der französischen Garden unter Bessières [...] sich auf die russische Linie stürzte und sie durchritt. Das Garde-Regiment zu Pferd des Grossfürsten Constantin [...] hieb ein Bataillon des 4. Regiments und ein leichtes des 24. Regiments zusammen und nahm seinen Adler. Napoleon, der, seiner Reserve voraus, sich bei Blaziowitz befand, befahl dem General-Adjutanten Rapp, sich an der Spitze seiner Mameluken, dann zweier Jäger- und einer Grenadier-Escadron dem Feinde entgegenzuwerfen.“ p. 165: „Fürst Reppnin, Oberst der Reiter-Garde, fiel verwundet in die Hände der Franzosen. [...] Mehrere Stunden dauerte nun schon der Kampf ohne Entscheidung, als gegen Mittag, nachdem die Höhen von Pratze verloren, und die 5. Colonne nebst den Garden zum Weichen gebracht worden waren, die feindliche Division Caffarelli in Colonnen gegen den linken Flügel Bagration's vordrang.“
- **translation**: "The enemy had attacked Blasowitz and thrown out the Guard jäger battalion [...] the cavalry of the French guards under Bessières [...] rode through the Russian line. The Grand Duke's Horse Guard regiment [...] cut down a battalion of the 4th and a light one of the 24th and took its eagle. Napoleon [...] ordered Rapp to throw himself on the enemy at the head of his Mamelukes, two squadrons of chasseurs and one of grenadiers." "Prince Repnin, colonel of the Horse Guard [sic], fell wounded into French hands. [...] when towards noon, after the heights of Pratze had been lost and the 5th column with the Guards had given way, Caffarelli's division advanced against Bagration's left"
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact; disputed against Rapp and Ségur.
- **settles**: Schönhals: the Guard's fight after Blasowitz's fall, and over (the Guards 'zum Weichen gebracht') before 'gegen Mittag'.
- **does not settle**: A clock hour for the charge.

### C1.22 `kutuzov1951.p226.disposition-seven`

- **source**: `kutuzov1951`, no. 233, p. 226 (n254); editors' note 7, pp. 226-227 (n254, n255); images: https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n254.jpg, https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n255.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): «Всем пяти колоннам выступить в 7 часов, причем наблюдать, чтобы после овладения каким-нибудь дефилеем дожидаться головы ближайшей и действовать по предписанной диспозиции.» Archive note: «ЦГВИА, ф. ВУА, д. 3117, ч. I, лл. 50—53 об. Копия. Опублик. в книге Леера, Подробный конспект войны 1805 года. Аустерлицкая операция, СПБ., 1888 г., стр. 74—77.» Editors' note 7: «Настоящая диспозиция была составлена генерал-квартирмейстером Соединенной армии Вейротером. В 2 часа в ночь на 20 ноября старшие начальники были собраны в квартире М. И. Кутузова в Кржижановице [...] Начальник канцелярии П. М. Волконского майор К. Ф. Толь перевел ее с немецкого на русский язык и лишь к шести часам утра она была доставлена в войска (см. реляцию А. Ф. Ланжерона Александру I о сражении при Аустерлице — «Военный сборник», № 11, 1900 г., приложения).»
- **translation**: "All five columns to set out at 7 o'clock, observing that after taking any defile they wait for the head of the nearest and act by the prescribed disposition." Note 7: "at 2 in the night of 20 November the senior commanders were gathered at Kutuzov's quarters [...] Major Toll translated it into Russian and only by six in the morning was it delivered to the troops (see Langeron's relation to Alexander I, Voennyi sbornik no. 11, 1900, appendices)."
- **grade**: A (the order; an archive copy); C (the editors' note). **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The Russian archive text of the disposition gives the same 7 o'clock as the Materialien's German.
- **does not settle**: When the columns marched.

### C1.23 `kutuzov1951.p230.miloradovich-eight`

- **source**: `kutuzov1951`, no. 238, Miloradovich to Kutuzov, 3 December [O.S.] 1805, p. 230 (n258); images: https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n258.jpg.

- **translation**: "On 20 November 1805, being appointed [...] to be in the fourth column with the Austrian regiments, I set out according to the disposition at eight o'clock in the morning from the camp [...] But as soon as this advance guard had passed the village of Sokolnitz [as printed], not far from the camp, it was met by two enemy columns"

### C1.24 `kutuzov1951.p241-242.buxhowden`

- **source**: `kutuzov1951`, appendix to no. 245, Buxhowden to Kutuzov, '[1805 г. декабрь]', pp. 241-242 (n269, n270); images: https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n269.jpg, https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n270.jpg.
- **locator, as the source skeptic gives it**: appendix to no. 245 (no. 245: Kutuzov to Alexander I, 26 Dec. 1805), Buxhowden to Kutuzov, undated ("[1805 г. декабрь]", editors), pp. 241-242 (n269, n270)
- **quote** (read on: page image (the '8' on a full-resolution crop)): p. 241: «1-я же колонна в продолжении 7 часов поражала побеждаемого неприятеля. Полки сей 1-й колонны, при которой я н[аходился], из лагеря выступили поутру в 7 часов ———³ с горы и, про[шед] деревню Аугест, всту[пили] ———³ Теллиц ———⁴ маршалами Сульт и Девуста⁵ [...] По упорном сражении принудили мы неприятеля ретироваться к деревне Теллиц» p. 242: «Между тем 2-я колонна, состоявшая под командою генерал-лейтенанта графа Ланжерона, спустилась с горы в 8 часов и, приближась к деревне Сокольниц, хотя из оной вытеснила неприятеля, но вскоре по[том] обойдена им в тыл [...] где однакож Фанагорийской гранодерской полк под предводительством генерал-майора графа Каменского 1-го с отменною храбростию оборонялся [...] как во 2 часу пополудни получил я от вашего высокопревосходительства известие, [что] и все прочие кол[онны], будучи разбиты, уже отс[тупили]»
- **translation**: "The regiments of this 1st column, with which I was, set out from the camp at 7 in the morning [word lost] from the hill and, having passed the village of Augezd, entered [...] Telnitz [...]" "Meanwhile the 2nd column under Count Langeron came down from the hill at 8 o'clock and, approaching Sokolnitz [...] the Fanagoria grenadier regiment under Count Kamensky I defended itself with exceptional bravery [...] when in the second hour after noon [between 1 and 2] I received from Your Excellency the news that all the other columns, being beaten, had already withdrawn"

### C1.25 `kutuzov1951.p255-258.relation`

- **source**: `kutuzov1951`, no. 247, Kutuzov's relation to Alexander I, 14 January 1806 [O.S.] (header OCR only), pp. 255-258 (n283-n286); images: https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n283.jpg, https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n284.jpg, https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n285.jpg, https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n286.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image, part on the text layer (see the source line)): p. 255: «Мы выступили в 7 часов утра 20 ноября из позиций наших, бывших в окружностях Аустерлица. На левом фланге находился генерал от инфантерии граф Буксгевден, а я в центре при четвертой колонне.» p. 256: «Согласно с таковым планом первая колонна, спустясь с горы и прошед деревню Аугест, около 8 часов утра, по упорном сражении принудила неприятеля ретироваться к деревне Тельниц» p. 257: «Но когда генерал-майоры Берг и Репнинский были ранены, тогда бригада их, оставшись без начальников, пришла в замешательство, а с нею и прочие начали отступать. В сие время видя, что силы неприятеля, устремленные на наш центр, были вчетверо или впятеро многочисленнее наших [...] я немедленно туда поехал для принятия нужных мер [...] Поднимаясь на гору, нашел я Фанагорийский и Ряжский¹ полки отрезанными от вто- / [p. 258] рой колонны. Построив оба полка, приказал я генерал-майору графу Каменскому немедленно занять оными хребет гор, по бокам которых тянулся неприятель. [...] стояли в виду неприятеля до половины 4-го часа.» Note 1: «В опубликованном тексте ошибочно указано наименование полка — «Рижский».»
- **translation**: "We set out at 7 in the morning of 20 November from our positions around Austerlitz." "[...] the first column, having come down the hill and passed Augezd, at about 8 in the morning after a stubborn fight forced the enemy to retire to Telnitz" "But when Major-Generals Berg and Repninsky were wounded, their brigade [...] fell into confusion and with it the others began to retreat. At this time [...] I rode there at once [...] Climbing the hill I found the Fanagoria and Ryazhsk regiments cut off from the second column. Having formed both regiments, I ordered Major-General Count Kamensky to occupy the ridge at once [...] [they] stood in sight of the enemy until half past three."
- **grade**: B (dated 14 January 1806: outside the brief's A by its letter, though the commander-in-chief's own report; the Materialien translation of the same text was graded A in step 2, §3.4: the synthesizer should decide). **label**: fact; disputed (who turned Kamensky).
- **grade, as the source skeptic corrects it**: A (printed 1806; written for publication at the Tsar's request, no. 242)
- **settles**: Kutuzov: the army set out at 7; the I Column at Telnitz c. 8; his order to Kamensky came after the 4th column's brigades broke (Berg and Repninsky wounded).
- **does not settle**: A clock hour for the order to Kamensky.

### C1.26 `kutuzov1951.p258.relation-guard`

- **source**: `kutuzov1951`, no. 247, p. 258 (n286); images: https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n286.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): «Когда ж вторая, третья и четвертая колонны наши, также Фанагорийский и Ряжский¹ полки отступили и неприятель занял между тем высоты, то его высочество государь цесаревич, предводительствуя гвардиею вашего императорского величества, повел на него атаку, дабы удержать, колико возможно, его стремление. [...] при чем Конный полк лейб-гвардии отбил у неприятеля штандарт, упорно им защищаемый. [...] Сему весьма много способствовал Кавалергардский полк, которой с чрезвычайною стремительностию ударил на неприятельскую кавалерию в то самое время, когда она готовилась напасть на ретирующийся гвардии корпус»
- **translation**: "When our second, third and fourth columns, and the Fanagoria and Ryazhsk regiments, had retreated and the enemy had meanwhile occupied the heights, the Tsesarevich, leading the guard, led an attack on him [...] whereby the Life Guard Horse regiment took from the enemy a standard [...] The Chevalier Guard regiment contributed much, striking the enemy cavalry at the very moment it was preparing to fall on the retreating guard corps"
- **grade**: B (as the relation above). **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Kutuzov's sequence: the Guard attacked after Kamensky's regiments and the 4th column had withdrawn and the French held the heights.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: The relation places the Guard's attack after "вторая, третья и четвертая колонны наши, также Фанагорийский и Ряжский полки отступили": after the II and III Columns' retreat too, not only the 4th's and Kamensky's. Read literally that is a late placing (the II and III were beaten after noon; Buxhowden heard "во 2 часу пополудни" that all columns had withdrawn), so the relation cannot be counted on the "before one" side; it is a summary order, not a clock (inference).
- **does not settle**: A clock hour.

### C1.27 `kutuzov1951.p269-270.przybyszewski`

- **source**: `kutuzov1951`, appendix to no. 255, Przybyszewski to Alexander I, 11 July 1806, pp. 269-270 (n297, n298); images: https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n297.jpg, https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n298.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 269: «Минувшего 1805 года ноября 20 дня поутру в 7 часов, сходно с диспозициею главнокомандующего армиею господина генерала от инфантерии Голенищева-Кутузова, выступила вся 3-я колонна с лагеря, бывшего при деревне Праце, и, пройдя сквозь ту деревню, шла по показанию наших и австрийских колонновожатых офицеров не дорогою, а по пашням, наполненным рвами, где, чтобы можно было перевозить артиллерию, должно было три раза поправлять дорогу пионерами.» p. 270: «что у многих солдат в продолжении сражения, от осьми часов утра и до четырех часов пополудни беспрерывно, патроны все были расстреляны»
- **translation**: "On 20 November 1805 at 7 in the morning, in accordance with the commander-in-chief's disposition, the whole 3rd column set out from the camp by the village of Pratzen and, passing through that village, went [...] not by the road but over ploughland full of ditches, where the road had to be mended three times by pioneers so the artillery could pass." "[...] many soldiers had fired off all their cartridges in the fighting, without a break from eight in the morning until four in the afternoon"
- **grade**: B (written in captivity at Lunéville, July 1806). **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The III Column set out at 7 and was in action from 8 (its commander's report).
- **does not settle**: Where its tail was at 9 (Stutterheim: still on the height).

### C1.28 `kutuzov1951.p267.lieven-two-hours`

- **source**: `kutuzov1951`, no. 254, Kutuzov to Lieven, 15 June 1806 [O.S.], No. 1605, p. 267 (n295); images: https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n295.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): «все Ряжского полка штаб- и обер-офицеры, бывшие в бригаде генерал-майора графа Каменского 1-го, вместе с Фанагорическим гренадерским полком на высоте при деревне Праце сражались через два часа³ со всем мужеством, которого должность от них требовала [...] Я после ретирады 4-й колонны оставался при сих двух полках до конца дела и до наступления ночи.» Editors' note 3: «В течение двух часов.»
- **translation**: "all the officers of the Ryazhsk regiment who were in Count Kamensky I's brigade, together with the Fanagoria grenadiers, fought on the height by Pratzen for two hours with all the courage their duty required [...] After the 4th column's retreat I stayed with these two regiments until the end of the action and nightfall."

- **grade**: B (a letter of 15 June 1806). **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Kamensky's two regiments fought about two hours on the height by Pratzen; Kutuzov with them after the 4th column's retreat.
- **does not settle**: When the two hours began.

### C1.29 `savary1828.p204-207`

- **source**: `savary1828`, t. II, pp. 204 (n215), 207 (n218); images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresduducder04savagoog/page/n215_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoiresduducder04savagoog/page/n218_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 204: «L'empereur me renvoya encore à l'extrême droite pour observer le mouvement des Russes : ils commençaient à déboucher sur le général Legrand, comme j'arrivais près de lui; mais le brouillard empêchait de bien juger le mouvement. Je revins en rendre compte. Il était à peu près sept heures du matin; le brouillard était déjà assez remonté pour que je n'eusse plus besoin de suivre la ligne des troupes pour ne pas m'égarer (on était à deux cents toises des Russes).» p. 207: «Le général Vandamme, en commençant son premier changement de direction à droite, eut un échec. Le 4e régiment de ligne perdit une de ses aigles dans une charge de cavalerie exécutée sur lui par la garde russe; mais les chasseurs de la garde et les grenadiers de service près de l'empereur chargèrent si à propos, que cet accident n'eut pas de suites.»
- **translation**: "The Emperor sent me again to the extreme right to observe the Russians: they were beginning to debouch on General Legrand as I came up; but the fog prevented judging the movement. I came back to report. It was about seven in the morning" "General Vandamme, as he began his first change of direction to the right, suffered a check. The 4th of the line lost one of its eagles in a cavalry charge of the Russian guard; but the chasseurs of the guard and the grenadiers on duty near the emperor charged so opportunely that the accident had no consequences."
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: About 7 the Russians beginning to debouch on Legrand; the eagle lost as Vandamme began his first right wheel (a sequence).
- **does not settle**: The I Column's departure from its camp; a clock for the eagle.

### C1.31 `marbot1891.p260.eagle`

- **source**: `marbot1891`, t. I (27th ed.), p. 260 (n279); p. 261 OCR; images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugn01marbuoft/page/n279.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image, part on the text layer (see the source line)): «Le maréchal Soult enlève non seulement le village de Pratzen, mais encore l'immense plateau de ce nom [...] Là s'engagea, sous les yeux de l'Empereur, un combat des plus vifs, dans lequel les Russes furent battus. Mais un bataillon du 4e de ligne, dont le prince Joseph, frère de Napoléon, était colonel, se laissant emporter trop loin à la poursuite des ennemis, fut chargé et enfoncé par les chevaliers-gardes et les cuirassiers du grand-duc Constantin, frère d'Alexandre, qui lui enlevèrent son aigle!... De nombreuses lignes de cavalerie russe s'avancèrent rapidement pour appuyer le succès momentané des chevaliers-gardes; mais Napoléon ayant lancé contre eux les mameluks, les chasseurs à cheval et les grenadiers à cheval de sa garde, conduits par le maréchal Bessières et par le général Rapp, il y eut une mêlée des plus sanglantes.»
- **translation**: "[...] But a battalion of the 4th of the line [...] carried too far in pursuit, was charged and broken by the chevaliers-gardes and the cuirassiers of Grand Duke Constantine, who took its eagle!... Many lines of Russian cavalry advanced [...] but Napoleon having launched against them the Mamelukes, horse chasseurs and horse grenadiers of his guard, led by Marshal Bessières and General Rapp, there was a most bloody mêlée."
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact; disputed (the eagle's captors).
- **settles**: Marbot's sequence: after Soult took the plateau; no clock. He gives the eagle to the chevaliers-gardes and cuirassiers, against the Horse Guard in Stutterheim, the Materialien's report, M-D, Schönhals and Kutuzov's relation.
- **does not settle**: Any hour; Marbot was not with the Guard.

### C1.33 `davout1885.p202-204.report-26dec`

- **source**: `davout1885`, no. 127, to the Emperor, 'Presbourg, 5 nivôse' [26 December 1805], pp. 202-204 (n267-n269); p. 203's left margin cut in the scan, letters in [ ] supplied; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n267_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n268_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n269_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 202: «J'ai l'honneur de rendre compte à Votre Majesté que le 11, entre cinq heures et demie et six heures du matin, je fis partir» p. 203: «[L]es troupes marchèrent d'abord sur Turas; je leur fis [pren]dre ensuite la direction de Sokolnitz, conformément aux [ordr]es que j'en avais. Pendant cette marche, vers huit heures, [un o]fficier du général Margaron vint me donner connaissance [que] le 3e régiment d'infanterie de ligne de la division Legrand [était] vivement attaqué à Telnitz; [...] Sokolnitz d'ailleurs n'était pas encore attaqué à cette [heu]re.» p. 204: «[à] neuf heures et demie le village était en notre pouvoir»
- **translation**: "On the 11th [frimaire], between half past five and six in the morning, I sent off [...] During this march, about eight o'clock, an officer of General Margaron informed me that the 3rd line regiment of Legrand's division was being vigorously attacked at Telnitz; [...] Sokolnitz besides was not yet attacked at that hour." "[...] at half past nine the village [Telnitz] was in our hands"
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Davout's report: by about 8 Telnitz under vigorous attack and Sokolnitz not yet; Heudelet held Telnitz at half past nine.
- **does not settle**: When the attack on Telnitz began (before c. 8: inference).

### C1.34 `ermolov1865.p33.before-dawn`

- **source**: `ermolov1865`, ч. I, p. 33 (n36); n37 OCR; images: https://archive.org/download/zapiskialeksejapetrovichaermolovach141/page/n36.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image, part on the text layer (see the source line)): «Генералъ-Адъютантъ Уваровъ позванъ былъ въ главную квартиру, откуда возвратился въ скоромъ времени. Немедля за нимъ присланъ офицеръ съ диспозиціею на нѣсколькихъ листахъ [...] одно то ясно было, что на завтра аттакуемъ мы непріятеля. Еще до разсвѣта выступила армія, опасаясь, по видимому, чтобы непріятель не успѣлъ уйти далеко. Войска на маршѣ должны были войти въ мѣста, по диспозиціи для нихъ назначенныя; и по тому начали колонны встрѣчаться между собою и проходить одна сквозь другую, отъ чего произошелъ безпорядокъ, который ночное время болѣе умножало.»
- **translation**: "Even before dawn the army set out, fearing, it seems, that the enemy might get away. The troops on the march had to reach the places the disposition assigned them; and so the columns began to meet and pass through one another, which caused a disorder that the night-time increased still more."
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact; disputed against the 7 o'clock of the order and the reports.
- **settles**: One participant (with Uvarov in Liechtenstein's column) has the army moving before dawn, in the dark, columns crossing: the only reading here that supports an early move.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: (1) "with Uvarov in Liechtenstein's column" is not on this page: it says only that Uvarov was called to headquarters and his division was later taken back toward the right flank; it also has Miloradovich asking for twenty hussars "въ моихъ глазахъ"; Ermolov's place in the march is not settled here. (2) The page goes on "Войска разорвались, смѣшались, и конечно не въ темнотѣ удобно имъ было отыскивать мѣста свои": the dark march may be the night move into the disposition's places rather than the columns' departure from their camps; the passage is ambiguous between the two.
- **does not settle**: Which columns; a clock hour.

### C1.36 `kavalergard1851.p60-61.half-hour`

- **source**: `kavalergard1851`, pp. 60-61 (n70, n71); images: https://archive.org/download/istorijakavalergardovikavalergardskogoee45/page/n70.jpg, https://archive.org/download/istorijakavalergardovikavalergardskogoee45/page/n71.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 60: «Еще до этихъ атакъ, послѣ подвига Конно-гвардейцевъ, Наполеонъ, замѣтивъ колебаніе въ своей пѣхотѣ, послалъ на помощь ей, изъ Гвардейскаго своего резерва, Генерала Раппа, съ Мамелюками, конными егерями и конными гренадерами. Прибывъ на мѣсто боя, въ то самое время, какъ вступали въ дѣло / Кавалергарды, Раппъ ударилъ на Репнина.» p. 61: «За 4 и 5 Кавалергардскими эскадронами послѣдовали два эскадрона Конной Гвардіи, съ Полковникомъ Оленинымъ, но бывъ задержаны тѣснотою мѣста, они не могли поспѣть въ одно съ ними время, и повели атаку, когда 4-й эскадронъ уже понесъ пораженіе. «Въ семъ несчастномъ состояніи» доносилъ Депрерадовичъ Малютину, «не могъ я ничего болѣе предпринять, какъ держаться съ полкомъ, пока всѣ наши ретирующіяся войска переправятся.» Около получаса поддерживали Кавалергарды неравный бой»
- **translation**: (p. 60) "Even before these attacks, after the Horse Guards' feat, Napoleon [...] sent Rapp with the Mamelukes, horse chasseurs and horse grenadiers. - **settles**: The regimental history: Rapp struck Repnin as the Chevalier Guards came in; the fight about half an hour.
- **does not settle**: Any clock hour; its order of the Horse Guard's attacks is not consistent between p. 60 and p. 61.

### C1.37 `segur1873.p466.huit-heures`

- **source**: `segur1877`, Livre XIX ch. VII, p. 466 (n487); images: https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n487_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): «Déjà, sur notre ligne oblique, à notre droite en arrière et refusée, leur attaque avait commencé, de Telnitz à Sokolnitz, vers les bas-fonds des lacs, où tous, à l'envi l'un de l'autre, descendaient et s'accumulaient, nous assaillant du fort au faible. Il n'était pas huit heures; le silence et l'obscurité régnaient encore sur le reste de la ligne, quand soudainement, et d'abord sur les hauteurs, le soleil, dissipant ce brouillard épais, nous montra le plateau de Pratzen, qui se dégarnissait de plus en plus par la marche de flanc des colonnes ennemies. [...] il lui montra Pratzen : « Combien vous faut-il de temps, lui dit-il, pour couronner ce sommet? — Dix minutes, répondit le maréchal. — « Partez donc, reprit l'Empereur; mais vous attendrez encore un quart d'heure, et alors il sera temps! » Le moment venu, les divisions Vandamme et Saint-Hilaire, s'élançant hors du brouillard qui les enveloppait, apparurent soudainement. Il était huit heures.»
- **translation**: "Already [...] their attack had begun, from Telnitz to Sokolnitz [...] It was not yet eight; silence and darkness still reigned over the rest of the line, when suddenly [...] the sun showed us the plateau of Pratzen, emptying more and more by the flank march of the enemy columns. [...] 'How much time do you need to crown that summit?' — 'Ten minutes.' — 'Go then; but you will wait another quarter of an hour' [...] When the moment came, the divisions of Vandamme and Saint-Hilaire [...] appeared suddenly. It was eight o'clock."
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Ségur: the Allied attack Telnitz-Sokolnitz begun before 8; the French divisions out of the fog at 8 ('Dix minutes' against Thiébault's 'au plus vingt minutes', step-2 §4.2).
- **does not settle**: The I Column's departure hour.

### C1.38 `segur1873.p467-469.une-heure`

- **source**: `segur1877`, pp. 467-469 (n488-n490); pp. 470-471 OCR; images: https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n488_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n489_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n490_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image, part on the text layer (see the source line)): p. 467: «Vers onze heures tout avait réussi selon les prévisions de l'Empereur. Le centre russe était percé, ses deux ailes séparées [...] Vers midi j'y retrouvai l'Empereur. Je revenais, par son ordre, d'appeler sa Garde à pied et de conduire sur une éminence, en arrière du ruisseau et de ce plateau, les grenadiers réunis de la réserve. Lui-même, avec la cavalerie de sa Garde, venait de s'y avancer et d'y faire monter à sa gauche Bernadotte.» p. 468: «Dans ce mouvement un retour offensif de l'infanterie alliée, appuyée par la Garde russe, faillit ébranler Vandamme et Saint-Hilaire; ils le repoussèrent à la baïonnette : ce fut le moment critique de la bataille! Il était une heure. Napoléon, au sommet de ce plateau dominateur, voyait devant lui la Garde d'Alexandre s'avançant en masse pour l'en chasser et le lui reprendre.» p. 469: «Il y avait à peine quelques minutes que mon retour avait rassuré l'Empereur sur ses derrières, que, devant lui, l'attaque de la Garde à cheval d'Alexandre commença. Elle fut si impétueuse que les deux bataillons de gauche de Vandamme en furent écrasés! L'un d'eux même, tout sanglant, son aigle et la plupart de ses armes perdues, ne se releva que pour fuir au pas de course. Ce bataillon était du 4me régiment.»
- **translation**: "Towards eleven everything had succeeded [...] Towards noon I found the Emperor there again [...]" "[...] it was the critical moment of the battle! It was one o'clock. Napoleon [...] saw before him Alexander's Guard advancing in mass to drive him off [the plateau]." "Hardly a few minutes later [...] the attack of Alexander's horse guard began. [...] Vandamme's two left battalions were crushed [...] one of them, its eagle [...] lost [...] This battalion was of the 4th regiment."
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: Ségur (at Napoleon's side by his own account): the Russian Guard advancing at one o'clock; its horse guard's attack and the 4th's eagle a few minutes later; Rapp's charge after.
- **does not settle**: The clock's basis.

### C1.c1 `kutuzov1951.p265.private-relation`

- **source**: `kutuzov1951`, no. 252, Kutuzov to Alexander I, "1806 г. марта 1", Дубно, pp. 265-266 (n293, n294); archive line p. 266 "ЦГВИА, ф. 26, оп. 152, д. 309, лл. 765—768. Подлинник."; editors' note 1 (p. 266); images: https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n293.jpg, https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n294.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 265: «Пробившись сквозь центр, не трудно было уже француз- / ским колоннам обойтить и 2-ю нашу, которая хотя и была рас- / строена, не менее того знатная часть людей спасена, а Фанаго- / рийской и Ряжской полк, из сей колонны обратившись в по- / рядке на неприятеля, заняли высоту, бывшую на левом фланге / 4-й колонны, держали оную очень долго и, наконец, уступая / уже превосходному числу, спустились к подошве ее, где стояли, / доколе 1-я колонна и самая гвардия отретировались, в чем не- / приятель не смел делать помешательство, видя твердость / и устройство тех двух полков.» p. 266, note 1: «Настоящая реляция является «неофициальной», предназначавшейся исключительно для сведения Александра I (см. также документ № 242).»
- **translation**: "Having broken through the centre, it was no longer hard for the French columns to get round our 2nd [column] too, which, though thrown into disorder, nonetheless saved a good part of its men; and the Fanagoria and Ryazhsk regiments, turning from this column in good order against the enemy, occupied the height that lay on the left flank of the 4th column, held it very long and at last, yielding to superior numbers, came down to its foot, where they stood until the 1st column and the Guard itself had withdrawn, the enemy not daring to interfere, seeing the firmness and order of those two regiments." Note 1: "This relation is 'unofficial', meant solely for Alexander I's information (see also document no. 242)."
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact; disputed (who turned the brigade).
- **settles**: Kutuzov's private relation (March 1806): the two regiments turned from the 2nd Column of themselves, after the French had broken through the centre, and took the height on the 4th Column's left; no order of his is mentioned. Against his public relation of 14 January 1806 (no. 247, the one Alexander asked "для публикования", no. 242), in which he found them cut off, formed them and ordered Kamensky onto the ridge. The two regiments stood at the foot until the I Column and the Guard had withdrawn.
- **does not settle**: A clock hour; who gave the order (no agent named). Rescript no. 242 (22 Dec. 1805) read in the OCR only.
- **bears on**: item 2: kamensky@3; kamensky@4; EVENTS[#kamensky].why; EVENTS[#kamensky].dispute; COMMAND.al[4][0]; FORMATIONS.kamensky.role; item 3 (the Guard's withdrawal before the two regiments' at the foot).
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

### C1.c2 `kutuzov1951.p266-267.ryazhsk-from-start`

- **source**: `kutuzov1951`, no. 253, Kutuzov to Alexander I, "1806 г. июня 1", pp. 266-267 (n294, n295); images: https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n294.jpg, https://archive.org/download/20250102_20250102_1833/page/n295.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 266: «А как сей полк, состоявши в тот день / [p. 267] в бригаде генерал-майора графа Каменского 1-го, действовал с особенным мужеством, ибо с начала на Прац[енс]ких горах вместе с Фанагорическим гренадерским полком поражал неприятеля долгое время, наконец, принужденным найдясь сойтить с оных, держал позицию свою у подошвы гор до самого вечера и подал средство протчим, в той стороне бывшим войскам, ретироваться» Note 2 (p. 266): «Упомянутые рапорты С. М. Каменского 1-го хранятся в ЦГВИА, ф. 26, оп. 152, св. 563, д. 1, ч. 4, лл. 12—14. В сборнике не публикуются.»
- **translation**: "And as this regiment, being that day in Major-General Count Kamensky I's brigade, acted with particular courage, for from the start on the Pratzen heights, together with the Fanagoria grenadier regiment, it struck the enemy a long time and at last, forced to come down from them, held its position at the foot of the hills until evening and gave the other troops on that side the means to withdraw" Note 2: "Kamensky I's reports mentioned are held in the TsGVIA [...]. They are not published in this collection."
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Kutuzov (June 1806): the Ryazhsk regiment fought "from the start" on the Pratzen heights beside the Fanagoria, then held at the foot of the hills until evening. No descent and turn is described.
- **does not settle**: A clock hour; whether "с начала" means from the start of the day or of its fight. Kamensky's own reports (archive, unpublished there) are a lead, unread. "до самого вечера" against the public relation's "до половины 4-го часа".

- **quote** (read on: page image): «Графъ Ланжеронъ выступилъ съ ночлега въ одно время съ Дохтуровымъ, лѣвымъ флангомъ. Впереди былъ 8-й егерскій полкъ; за нимъ шли полки Выборгскій, Пермскій и Курскій; въ замкѣ слѣдовалъ Графъ Каменскій 1-й, съ полками Ряжскимъ мушкетерскимъ и Фанагорійскимъ гренадерскимъ. Въ половинѣ 9-го, когда Дохтуровъ началъ атаку Тельница, Графъ Ланжеронъ подошелъ къ Гольдбаху, былъ встрѣченъ поставленными на другомъ берегу ручья стрѣлками и батареями, и завязалъ дѣло съ непріятелемъ.»
- **translation**: "Count Langeron left his night camp at the same time as Dokhturov, by the left flank. In front was the 8th Jäger regiment; [...] at the rear followed Count Kamensky I with the Ryazhsk musketeer and Fanagoria grenadier regiments. At half past eight, when Dokhturov began the attack on Telnitz, Count Langeron reached the Goldbach, was met by skirmishers and batteries posted on the far bank of the stream, and engaged the enemy."
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact; disputed.
- **settles**: M-D: Dokhturov began his attack on Telnitz at 08:30, and Langeron, who left camp with him, reached the Goldbach then. Against Kutuzov's public relation (the I Column forced the enemy back to Telnitz "около 8 часов утра") and Buxhowden's II Column "спустилась с горы в 8 часов".
- **does not settle**: M-D's source for 08:30 (the footnote on this page cites Buxhowden's report for the retaking of Telnitz, not for the hour).
- **bears on**: item 1: dok@1 (the proposed at:480); lang@1; EVENTS[#telnitz]; EVENTS[#sokolnitz]; FORMATIONS.kamensky.track[2].
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

### C1.c5 `thiebault3_1894.p456-457.night-noise`

- **source**: `thiebault3_1894`, pp. 456-457 (n467, n468); second reading of step-2 §4.2; images: https://archive.org/download/mmoires03thieuoft/page/n467.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoires03thieuoft/page/n468.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): p. 456: «Cet ordre était ridicule, car le jour ne venait qu'à huit heures [...] Cependant, et d'heure en heure, l'Empereur recevait des rapports sur la continuation du mouvement de toute la gauche de l'armée alliée, ainsi qu'on en jugea par le bruit que faisaient les voitures d'artillerie, et chacun de ces rapports était pour lui le sujet d'une joie nouvelle. A trois heures, on n'entendait plus rien. [...] Bien avant le jour, l'Empereur était à cheval; avant huit heures, il avait réuni autour de lui le prince Murat, les maréchaux [...] Au jour naissant, on vint rendre compte à l'Empereur / [p. 457] que les dernières troupes russes qui avaient encore passé la nuit sur le plateau de Pratzen, le quittaient, se dirigeant vers Telnitz»
- **translation**: "This order was absurd, for day came only at eight [...] Meanwhile, hour by hour, the Emperor received reports on the continuing movement of the whole left of the Allied army, as was judged from the noise of the artillery vehicles [...] At three o'clock nothing more was heard. [...] Long before day the Emperor was on horseback; before eight he had gathered round him [...] At daybreak it was reported to the Emperor that the last Russian troops who had spent the night on the Pratzen plateau were leaving it toward Telnitz"
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact; disputed.
- **settles**: Thiébault (memoir, 1894): the Allied left was heard moving through the night until three; daybreak (by his account at eight) saw the last Russians leave the plateau toward Telnitz. The one reading here of an Allied movement in the dark before 04:00; it is a night movement into position, by sound, not a dated descent.
- **does not settle**: Which columns moved in the night; a clock for the morning departure ("au jour naissant" is not a clock hour; his "le jour ne venait qu'à huit heures" is his own figure for daybreak).
- **bears on**: item 1; PHASES[0].events[1]; EVENTS[#columns-move]; ermolov1865.p33.before-dawn.
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

- **source**: `thiebault3_1894`, p. 504 (n517); second reading of step-2 §4.10; images: https://archive.org/download/mmoires03thieuoft/page/n517.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): «Sans doute, il avait été trompé par l'aide de camp qui, à huit heures et demie du matin, vint lui dire que les derniers corps de l'ennemi avaient quitté les hauteurs de Pratzen, alors que la brigade Kamenski, les régiments Fanagorski et Riajski et la quatrième colonne qui devait suivre le mouvement des trois premières s'y trouvaient encore en entier»
- **translation**: "No doubt he had been misled by the aide-de-camp who at half past eight in the morning came to tell him that the enemy's last corps had left the Pratzen heights, whereas Kamensky's brigade, the Fanagoria and Ryazhsk regiments and the fourth column [...] were still there in full"
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Thiébault: at 08:30 Kamensky's brigade was still entire on the Pratzen heights, with the 4th Column.
- **does not settle**: When the brigade turned; whether it had begun to descend. Thiébault lists the brigade and its two regiments as if separate.
