**The source skeptic's summary** (C1; its counts and "CRn" numbers are its working list's: the verdicts above are the record): Every one of the 38 readings was checked on page images downloaded afresh into work/C1-skeptic/img (the researcher's files were not opened). No quotation was missing from its page: 35 exact, 3 with minor typographic differences (repeated guillemets, letters supplied in a cut margin). No reading is rejected. 10 are accepted with corrections: locators, grades or a 'settles' that says more than the words. The heaviest corrections are four. (1) M-D's and the Bulletin's 'before one' for the Guard is an inference from the order of the narrative, not their words. (2) Kutuzov's public relation puts the Guard after the II-IV Columns' retreat, so it cannot be counted on the 'before one' side. (3) Buxhowden's report is undated; its grade A rests on the editors' dating. (4) Ermolov's dark march may be the night move into position. Seven counter-readings, read on their images. The most important is Kutuzov's private relation of 1 March 1806 (1951 ed. no. 252, p. 265), which the researcher missed. It was written 'единственно для сведения' of the Tsar, against the public one written 'для публикования' (rescript no. 242, OCR only). In it the Fanagoria and Ryazhsk turn from the 2nd Column with no order of Kutuzov's, so his own private account undercuts the public version the map cites as his. M-D 1844 p. 179 dates Dokhturov's attack on Telnitz to 08:30, against the proposal's A-graded 08:00 arrival. Thiébault pp. 456-457 has the Allied left heard moving in the night until three, the one reading of a pre-dawn movement besides Ermolov. Leads not followed: Kamensky's own reports (ЦГВИА ф. 26, оп. 152, св. 563, д. 1, ч. 4, лл. 12—14; unpublished in the 1951 edition); Langeron's relation (Voennyi sbornik 1900 no. 11). Ségur pp. 470-471 and Rapp p. 61 were left OCR-only, as the researcher had them. The chronology effects (REVIEW rows, CONFLICTS, ALLOW_TIMED names) were matched against tools/stage2/chronology.js by name only; the suites were not run (read-only task). The partial log is results/C1-challenge.partial.md.

**The researcher's notes** (C1): Resumed from attempt 1 (no notes left; its downloads reused; every quotation here re-read on the image by this attempt). The strongest new evidence is the 1951 Soviet documentary edition (archive.org 20250102_20250102_1833): Buxhowden's own report (A) and Miloradovich's (A), Kutuzov's relation of 14 January 1806 (the Russian text of the report the Materialien translate, step-2 §3.4), his letter to Lieven of June 1806 and Przybyszewski's report; its title leaves should be viewed by the skeptic (attempt 1 viewed n2-n7). The 1951 editors' note to the regiment's name in Kutuzov's relation reads 'В опубликованном тексте ошибочно указано наименование полка — «Рижский»' (p. 257): the original says Ряжский; this bears on the Ryazhsk/Ryazan question (C9, question 153), outside this cluster. Contradictions inside the map noticed: (1) PHASES[0].events[1] 'c. 04:00 Allied columns begin to move off the plateau' has no source among those read; (2) lang@2/prz@2 and EVENTS[#sokolnitz] at 08:00 sit early against M-D 1844 p. 179 (Langeron at the Goldbach at 08:30), Davout's report (Sokolnitz not yet attacked about 08:00) and Schönhals p. 160 (the III Column's head at Sokolnitz after the Pratzen attack began); (3) the M-D 1846 translation differs from the 1844 original on the Chevalier Guard's losses (16 against 15 officers) and the squadrons (3rd and 4th against 4th and 5th), and renders the Russian hour idiom inconsistently ('à cinq heures' for 'въ шестомъ часу', 'à huit heures' for 'въ 8-мъ часу'); (4) Ségur has Soult answer 'Dix minutes' (p. 466) against Thiébault's 'au plus vingt minutes' (EVENTS[#decision].why); Ségur also puts the French divisions out of the fog at 8, earlier than the map's 08:45 (outside this cluster). (5) Stutterheim numbers Przybyszewski's column the 3rd and calls Kamensky's brigade separated 'von dieser Colonne' (the 3rd), not Langeron's 2nd. Schönhals 1873's pages carry the signature 'Österr. militär. Zeitschrift. 1874. (Feldzug 1805.)': the book is set from the journal's sheets; the date discrepancy (1873 title, 1874 signature) is for the register. Leads not followed: Langeron's relation (Voennyi sbornik 1900 no. 11), Sokolov 2006, Lejeune, Drouet d'Erlon, Friant 1857, Czartoryski, Thiers. Grading question for the synthesizer: Kutuzov's relation (14 Jan 1806) and Przybyszewski's report (July 1806) are contemporary reports not dated 1805; graded B here by the brief's letter.

## C2: the other hours, the ceiling-derived climbs and the forced dated legs

### C2.1 `segur1873.p466.dix-minutes`

- **source**: `segur1877`, t. II, p. 466 (leaf n487; title leaf n10 'TOME DEUXIÈME', 'Deuxième édition', text layer); images: https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n487.jpg.
- **locator, as the source skeptic gives it**: Ségur, Histoire et mémoires, t. II, 2e éd., Paris, Librairie de Firmin-Didot et Cie, 1877, p. 466 (leaf n487; title leaf n10)
- **quote** (read on: page image, part on the text layer (see the source line)): Il n'était pas huit heures ; le silence et l'obscurité régnaient encore sur le reste de la ligne, quand soudainement, et d'abord sur les hauteurs, le soleil, dissipant ce brouillard épais, nous montra le plateau de Pratzen, qui se dégarnissait de plus en plus par la marche de flanc des colonnes ennemies. [...] A cet aspect, le maréchal Soult, que l'Empereur avait gardé le dernier près de lui, voulut courir à ses divisions et leur donner le signal ; mais Napoléon plus calme, laissant l'ennemi achever sa faute, le retint encore ; il lui montra Pratzen : « Combien vous faut-« il de temps, lui dit-il, pour couronner ce som-« met? — Dix minutes, répondit le maréchal. — « Partez donc, reprit l'Empereur ; mais vous atten-« drez encore un quart d'heure, et alors il sera « temps! » / Le moment venu, les divisions Vandamme et Saint-Hilaire, s'élançant hors du brouillard qui les enveloppait, apparurent soudainement. Il était huit heures.
- **translation**: It was not yet eight; silence and darkness still reigned over the rest of the line when suddenly, and first on the heights, the sun, scattering this thick fog, showed us the Pratzen plateau, emptying more and more by the flank march of the enemy columns. [...] At this sight Marshal Soult, whom the Emperor had kept last beside him, wanted to rush to his divisions and give them the signal; but Napoleon, calmer, letting the enemy complete his mistake, held him back again; he pointed to Pratzen: 'How much time do you need to crown that summit?' 'Ten minutes,' answered the marshal. 'Go then,' the Emperor went on, 'but you will wait another quarter of an hour, and then it will be time!' When the moment came, the divisions of Vandamme and Saint-Hilaire, springing out of the fog that enveloped them, suddenly appeared. It was eight o'clock.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: Ségur's version of the exchange: 'Dix minutes' (not twenty) and a quarter of an hour's wait; the sun on the heights before eight; the two divisions appearing at eight.
- **does not settle**: Whether Ségur heard the exchange; the clock's basis; the end of the climb.
- **bears on**: decision; EVENTS[#decision]; PHASES[2].events[2]; SOURCE_NOTE.refs[3]; sthilaire@3; vandamme@3; sunrise.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 2 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept with corrections. Quote exact on p. 466 (cut drops the sentence on Soult's troops in the ravine). The title leaf n10 reads 'TOME DEUXIÈME / Deuxième édition / PARIS / LIBRAIRIE DE FIRMIN-DIDOT ET Cie / IMPRIMEURS DE L'INSTITUT, RUE JACOB, 56 / 1877': the copy read is the 2nd edition of 1877, not 1873, and not 'Firmin Didot frères, fils et Cie' (that is archive.org's metadata, which the title page contradicts). The key should be segur1877 (or src_new corrected). Grade B and label disputed are right. Not read by a person.

### C2.2 `segur1873.p467-469.noon-one`

- **source**: `segur1877`, t. II, pp. 467-469 (leaves n488-n490; read at reduced width, agreeing with the text layer); images: https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n488.jpg, https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n489.jpg, https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n490.jpg.
- **locator, as the source skeptic gives it**: t. II (2e éd., 1877), pp. 467-469 (leaves n488-n490)
- **quote** (read on: page image, part on the text layer (see the source line)): A neuf heures, et de notre côté, la bataille, péniblement sur la défensive en arrière à droite, déjà victorieuse en avant au centre, et menaçante à notre gauche, était engagée sur tout notre front. / Vers onze heures tout avait réussi selon les prévisions de l'Empereur. Le centre russe était percé, ses deux ailes séparées [...] Vers midi j'y retrouvai l'Empereur. Je revenais, par son ordre, d'appeler sa Garde à pied et de conduire sur une éminence, en arrière du ruisseau et de ce plateau, les grenadiers réunis de la réserve. [...] (p. 468) Il était une heure. Napoléon, au sommet de ce plateau dominateur, voyait devant lui la Garde d'Alexandre s'avançant en masse [...] (p. 469) C'était en effet la division des grenadiers réunis de notre réserve. Duroc, apercevant les progrès de la gauche russe contre notre droite, marchait du côté des lacs, au secours de Soult et de Davout.
- **translation**: At nine, on our side, the battle, laboriously on the defensive back on the right, already victorious forward in the centre and threatening on our left, was engaged along our whole front. Toward eleven everything had succeeded as the Emperor foresaw. The Russian centre was pierced, its two wings separated [...] Toward noon I found the Emperor there again. I was returning, by his order, from calling up his Foot Guard and leading the united grenadiers of the reserve onto an eminence behind the brook and this plateau. [...] It was one o'clock. Napoleon, on top of this commanding plateau, saw before him Alexander's Guard advancing in mass [...] It was in fact the division of united grenadiers of our reserve. Duroc, seeing the Russian left's progress against our right, was marching toward the lakes to help Soult and Davout.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: In Ségur, the grenadiers brought up about noon and marching toward the lakes at about one; the centre pierced toward eleven.
- **does not settle**: Where the grenadiers stood at 14:30; whether they fought.
- **bears on**: c_gren@7; c_gren@8; blasowitz (sequence); EVENTS[#wheel].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 3 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept with corrections. Quotes exact. Edition is 1877 (see above). At 'Vers midi' Ségur places the grenadiers on an eminence behind the brook and the plateau; their march 'du côté des lacs' is seen after 'Il était une heure' and the Russian Guard's advance, i.e. at or just after one in his sequence. p. 468 also dates the Russian Guard's advance at one (bears on C3). Not read by a person.

### C2.4 `savary1828.p204-205.seven-davout`

- **source**: `savary1828`, t. II, pp. 204-205 (leaves n215-n216); images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresduducder04savagoog/page/n215.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoiresduducder04savagoog/page/n216.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Il était à peu près sept heures du matin; le brouillard était déjà assez remonté pour que je n'eusse plus besoin de suivre la ligne des troupes pour ne pas m'égarer [...] Tous les maréchaux étaient près de lui et le tourmentaient pour commencer : il résista à leurs instances jusqu'à ce que l'attaque des / Russes se fût plus prononcée à sa droite; il avait fait dire au maréchal Davout d'appuyer le général Legrand, qui bientôt après fut attaqué et eut toute sa division engagée. Lorsque l'empereur jugea à la vivacité du feu que l'attaque était sérieuse, il fit partir tous les maréchaux et leur ordonna de commencer. [...] Le général Saint-Hilaire attaqua de front la position russe qu'on appelle dans le pays montagne du Pratzer. [...] Ce feu dura deux heures [...] L'empereur fit de suite marcher une des divisions du maréchal Bernadotte derrière la division Vandamme, et une portion des grenadiers réunis derrière celle de Saint-Hilaire.
- **translation**: It was about seven in the morning; the fog had already lifted enough that I no longer needed to follow the line of troops not to lose my way [...] All the marshals were near him, pressing him to begin: he resisted until the Russian attack on his right had become more pronounced; he had had Marshal Davout told to support General Legrand, who was soon afterwards attacked and had his whole division engaged. When the Emperor judged from the liveliness of the fire that the attack was serious, he sent off all the marshals and ordered them to begin. [...] General Saint-Hilaire attacked the Russian position called locally the Pratzer hill frontally. [...] This fire lasted two hours [...] The Emperor at once had one of Marshal Bernadotte's divisions march behind Vandamme's division, and a portion of the united grenadiers behind Saint-Hilaire's.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: In Savary the release follows the Allied attack on the right becoming serious; no clock hour for it; a portion of the grenadiers behind Saint-Hilaire in the morning.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it** (from its note): Quote exact. Settles understates: Savary's sequence puts the release after 'à peu près sept heures' (his return from the right), after Legrand 'bientôt après fut attaqué' and the fire was judged serious: an unstated interval after about seven, earlier than Thiébault's half past eight. It is a side of the release dispute, not merely 'no clock hour'.
- **does not settle**: The hour of the release; the twenty-minutes exchange (Savary does not tell it).
- **bears on**: EVENTS[#decision]; c_gren.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 2 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept with corrections. ### C2.6 `corr11.9541.p451.instant`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9541 (30th Bulletin), p. 451 (leaf n460) [extends step-2 register §1.5]; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n460.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Un instant après, la canonnade se fit entendre à l'extrémité de la droite, que l'avant-garde ennemie avait déjà débordée. Mais la rencontre imprévue du maréchal Davout arrêta l'ennemi tout court, et le combat s'engagea. / Le maréchal Soult s'ébranle au même instant, se dirige sur les hauteurs du village de Pratzen avec les divisions des généraux Vandamme et Saint-Hilaire [...] Il n'y avait pas une heure que l'on se battait, et toute la gauche de l'ennemi était coupée. [...] A une heure après midi la victoire était décidée. Elle n'avait pas été un moment douteuse. Pas un homme de la réserve n'avait été nécessaire et n'avait donné nulle part.
- **translation**: A moment later the cannonade was heard at the far right, which the enemy advance guard had already outflanked. But Marshal Davout's unforeseen encounter stopped the enemy short, and the fight began. Marshal Soult moves off at the same instant toward the heights of the village of Pratzen with the divisions of Generals Vandamme and Saint-Hilaire [...] We had not been fighting an hour, and the enemy's whole left was cut off. [...] At one in the afternoon the victory was decided. It had not been doubtful for a moment. Not one man of the reserve had been needed or had engaged anywhere.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The Bulletin's sequence: orders at the first rays (p. 450, §1.4), then Davout's encounter on the right and Soult's move 'au même instant'; the victory decided at one; the reserve not engaged.
- **does not settle**: Any clock hour for the release or the climb.

### C2.7 `md1844.p177.half-hour`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, p. 177 (IIIF k188) [the step-2 register §6.3 quoted its first lines; this is the order]; images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$188/full/1100,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Когда солнце озарило вершины горъ, Наполеонъ увидѣлъ, что занимаемыя нами высоты обнажались отъ войскъ [...] отдалъ онъ маршаламъ окончательныя повелѣнія: 1), Бернадоту и Сульту, овладѣвъ Праценскими высотами, разрѣзать нашу армію на двѣ части, и начать атаку черезъ полчаса, разсчитывая, что къ тому времени союзныя войска еще болѣе разобщатся; 2), резервной конницѣ Мюрата, гренадерамъ Удино и гвардіи Бессіера слѣдовать за Сультомъ и Бернадотомъ
- **translation**: When the sun lit the tops of the hills, Napoleon saw that the heights we held were emptying of troops [...] he gave the marshals his final orders: 1) Bernadotte and Soult, having seized the Pratzen heights, to cut our army in two, and to begin the attack in half an hour, reckoning that by then the Allied troops would be further separated; 2) Murat's reserve cavalry, Oudinot's grenadiers and Bessières's guard to follow Soult and Bernadotte
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: M-D: the order given when the sun lit the summits, the attack to begin half an hour later.
- **does not settle**: A clock hour for the sun on the summits.

### C2.8 `md1844.p178-179.davout-telnitz`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, pp. 178-179 (IIIF k189-k190); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$189/full/1100,/0/default.jpg, https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$190/full/1100,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Черезъ часъ появился Графъ Буксгевденъ съ колонною Дохтурова [...] Кинмейеръ и Левизъ атаковали и взяли Тельницъ. [...] Едва утихъ огонь, пришелъ отъ Райгерна Даву, съ пѣхотною дивизіею Фріана и конною Бурсье, и принялъ начальство надъ дивизіями Леграна и Маргарона. Онъ двинулся въ Тельницъ среди внезапно павшаго тумана и ударилъ въ штыки на батальонъ 7-го егерскаго полка и Австрійскую пѣхоту, / занимавшіе селеніе. [...] послѣ чего Графъ Буксгевденъ вновь овладѣлъ Тельницомъ и утвердился въ немъ. (*) [footnote] (*) Донесеніе Графа Буксгевдена Кутузову.
- **translation**: An hour later Count Buxhowden appeared with Dokhturov's column [...] Kienmayer and Levis attacked and took Telnitz. He moved on Telnitz amid a fog that had suddenly fallen and charged with the bayonet the battalion of the 7th jäger regiment and the Austrian infantry holding the village. [...] after which Count Buxhowden took Telnitz again and established himself in it. (*) Count Buxhowden's report to Kutuzov.

### C2.9 `md1844.p181.ninth-hour`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, p. 181 (IIIF k192); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$192/full/1100,/0/default.jpg.

Сначала выступленію его въ опредѣленный часъ мѣшала проходившая передъ его колонною кавалерія Князя Лихтенштейна. Вскорѣ конница очистила ему путь, но Кутузовъ, хотя и долженъ былъ по диспозиціи идти впередъ, стоялъ неподвижно [...] Въ 9-мъ часу прибыли на поле сраженія Императоры Александръ и Францъ.
- **translation**: From first light Kutuzov was with the 4th column, appointed to go toward Kobelnitz. ### C2.14 `davout1885.no123.p192-193`

- **source**: `davout1885`, t. I, no. 123, pp. 192-193 (leaves n257-n258); title leaf n12; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n257.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n258.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): 123. — AU MINISTRE DE LA GUERRE, major général. / A Menitz, 11 frimaire an XIV (2 décembre 1805). / Monsieur le Maréchal, j'ai l'honneur de rendre compte à Votre Excellence que ce matin, à cinq heures, la division / Friant s'est mise en marche de ses positions de Raygern [...] ayant eu connaissance pendant le mouvement que le 3e régiment de ligne était attaqué à Telnitz, j'y ai dirigé cette division. Le village, que le 3e régiment avait été obligé de quitter après la plus belle résistance, a été attaqué et emporté par le général Heudelet, à la tête du 108e régiment et de quelques compagnies du 15e régiment d'infanterie légère; mais des forces supérieures de l'ennemi l'ont repris de nouveau.
- **translation**: To the Minister of War, major-general. Menitz, 11 frimaire an XIV (2 December 1805). Monsieur le Maréchal, I have the honour to report to Your Excellency that this morning at five Friant's division set off from its positions at Raygern [...] learning during the movement that the 3rd of the line was attacked at Telnitz, I directed this division there. The village, which the 3rd had been forced to leave after the finest resistance, was attacked and carried by General Heudelet at the head of the 108th and some companies of the 15th light; but superior enemy forces retook it.
- **grade**: A. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: Davout's report of the day: Friant off from Raigern at five; Heudelet retakes Telnitz, which is lost again.
- **does not settle**: The hour of the retaking (see no. 127); the departure hour is disputed within Davout (five here; half past five to six in no. 127).
- **bears on**: EVENTS[#raigern]; EVENTS[#davout]; EVENTS[#telnitz-retaken]; friant@2.

### C2.15 `davout1885.no127.p202-204`

- **source**: `davout1885`, t. I, no. 127 (Presbourg, 5 nivôse [26 December 1805]), pp. 202-204 (leaves n267-n269); p. 203's left margin is cut in the scan: letters in [ ] restored from sense and the text layer; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n267.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n268.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n269.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image, part on the text layer (see the source line)): J'ai l'honneur de rendre compte à Votre Majesté que le 11, entre cinq heures et demie et six heures du matin, je fis partir / [la di]vision Friant de l'abbaye de Raygern [...] [L]es troupes marchèrent d'abord sur Turas; je leur fis [pren]dre ensuite la direction de Sokolnitz, conformément aux [ordr]es que j'en avais. Pendant cette marche, vers huit heures, [un o]fficier du général Margaron vint me donner connaissance [que] le 3e régiment d'infanterie de ligne de la division Legrand [était] vivement attaqué à Telnitz [...] [Su]r ces entrefaites, le général Heudelet, arrivé avec sa bri[gade] à hauteur de Telnitz, s'y précipita à la tête de ses troupes. [...] à / neuf heures et demie le village était en notre pouvoir, le champ de bataille, les rues et les maisons étaient jonchés de morts [...] le brouillard ne lui permettant pas de reconnaître nos troupes, ce régiment engagea un feu très-vif qui fit beaucoup souffrir la brigade du général Heudelet; les Russes reprirent les armes, et à l'aide de nouvelles troupes ils se rendirent de nouveau maîtres du village.
- **translation**: I have the honour to report to Your Majesty that on the 11th, between half past five and six in the morning, I sent Friant's division off from the abbey of Raygern [...] The troops marched first on Turas; I then had them take the direction of Sokolnitz, as ordered. During this march, toward eight, an officer of General Margaron came to tell me that the 3rd of the line of Legrand's division was hard pressed at Telnitz [...] Meanwhile General Heudelet, arriving with his brigade level with Telnitz, threw himself into it at the head of his troops. [...] by half past nine the village was in our hands, the field, streets and houses strewn with dead [...] the fog not letting it recognise our troops, this regiment [the 26th light] opened a very lively fire that made General Heudelet's brigade suffer greatly; the Russians took up their arms again and with fresh troops became masters of the village once more.
- **translation, the source skeptic's correction**: ... at half past nine the village was in our hands ...
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Davout's report: news of the attack on Telnitz reached him on the march about eight; Heudelet's brigade then reached Telnitz; the village in French hands by half past nine, then lost again in the fog.
- **does not settle**: The minute Heudelet's attack began.
- **bears on**: EVENTS[#davout]; EVENTS[#telnitz-retaken]; PHASES[1].events[2]; PHASES[2].events[1]; friant@2.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 3 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept with corrections. Quote exact; p. 203's cut margin is as stated and the restorations are sound. 'à neuf heures et demie le village était en notre pouvoir' is AT half past nine, not 'by'. Grade A holds by the brief's rule, but the report is retrospective (Presbourg, 26 December, written on Napoleon's request for 'un détail plus circonstancié', footnote p. 202): weaker on minutes than no. 123. It gives two hours only (news 'vers huit heures'; the village at half past nine); Heudelet's arrival is untimed. Not read by a person.

### C2.28 `mat.p114-116.report-blasowitz`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, Zusatz 13 (an 'officieller, ungedruckter Bericht'), pp. 114-116 (leaves n123-n125); images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n123.jpg, https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n124.jpg, https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n125.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Ein officieller, ungedruckter Bericht verdient hier mit der nun folgenden Erzählung des Vfs. verglichen zu werden [...] „Nach der Disposition formirte sich am 2ten December früh 7 Uhr die Armee in 4 Colonnen [...] Zu eben dieser Zeit war die 3te und 4te Colonne gegen Sokolnitz und Kobelnitz über angekommen, der Feind marschirte ihnen mit Uebermacht entgegen, griff beide an und warf sie ebenfalls [...] gegen Aujest und Klein-Hostjeradek zurück. — Als der Feind mit seinem rechten Flügel obige Vortheile errungen hatte, brach er sein Centrum und linken Flügel, bildete mehrere Colonnen, und / rückte mit selben gegen unsern rechten Flügel und Centrum vor. — Die erste dieser aus dem Centro vorbrechenden Colonnen, ohngefähr 4000 Mann Infanterie stark, zog sich gegen das Dorf Blasowitz und warf aus selbem ein in Eile hineingeworfenes Bataillon Garde-Jäger
- **translation**: An official, unprinted report deserves comparison here with the author's narrative that follows [...] 'By the disposition the army formed in 4 columns at 7 in the morning on 2 December [...] At this same time the 3rd and 4th columns had arrived opposite Sokolnitz and Kobelnitz; the enemy marched against them in superior force, attacked both and threw them back likewise toward Aujest and Klein-Hostjeradek. When the enemy had won these advantages with his right wing, he broke forward his centre and left wing, formed several columns and advanced with them against our right wing and centre. The first of these columns breaking from the centre, about 4,000 infantry, moved on the village of Blasowitz and threw out of it a battalion of Guard jägers thrown in in haste'
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The report: Blasowitz taken after the French right had thrown back the 3rd and 4th columns; no hour.
- **does not settle**: The report's author and date (unstated); an hour.
- **bears on**: EVENTS[#blasowitz]; FEATURES[#blasowitz].facts.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 3 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept with corrections. Quote exact. The report opens with the Russian Guards and is told from their side: a Guard report, author unstated. Its order differs from Schönhals: Blasowitz falls before Liechtenstein sends the Uhlans (p. 116), whereas Schönhals tells the Uhlans' charge first (p. 163). It also compresses the French centre's advance to after the 1st-2nd and 3rd-4th columns were thrown back. Its sequence cannot date the fall; settles should say 'in this report's order'. Not read by a person.

### C2.29 `mat.p118-119.report-evening`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, Zusatz 13, pp. 118-119 (leaves n127-n128); images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n127.jpg, https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n128.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Kaum waren die Garden auf der anbefohlnen Anhöhe von Krzenowitz aufmarschirt, als der Feind mit dreifacher Uebermacht gegen die Fronte und beide Flanken anrückte [...] war man bemüssigt der Uebermacht zu weichen und sich auf die Anhöhen hinter Krzenowitz und Birnbaum zurückzuziehen, wodurch der Feind den dominirenden Terrain gegen über Krzenowitz gewann, und die Schlacht entschieden war." [...] (p. 119) die sich Zurückziehenden [...] nahmen eine Stellung zwischen Krzenowitz und Austerlitz, an welcher sich links die kais. östreichische Cavallerie und rechts Fürst Bagration anschloß. — Der Feind marschirte auf den gegen über liegenden Anhöhen auf und formirte eine Linie von dem Rausnitzer Posthause bis Klein-Hostjeradek. — Von Nachmittag 3 Uhr bis zur einbrechenden Nacht blieben beide Armeen ruhig (bis auf einzelne Kanonenschüsse und kleine unbedeutende Plänkeleien) auf einen halben Kanonenschuß von einander entfernt durch ein schmales Thal getrennt. — Nach eingebrochener Nacht trat die kaiserl. östreichische und kais. russische Armee den Rückzug gegen Göding an
- **translation**: Hardly had the Guards deployed on the height of Krzenowitz as ordered when the enemy advanced with triple superiority against the front and both flanks [...] one was forced to yield and withdraw onto the heights behind Krzenowitz and Birnbaum, whereby the enemy won the commanding ground opposite Krzenowitz and the battle was decided. [...] those retreating took a position between Krzenowitz and Austerlitz, joined on the left by the Austrian cavalry and on the right by Prince Bagration. The enemy deployed on the heights opposite and formed a line from the Rausnitz post house to Klein-Hostjeradek. From 3 in the afternoon until nightfall both armies stayed quiet (but for single cannon shots and small skirmishes) half a cannon shot apart, separated by a narrow valley. After nightfall the Austrian and Russian army began the retreat toward Göding
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: The report: from three in the afternoon to nightfall the northern front quiet, Bagration on the right of a Krzenowitz-Austerlitz position, the French line from the Rausnitz post house to Klein-Hostjeradek.
- **does not settle**: Its author and date; Bagration's own hour.

### C2.30 `thieb.p467.pointe-du-jour`

- **source**: `thiebault3_1894`, p. 467 (leaf n478); images: https://archive.org/download/mmoires03thieuoft/page/n478.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Au moment où, à la pointe du jour, le maréchal Soult (je dirai plus loin comment nous ne le vîmes plus de la journée) nous mit en mouvement, Morand reçut du général Saint-Hilaire l'ordre de gravir le plateau de Pratzen et d'y prendre position, Varé de suivre le mouvement du général Vandamme et de recevoir ses ordres, et moi de chasser l'ennemi du village de Pratzen, puis de rejoindre notre avant-garde sur le plateau vers lequel Saint-Hilaire se dirigea en même temps que Morand.
- **translation**: At the moment when, at break of day, Marshal Soult (I shall say later how we did not see him again that day) set us in motion, Morand received from General Saint-Hilaire the order to climb the Pratzen plateau and take position there, Varé to follow General Vandamme's movement and take his orders, and I to drive the enemy out of the village of Pratzen and then join our advance guard on the plateau, toward which Saint-Hilaire went at the same time as Morand.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Thiébault's brigade was to clear Pratzen village; set in motion 'à la pointe du jour' (words, not a clock hour; p. 456 has day at eight, §4.2). On the same page he says he reproduces observations he printed in 1806.

[...] и 1 декабря будет уже поблизости от Брюнна, пройдя еще 45 км. Таким образом, солдаты Фриана меньше чем за двое суток прошли 115 км почти без отдыха! (p. 38) Генерал Фриан выступил около 6 часов из Райгерна [...] неопубликованный рапорт Фриана, хранящийся в архиве исторической службы французской армии. «Она (дивизия), построившись поэшелонно, направлялась на Турас, — докладывал после битвы генерал Фриан своему непосредственному начальнику, — когда, дойдя до деревни Ребешовиц, мы получили приказ двинуться на Сокольниц» (p. 41) Наконец, в восемь с небольшим утра стало заметно движение союзников в районе Працена. [...] «Двадцать минут», — ответил маршал. (p. 61) Примерно в 16.30 огонь прекратился по всей линии войск Мюрата и Багратиона.
- **translation**: (p. 19) Friant's division of Davout's corps left Vienna on 29 November at 9 in the evening and reached Nikolsburg on 30 November at 10 in the evening, covering 70 km in a day [...] and on 1 December was already near Brünn after another 45 km. (p. 38) General Friant set off from Raigern about 6 [...] Friant's unpublished report, kept in the French army's historical service archive: 'It (the division), formed in echelons, was heading for Turas,' General Friant reported to his immediate superior after the battle, 'when, reaching the village of Rebeschowitz, we received the order to move on Sokolnitz' (p. 41) At last, a little after eight in the morning, the Allies' movement near Pratzen became visible. [...] 'Twenty minutes,' answered the marshal. (p. 61) At about 16:30 the firing ceased along the whole line of Murat's and Bagration's troops.

### C2.c7 `schoenhals1873.p162.erstiegen`

- **source**: `schoenhals1873`, p. 162 (leaf n166); images: https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n166.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Nur ein allgemeiner Angriff mit dem Bajonnete schien das einzige noch übrige Mittel zur Wieder-Eroberung dieser wichtigen Höhen. Er ward befohlen. Mit Muth stürzten sich die österreichischen Brigaden und Kamenskoi's Truppen auf den Feind; dieser wankte einen Augenblick, und die Höhe ward erstiegen. Das Regiment Salzburg und Auersperg nebst den Truppen Kamenskoi's schlugen sich mit ausgezeichneter Tapferkeit; der österreichische General Jurschek ward schwer verwundet.
- **translation**: Only a general bayonet attack seemed the one remaining means of retaking these important heights. It was ordered. The Austrian brigades and Kamensky's troops threw themselves bravely on the enemy; he wavered a moment, and the height was climbed. The Salzburg and Auersperg regiments with Kamensky's troops fought with outstanding bravery; the Austrian General Jurschek was badly wounded.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: Schönhals: in a second, joint attack the Austrians and Kamensky climbed the height for a time; Jurschek wounded; then driven off after Boyer's dragoons came up.
- **does not settle**: An hour; how long they held. Thiébault (p. 472) has the Austrian regiments dispersed by his guns.
- **bears on**: kollo@4; kollo@5; EVENTS[#kamensky]; PHASES[4].events[2].

## C3: Drouet, I Corps and the Guard (questions 157, 161)

### C3.1 `corr11_1863.p442.dispositions-ic`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9535, p. 442 (leaf n451); second reading of step-2 §1.2, extended; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n451_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Il est ordonné au général Caffarelli de se porter à sept heures du matin, avec sa division, pour se placer à la droite de la division Suchet, après avoir passé le ruisseau. [...] M. le maréchal Bernadotte, avec ses deux divisions d'infanterie, se portera, à sept heures du matin, sur la même position qu'occupe, aujourd'hui 10, la division du général Caffarelli, hormis que sa gauche sera à hauteur derrière le Santon, et y restera en colonne par régiment. M. le maréchal Lannes ordonnera à la division de grenadiers de se placer en bataille en avant de sa position actuelle, la gauche derrière la droite du général Caffarelli. Le général Oudinot fera reconnaître le débouché où il devra passer le ruisseau, lequel débouché sera le même par où aura passé le maréchal Soult.
- **translation**: General Caffarelli is ordered to move at seven in the morning with his division to place himself on the right of Suchet's division, after crossing the stream. [...] Marshal Bernadotte with his two infantry divisions will move at seven in the morning onto the same position that General Caffarelli's division occupies today, the 10th, except that his left will be level behind the Santon, and will remain there in column by regiment. Marshal Lannes will order the grenadier division to form in line of battle in front of its present position, its left behind General Caffarelli's right. General Oudinot will have the debouch reconnoitred where he is to cross the stream, which debouch will be the same by which Marshal Soult will have crossed.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact (what was ordered).
- **settles**: For 07:00 on 2 December: Caffarelli leaves his ground and crosses the stream; I Corps' two divisions take Caffarelli's ground of the 10th, left level behind the Santon, in column by regiment, and are to 'y rester'; the grenadier division is under Lannes's orders and is to cross by Soult's debouch.
- **does not settle**: Where Caffarelli's ground of 1 December was; whether the order was carried out; any later order to I Corps.
- **bears on**: c_i.role; PLANS.fr.cols[4]; FORMATIONS.drouet.track[0]; FORMATIONS.rivaud.track[0]; COMMAND.fr[4][1]; FORMATIONS.c_gren.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 1 freshly downloaded image: quotation exact, accept. Printed p. 442 on n451; no. 9535 'Dispositions générales pour la journée du 11', 10 frimaire, 8 h 1/2 du soir. All four sentences exact. Settles orders of 1 December, not their execution (correctly limited). Not read by a person.

### C3.2 `corr11_1863.p443.no-guard`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9535, p. 443 (leaf n452); images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n452_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): A sept heures et demie MM. les maréchaux se trouveront près de l'Empereur, à son bivouac, pour, selon les mouvements qu'aura faits l'ennemi pendant la nuit, donner de nouveaux ordres. La cavalerie de M. le maréchal Bernadotte, en conséquence des dispositions ci-dessus, est mise aux ordres du maréchal Murat [...]. Toutes les troupes resteront dans les dispositions indiquées ci-dessus, jusqu'à nouvel ordre.
- **translation**: At half past seven the marshals will be near the Emperor at his bivouac, to give new orders according to the movements the enemy has made in the night. Marshal Bernadotte's cavalry, in consequence of the above dispositions, is placed under Marshal Murat's orders [...]. All troops will remain in the dispositions given above until new orders.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The dispositions name neither the Imperial Guard, nor Bessières, nor 'la réserve' (pp. 442-443 read whole on the images); all troops stay as disposed until new orders given at 07:30.
- **does not settle**: What the new orders of the morning were.
- **bears on**: PLANS.fr.cols[4]; FORMATIONS.c_gd.role; COMMAND.fr[4][1].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 1 freshly downloaded image: quotation exact, accept. p. 443 on n452; exact (the cut covers the hour sentence and Murat's light-cavalry sentence). pp. 442-443 name no Guard, Bessières or 'réserve' (checked on both images). The next numbers printed are 9536 (3 December, 8 a.m., Bernadotte to pursue on the Austerlitz-Göding road) and 9537 (3 December): no order of the morning of 2 December is printed here. Not read by a person.

### C3.3 `corr11_1863.p450.reserve-defined`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, 30th Bulletin, no. 9541, p. 450 (leaf n459); extends step-2 §1.4; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n459_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): L'Empereur, avec son fidèle compagnon de guerre le maréchal Berthier, son premier aide de camp le colonel général Junot, et tout son état-major, se trouvait en réserve avec les dix bataillons de sa Garde et les dix bataillons de grenadiers du général Oudinot, dont le général Duroc commandait une partie. Cette réserve était rangée sur deux lignes, en colonnes par bataillon, à distance de déploiement, ayant dans les intervalles quarante pièces de canon servies par les canonniers de la Garde. C'est avec cette réserve que l'Empereur avait le projet de se précipiter partout où il eût été nécessaire. On peut dire que cette réserve seule valait une armée.
- **translation**: The Emperor, with his faithful companion in war Marshal Berthier, his first aide-de-camp Colonel-General Junot and all his staff, was in reserve with the ten battalions of his Guard and the ten battalions of General Oudinot's grenadiers, part of which General Duroc commanded. This reserve was drawn up on two lines, in columns by battalion at deploying distance, with forty guns served by the Guard's gunners in the intervals. It was with this reserve that the Emperor intended to throw himself wherever it was needed. One may say that this reserve alone was worth an army.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact (what the Bulletin says).
- **settles**: In the Bulletin 'the reserve' is the Guard's ten battalions and Oudinot's ten grenadier battalions with forty guns served by the Guard's gunners, with the Emperor; I Corps is 'le centre' (p. 449), not the reserve.
- **does not settle**: Where the reserve stood; the Guard cavalry's place; the Guard's own gun count.
- **bears on**: c_i.role; PLANS.fr.cols[4]; FORMATIONS.c_gd.strengthNote; FORMATIONS.c_gd.role; FORMATIONS.c_gren.role.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 1 freshly downloaded image: quotation exact, accept. p. 450 on n459; exact. Same page: 'Aux premiers rayons du soleil les ordres furent donnés, et chaque maréchal rejoignit son corps au grand galop.'. Not read by a person.

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, 30th Bulletin, p. 449 (leaf n458); second reading of step-2 §1.3; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n458_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Le maréchal Bernadotte, c'est-à-dire le centre, avait à sa gauche la division du général Rivaud, appuyée à la droite du prince Murat, et à sa droite la division du général Drouet.

### C3.5 `corr11_1863.p451.guard-centre-reserve`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, 30th Bulletin, p. 451 (leaf n460); second reading of step-2 §1.5, extended; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n460_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Un bataillon du 4e de ligne fut chargé par la garde impériale russe à cheval, et culbuté; mais l'Empereur n'était pas loin; il s'aperçut de ce mouvement, il ordonna au maréchal Bessières de se porter au secours de sa droite avec ses invincibles, et bientôt les deux gardes furent aux mains. Le succès ne pouvait être douteux : dans un moment la garde russe fut en déroute; colonel, artillerie, étendards, tout fut enlevé. [...] Des hauteurs d'Austerlitz, les deux empereurs virent la défaite de toute la garde russe. Au même moment, le centre de l'armée, commandé par le maréchal Bernadotte, s'avança. Trois de ses régiments soutinrent une très-belle charge de cavalerie. La gauche, commandée par le maréchal Lannes, donna plusieurs fois [...]. A une heure après midi la victoire était décidée. Elle n'avait pas été un moment douteuse. Pas un homme de la réserve n'avait été nécessaire et n'avait donné nulle part.
- **translation**: A battalion of the 4th of the line was charged by the Russian Imperial Guard horse and overthrown; but the Emperor was not far off; he saw this movement and ordered Marshal Bessières to go to the help of his right with his invincibles, and soon the two Guards were at grips. Success could not be in doubt: in a moment the Russian Guard was routed; colonel, artillery, standards, all were taken. [...] From the heights of Austerlitz the two emperors saw the defeat of the whole Russian Guard. At the same moment the centre of the army, commanded by Marshal Bernadotte, advanced. Three of its regiments withstood a very fine cavalry charge. The left, commanded by Marshal Lannes, engaged several times [...]. At one in the afternoon the victory was decided. It had not been in doubt for a moment. Not one man of the reserve had been needed or had engaged anywhere.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact (the Bulletin's claim); disputed for when I Corps moved.
- **settles**: Bessières's Guard answers the Russian Guard cavalry; the centre advances at the moment of the Russian Guard's rout and three of its regiments withstand a cavalry charge; with p. 450 the reserve that 'n'avait donné nulle part' is the Guard's ten battalions and the grenadiers.
- **does not settle**: Which three regiments or which division; whether the centre had crossed before; clock hours of the Guard fight ('une heure après midi' is the victory's).
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.drouet.track[6]; FORMATIONS.c_gd.role; FORMATIONS.guard_inf.track[6]; FORMATIONS.c_gren.track[8]; EVENTS[#guard-broken].forms; PHASES[6].lede; ANALYSIS[#guard].text.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 1 freshly downloaded image: quotation exact, accept. p. 451 on n460; exact (cut: 'Le régiment du grand-duc Constantin fut écrasé; lui-même ne dut son salut qu'à la vitesse de son cheval.'). Bessières is sent 'au secours de sa droite' (the Emperor's right). Same page, before the Russian Guard is sent in: 'Il n'y avait pas une heure que l'on se battait, et toute la gauche de l'ennemi était coupée' (a sequence, no clock). The centre 'Au même moment' advances, i.e. at the Russian Guard's defeat. Not read by a person.

### C3.6 `rapp1823.p60-61.guard-charge`

- **source**: `rapp1823`, pp. 60-61 (leaves n79-n80); images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugnralr00rappgoog/page/n79_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugnralr00rappgoog/page/n80_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Il était déjà une heure, et la bataille était loin de se décider pour eux. Ils résolurent de tenter au centre un dernier effort. La garde impériale se déploya; infanterie, cavalerie, artillerie, marchèrent sur le pont sans que Napoléon aperçût ce mouvement, que lui dérobaient les accidents du terrain. Un feu de mousqueterie se fit bientôt entendre, c'était une brigade commandée par le général Schinner que les Russes enfonçaient. Napoléon m'ordonna de prendre les Mamelouks, deux escadrons de chasseurs, un de grenadiers de la garde, et de me porter en avant pour reconnaître l'état des choses. [...] La cavalerie était au milieu de nos car- / rés, et sabrait nos soldats. Un peu en arrière nous discernions les masses à pied et à cheval qui formaient la réserve. [...] Un escadron de grenadiers à cheval vint me renforcer, je fus à même de recevoir les réserves qui arrivaient au secours de la garde russe.
- **translation**: It was already one o'clock, and the battle was far from being decided for them. They resolved to try a last effort in the centre. The [Russian] Imperial Guard deployed; infantry, cavalry, artillery marched on the bridge without Napoleon seeing the movement, hidden from him by the ground. Musketry was soon heard: it was a brigade commanded by General Schinner that the Russians were breaking. Napoleon ordered me to take the Mamelukes, two squadrons of chasseurs and one of the Guard's grenadiers and go forward to see how things stood. [...] The cavalry was among our squares, sabring our soldiers. A little behind we made out the masses on foot and on horse that formed the reserve. [...] A squadron of horse grenadiers came to reinforce me; I was able to receive the reserves coming to the Russian Guard's help.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact (what Rapp says).
- **settles**: In Rapp the French sent against the Russian Guard are Guard cavalry only; the troops broken are Schiner's brigade (of Vandamme's division: inference from the name); 'la réserve' here is the Russian; his clock: 'déjà une heure' when the Russian Guard attacked.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: 'the French sent against the Russian Guard are Guard cavalry only': p. 61 also has 'Quatre pièces d'artillerie arrivèrent au galop et se mirent en batterie' with Rapp's charge. 'la réserve here is the Russian' is an inference from context ('les réserves qui arrivaient au secours de la garde russe'), to be labelled so.
- **does not settle**: Anything about I Corps or the French Guard infantry (neither named on pp. 60-62).
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.c_gd.role; FORMATIONS.guard_inf.track[6]; EVENTS[#guard-broken].forms; FORMATIONS.drouet.track[6].

### C3.8 `savary1828.p205-208.bernadotte-division`

- **source**: `savary1828`, t. II, pp. 205-208 (leaves n216-n219; headers of 206-208 read); images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresduducder04savagoog/page/n216_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoiresduducder04savagoog/page/n217_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoiresduducder04savagoog/page/n218_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoiresduducder04savagoog/page/n219_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): L'empereur fit de suite marcher une des divisions du maréchal Bernadotte derrière la division Vandamme, et une portion des grenadiers réunis derrière celle de Saint-Hilaire. [...] (p. 206) Le général Vandamme, dirigé par le maréchal Soult, et appuyé d'une division de Bernadotte, fit un changement de direction par le flanc droit [...]. Ce mouvement réussit pleinement, et les deux divisions, réunies sur le Pratzer même par ce mouvement, n'eurent plus besoin des secours de la division Bernadotte [...]. (p. 207) L'empereur fit appuyer le mouvement par les grenadiers réunis et la division de la garde à pied; il eut un plein succès et décida la bataille. Le général Vandamme, en commençant son premier changement de direction à droite, eut un échec. Le 4e régiment de ligne perdit une de ses aigles dans une charge de cavalerie exécutée sur lui par la garde russe; mais les chasseurs de la garde et les grenadiers de service près de l'empereur chargèrent si à propos, que cet accident n'eut pas de suites. C'est après le deuxième changement de direction à droite de la même division Vandamme, alors en communication avec Saint-Hilaire, que l'empereur ordonna à celle des divisions de Bernadotte qui suivait le mouvement d'aller droit devant elle, et de ne plus suivre la direction de Vandamme. Cette division le fit; elle combattit l'infanterie de la garde russe, l'enfonça et la mena battant une bonne lieue; mais elle revint à sa position, on ne put savoir pourquoi. L'empereur, qui avait suivi le mouvement de la division Vandamme, fut fort étonné, en revenant le soir, de trouver cette division de Bernadotte sur la place d'où il l'avait lancée lui-même le matin. (p. 208) d'être mécontent du mouvement rétrograde de cette division.
- **translation**: The Emperor at once had one of Marshal Bernadotte's divisions march behind Vandamme's division, and part of the united grenadiers behind Saint-Hilaire's. [...] General Vandamme, directed by Marshal Soult and supported by a division of Bernadotte's, wheeled by the right flank [...]. This succeeded fully, and the two divisions, united on the Pratzer itself by this movement, no longer needed the help of Bernadotte's division [...]. The Emperor had the movement supported by the united grenadiers and the division of the Guard on foot; it succeeded fully and decided the battle. General Vandamme, beginning his first change of direction to the right, suffered a check. The 4th of the line lost one of its eagles in a cavalry charge made on it by the Russian Guard; but the Guard's chasseurs and the grenadiers on duty near the Emperor charged so opportunely that the accident had no consequences. It was after the second change of direction to the right of the same Vandamme division, then in touch with Saint-Hilaire, that the Emperor ordered the one of Bernadotte's divisions that was following the movement to go straight ahead and no longer follow Vandamme's direction. This division did so; it fought the Russian Guard infantry, broke it and drove it a good league; but it returned to its position, no one could tell why. The Emperor, who had followed Vandamme's division, was much astonished on coming back in the evening to find this division of Bernadotte's on the place from which he himself had launched it in the morning. [...] to be displeased with this division's retrograde movement.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact (what Savary says); disputed (Thiébault p. 466 and the Materialien's note l).
- **settles**: In Savary one unnamed I Corps division was sent behind Vandamme as soon as Vandamme had taken the position, followed him, then went straight ahead, fought and broke the Russian Guard infantry and drove it 'une bonne lieue', then returned to its morning place; part of the grenadiers went behind Saint-Hilaire; the grenadiers and 'la division de la garde à pied' supported the wheel off the Pratzen; the Guard's chasseurs and the 'grenadiers de service' (cavalry, by the verb 'chargèrent' and Rapp: inference) answered the Russian Guard cavalry.
- **does not settle**: Which division (Drouet or Rivaud); any clock hour; what the other I Corps division did.
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.drouet.track[3]; FORMATIONS.drouet.track[6]; FORMATIONS.rivaud.track[3]; PHASES[6].lede; ANALYSIS[#guard].text; FORMATIONS.rg_inf.track[7]; COMMAND.fr[3][1]; TOUR[5].x; COMMAND.fr[7][1]; FORMATIONS.c_gren.track[6]; FORMATIONS.c_gren.track[7]; FORMATIONS.guard_inf.track[7].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 4 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept. n216-n219 read; pp. 205-208 confirmed; every cut segment exact. p. 205 also: Saint-Hilaire's fire 'dura deux heures'. 'grenadiers de service' as cavalry stays inference (flagged by the researcher). Sequence (pp. 206-207, re-read in the second attempt): the Russian Guard cavalry's charge on the 4e falls at Vandamme's FIRST change of direction; the Bernadotte division is sent straight ahead and fights the Russian Guard infantry only 'après le deuxième changement de direction à droite', the descent from the Pratzer onto the rear of the troops facing Legrand: in Savary that fight comes after the wheel south begins, later than the map's phase 6. The 'mouvement' supported by the grenadiers and the Guard on foot (p. 207) is the whole semicircle, the descent included (p. 206: 'elles avaient ainsi parcouru le demi-cercle complet'). ### C3.9 `segur1873.p464-465.plan-bernadotte`

- **source**: `segur1877`, t. II, pp. 464-465 (leaves n485-n486; headers read); images: https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n485_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n486_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Bernadotte, arrivant obliquement de notre gauche, dut assaillir à la fois, de ce côté, ce même plateau. Cet effort simultané, de quatre divisions, sur le centre des Austro-Russes dégarni par leur mouvement en avant et à leur gauche, l'Empereur lui-même, avec sa double réserve des grenadiers réunis et de sa Garde, le soutiendra! [...] (p. 465) Quand il en fut à Bernadotte, l'accent de sa voix devint remarquablement plus sec et plus impérieux; et comme, peu d'instants après, les deux divisions de ce maréchal se portaient au point d'attaque, lui-même les harangua.
- **translation**: Bernadotte, coming obliquely from our left, was to assail that same plateau from that side at the same time. This simultaneous effort of four divisions on the Austro-Russian centre, stripped by their movement forward and to their left, the Emperor himself, with his double reserve of the united grenadiers and his Guard, will support! [...] When he came to Bernadotte, the tone of his voice became remarkably drier and more imperious; and as, a few moments later, that marshal's two divisions were moving to the point of attack, he harangued them himself.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact (what Ségur says).
- **settles**: In Ségur the morning orders give Bernadotte's two divisions a part with Soult's two in the assault on the plateau ('quatre divisions'), from the left; the reserve is the grenadiers and the Guard, with the Emperor; I Corps moved toward the point of attack moments after the marshals were dismissed.
- **does not settle**: When it crossed; by what route; whether it climbed in the morning (see his p. 467).

### C3.10 `segur1873.p467-468.midi-bernadotte`

- **source**: `segur1877`, t. II, pp. 467-468 (leaves n488-n489); images: https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n488_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n489_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Vers onze heures tout avait réussi selon les prévisions de l'Empereur. [...] Vers midi j'y retrouvai l'Empereur. Je revenais, par son ordre, d'appeler sa Garde à pied et de conduire sur une éminence, en arrière du ruisseau et de ce plateau, les grenadiers réunis de la réserve. Lui-même, avec la cavalerie de sa Garde, venait de s'y avancer et d'y faire monter à sa gauche Bernadotte. Il se / défiait de ce maréchal; il m'envoya lui renouveler ses ordres et en observer l'exécution. Je le trouvai à pied à la tête de son infanterie. [...] il se plaignait, à trop haute voix, de n'avoir point un seul escadron à leur opposer; [...] Napoléon me répondit avec impatience : « Hé! il sait bien que je n'en ai point de « disponible! » Il venait, en ce moment, de placer en avant de lui tout ce qu'il en avait sous la main. C'était, avec une batterie d'artillerie, toute la cavalerie de sa Garde! [...] Il était une heure. Napoléon, au sommet de ce plateau dominateur, voyait devant lui la Garde d'Alexandre s'avançant en masse pour l'en chasser et le lui reprendre.
- **translation**: About eleven everything had succeeded as the Emperor foresaw. [...] About noon I rejoined the Emperor there. I was coming back, by his order, from calling up his Guard on foot and leading the united grenadiers of the reserve to an eminence behind the stream and this plateau. He himself, with his Guard cavalry, had just advanced there and had Bernadotte climb up on his left. He distrusted this marshal; he sent me to renew his orders and watch them carried out. I found him on foot at the head of his infantry. [...] he complained, too loudly, of not having a single squadron to oppose to them; [...] Napoleon answered me impatiently: 'Eh! he knows very well I have none available!' He had just placed in front of himself all he had to hand: with a battery, the whole of his Guard cavalry! [...] It was one o'clock. Napoleon, on the top of this commanding plateau, saw before him Alexander's Guard advancing in mass to drive him off it and retake it.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact (what Ségur says); disputed (Savary: a Bernadotte division behind Vandamme 'de suite'; the Bulletin: the centre advanced at the Russian Guard's rout).
- **settles**: In Ségur about noon the Emperor, with the Guard cavalry, has advanced onto the plateau and has had Bernadotte climb on his left; Bernadotte at the head of his infantry has no cavalry; the Guard on foot was being called up and the grenadiers led to an eminence behind the stream and the plateau; 'Il était une heure' when the Russian Guard advanced in mass.
- **does not settle**: Which I Corps division; when I Corps crossed the stream.

### C3.11 `segur1873.p469.grenadiers-duroc`

- **source**: `segur1877`, t. II, p. 469 (leaf n490); images: https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n490_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): C'était en effet la division des grenadiers réunis de notre réserve. Duroc, apercevant les progrès de la gauche russe contre notre droite, marchait du côté des lacs, au secours de Soult et de Davout. Il y avait à peine quelques minutes que mon retour avait rassuré l'Empereur sur ses derrières, que, devant lui, l'attaque de la Garde à cheval d'Alexandre commença. Elle fut si impétueuse que les deux bataillons de gauche de Vandamme en furent écrasés! [...] Ce bataillon était du 4me régiment.
- **translation**: It was indeed the united grenadier division of our reserve. Duroc, seeing the Russian left's progress against our right, was marching toward the lakes to help Soult and Davout. Scarcely a few minutes after my return had reassured the Emperor about his rear, the attack of Alexander's Horse Guard began in front of him. It was so impetuous that Vandamme's two left battalions were crushed! [...] That battalion was of the 4th regiment.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: In Ségur the grenadiers under Duroc were marching south toward the lakes before the Russian Guard cavalry's attack; the attack crushed Vandamme's two left battalions, one of the 4e.

### C3.12 `segur1873.p470-472.guard-bernadotte`

- **source**: `segur1877`, t. II, pp. 470-472 (leaves n491-n493); images: https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n491_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n492_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n493_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): il envoya Raap à la cavalerie de sa Garde. [...] Sur ce sommet, l'Empereur était à deux ou trois pas en avant de nous [...]. La Garde à pied, l'escadron de service lui-même, étaient à une assez grande distance, en arrière à droite. [...] (p. 471) Cette victoire, c'était en effet Raap, avec les Mamelouks dont il était colonel et les Chasseurs à cheval de la Garde, qui venait de la décider. La cavalerie et l'artillerie de la Garde russe avaient été sabrées et culbutées; Ordener et ses grenadiers à cheval avaient achevé. [...] D'autres lignes de morts, de blessés, et de sacs de fantassins [...] indiquaient les autres positions où venait de succomber l'infanterie de la Garde ennemie, dont Bernadotte allait compléter la défaite. [...] (p. 472) Dès lors, sûr de sa victoire au centre et à sa gauche, où il sait que triomphent Lannes et Murat, il laisse Bernadotte et sa Garde à pied sur le plateau. Toute son attention se retourne, en arrière à sa droite, du côté des bas-fonds des lacs.
- **translation**: He sent Rapp to his Guard cavalry. [...] On this summit the Emperor was two or three paces in front of us [...]. The Guard on foot, even the duty squadron, were at a fair distance, behind on the right. [...] This victory was indeed Rapp's, with the Mamelukes of whom he was colonel and the Guard's chasseurs à cheval, who had just decided it. The Russian Guard's cavalry and artillery had been sabred and overthrown; Ordener and his horse grenadiers had finished it. [...] Other lines of dead, wounded and infantry packs [...] marked the other positions where the enemy Guard's infantry had just succumbed, whose defeat Bernadotte was about to complete. [...] From then on, sure of his victory in the centre and on his left, where he knows Lannes and Murat are winning, he leaves Bernadotte and his Guard on foot on the plateau. All his attention turns back, behind on his right, toward the low ground of the lakes.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: In Ségur the Russian Guard was broken by the Guard cavalry (Rapp with Mamelukes and chasseurs, Ordener's horse grenadiers finishing); the Guard on foot was at a fair distance behind on the right during the charge; Bernadotte was 'about to complete' the defeat of the Russian Guard infantry; afterwards Bernadotte and the Guard on foot were left on the plateau while the Emperor turned south.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: 'the Guard on foot was at a fair distance behind on the right during the charge': the sentence describes the scene on the summit when Rapp came back from the charge ('Raap, en arrivant'), not during it.
- **does not settle**: Which I Corps division; whether the Guard on foot fired.

### C3.13 `coignet1909.p121-123.guard-followed`

- **source**: `coignet1909`, pp. 121-123 (leaves n152-n154; headers read); images: https://archive.org/download/lescahiersducapi00coig/page/n152_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/lescahiersducapi00coig/page/n153_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/lescahiersducapi00coig/page/n154_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Nous voici au 2 décembre; l'Empereur partit de grand matin pour visiter ses avant-postes et voir la position de l'armée russe; il revint sur un plateau au-dessus de celui où il avait passé la nuit; il nous fait mettre en bataille derrière lui avec les grenadiers d'Oudinot. Tous ses maréchaux étaient près de lui; il les fit partir à leur poste. L'armée montait ce mamelon pour redescendre dans les bas-fonds, franchir un ruis- / seau et arriver au pied de la montagne de Pratzen [...]. Lorsque les colonnes furent passées, l'Empereur nous fit suivre le mouvement. Nous étions vingt-cinq mille bonnets à poils, et des gaillards. [...] Du premier choc nos soldats enfoncèrent la première ligne, et nous, derrière les soldats, la seconde ligne. [...] Leur cavalerie s'avança sur un bataillon du 4e [...]. L'Empereur l'aperçoit et dit au général Rapp de charger. Rapp s'élance avec les chasseurs à cheval et les mamelucks, délivre le bataillon, mais est ramené par la garde russe. Le maréchal Bessières part au galop avec les grenadiers à cheval qui prennent la revanche. [...] On nous avait fait avancer au pas / de charge pour soutenir cette masse et nous croyions notre tour arrivé, mais ils battirent en retraite dans la vallée des étangs. [...] l'Empereur, qui s'aperçut de leur embarras, fait descendre son artillerie et le 2e régiment de grenadiers. [...] Le soir, nous couchâmes sur la belle position que la garde russe occupait le matin
- **translation**: Here we are at 2 December; the Emperor left very early to visit his outposts and see the Russian army's position; he came back to a plateau above the one where he had spent the night; he has us form in line behind him with Oudinot's grenadiers. All his marshals were near him; he sent them to their posts. The army climbed this hillock to go down again into the low ground, cross a stream and reach the foot of the Pratzen hill [...]. When the columns had passed, the Emperor had us follow the movement. We were twenty-five thousand bearskins, and fine fellows. [...] At the first shock our soldiers broke the first line, and we, behind the soldiers, the second line. [...] Their cavalry advanced on a battalion of the 4th [...]. The Emperor sees it and tells General Rapp to charge. Rapp dashes off with the chasseurs à cheval and the Mamelukes, frees the battalion, but is driven back by the Russian Guard. Marshal Bessières sets off at the gallop with the horse grenadiers, who take revenge. [...] We had been advanced at the charge step to support this mass and thought our turn had come, but they retreated into the valley of the ponds. [...] the Emperor, seeing their difficulty, sends down his artillery and the 2nd grenadier regiment. [...] In the evening we slept on the fine position the Russian Guard had held in the morning
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact (what Coignet says; his 'vingt-cinq mille bonnets à poils' is not credible as a count: inference).
- **settles**: In Coignet the Guard infantry, formed behind the Emperor with Oudinot's grenadiers at dawn, followed once the columns had passed, went onto the Pratzen, was advanced to support the Guard cavalry but did not come into action ('nous croyions notre tour arrivé, mais...'); the Guard's artillery and the 2e grenadiers were sent down toward the ponds; it slept on the Russian Guard's morning position.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: 'did not come into action': the first cut hides p. 122, where Coignet writes 'Du premier choc nos soldats enfoncèrent la première ligne, et nous, derrière les soldats, la seconde ligne. On perça le centre de leur armée et nous fûmes maîtres du plateau de Pratzen', in its plain sense the Guard breaking the Russian second line (counter-reading coignet1909.p122.second-line). Coignet both claims that and says 'nous croyions notre tour arrivé, mais ils battirent en retraite' of the Guard-cavalry fight (p. 123). The reading must carry both sides.
- **does not settle**: Hours; 'et nous, derrière les soldats, la seconde ligne' is ambiguous (following, not fighting: inference).
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.guard_inf.track[0]; FORMATIONS.guard_inf.track[6]; FORMATIONS.guard_inf.track[7]; FORMATIONS.c_gd.role; PLANS.fr.staging[1].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 3 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept with corrections. n152-n154; pp. 121-123 confirmed; every quoted segment exact. Also p. 122: 'Lorsque les colonnes furent passées, l'Empereur nous fit suivre le mouvement' (the Guard followed as soon as the columns had crossed: early, against Ségur's call at noon) and 'on nous fit appuyer fortement à droite'; p. 123: the Guard's artillery fires on the ice. Coignet is loose (25,000 bearskins): B kept. Not read by a person.

### C3.15 `stutterheim1806de.p56.rivaud-skirmishers`

Kaum war er auf diesem Puncte angelangt, als er sogleich auf die Plänkler von der Division Rivaud und von der leichten Cavallerie des Fürsten Murat unter Commando des General Kellermann stieß, und mit ihnen ins Gefecht verwickelt war.

### C3.16 `stutterheim1806de.p62.bernadotte-column`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, p. 62 (leaf n65); images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n65_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Jetzt ließ sich eine dritte feindliche Colonne links von Pratzen sehen, und drohte, in den Raum zwischen der 4ten Colonne und der Cavallerie des Fürsten Johann von Liechtenstein einzubrechen. Diese Französische Colonne war von dem Corps des Marschall Bernadotte. Die Russische Infanterie von der 4ten Colonne marschirte nun unverzüglich rechts gegen Pratzen
- **translation**: Now a third enemy column showed itself left of Pratzen and threatened to break into the space between the 4th Column and Prince Johann Liechtenstein's cavalry. This French column was of Marshal Bernadotte's corps. The Russian infantry of the 4th Column now at once marched right toward Pratzen
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact; disputed (the Bulletin p. 451; Thiébault pp. 464-466); the side ('links') disagrees with the French original ('sur la droite de Pratzen', stutterheim1806fr.p87).

What the 4th Column saw is closer to the author's own side than his report of French orders (inference).

### C3.17 `stutterheim1806de.p68-69.guard-line`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, pp. 68-69 (leaves n71-n72); second reading of step-2 §2.5's pages, extended; images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n71_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n72_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Das Dorf Blasowitz [...] wurde von den Truppen des Corps des Marschall Bernadotte angegriffen und genommen. Der Großfürst wollte den Feind aufhalten, verließ die dominirenden Anhöhen, worauf er stand, und rückte in Linie gegen die feindlichen Massen an. [...] aber in dem Augenblicke, da sich dieser Prinz den damahls schon in Linie stehenden Franzosen näherte, hieb die Cavallerie der Garden, welche von dem Marschall Bessieres commandirt war, und in den Zwischenräumen der Infanterie stand, in die Russische Linie ein, die somit, weil sie ohne Unterstützung war, nach einer tapferen Gegenwehr weichen mußte. (p. 69) Um der Infanterie Luft zu machen, that das Garde-Regiment zu Pferd des Großfürsten einen Anfall auf die Flanke der Franzosen, hielt selbst die Cavallerie auf, warf sie, und griff die feindliche Infanterie an, welche zur Unterstützung der Cavallerie vorgerückt war. Bey dieser Gelegenheit eroberte das Garde-Regiment zu Pferd einen Französischen Adler von einem Bataillon des vierten Regiments. [...] Hierauf erfolgte der Rückzug der Russischen Garden nach Austerlitz, ohne dabey von den Franzosen beunruhigt zu werden, welche auf den Anhöhen vorwärts von Blasowitz stehen blieben.
- **translation**: The village of Blasowitz [...] was attacked and taken by the troops of Marshal Bernadotte's corps. The Grand Duke, wishing to stop the enemy, left the commanding heights on which he stood and advanced in line against the enemy masses. [...] but at the moment this prince approached the French, by then already formed in line, the Guard cavalry, commanded by Marshal Bessières and standing in the intervals of the infantry, cut into the Russian line, which, being unsupported, had to give way after a brave resistance. To relieve the infantry the Grand Duke's Horse Guard regiment attacked the French flank, itself stopped the cavalry, threw it back and attacked the enemy infantry which had advanced in support of the cavalry. On this occasion the Horse Guard took a French eagle from a battalion of the fourth regiment. [...] Then the Russian Guards retreated to Austerlitz without being troubled by the French, who stayed on the heights in front of Blasowitz.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact; disputed (the map's Guard fight against Vandamme at Stare Vinohrady; Ségur p. 469 and Savary p. 207: the 4e of Vandamme's division).
- **settles**: In Stutterheim the Russian Guard fights French 'masses' near Blasowitz after Bernadotte's corps took the village; the French were 'schon in Linie'; Bessières's Guard cavalry stood 'in den Zwischenräumen der Infanterie'; French infantry advanced in support of the cavalry and lost the 4e's eagle; the French stayed on the heights in front of Blasowitz.
- **does not settle**: That the infantry in line was I Corps' (the context, Blasowitz just taken by Bernadotte's corps, suggests it: inference); which division; any hour.
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.drouet.track[6]; PHASES[6].lede; ANALYSIS[#guard].text; FORMATIONS.rg_inf.track[7]; EVENTS[#guard-broken].forms; FORMATIONS.c_gd.role.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 2 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept. n71-n72; pp. 68, 69 exact. p. 69 also: the Chevalier Guards fought 'mit den Grenadiers zu Pferd von der Französischen Garde, die unter dem General Rapp gekommen waren'. Note for the proposals: 'den damahls schon in Linie stehenden Franzosen' is a French line already formed, whose infantry is not named. Not read by a person.

### C3.21 `mikhailovsky1846.p245-246.bernadotte-left`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1846`, pp. 245-246 (leaves n271-n272); images: https://archive.org/download/relationdelacam00dangoog/page/n271_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/relationdelacam00dangoog/page/n272_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Ce mouvement de notre avant-garde avait lieu précisément au moment où Napoléon, bien assuré que notre aile gauche entrait dans les défilés de Tellnitz et de Sokolnitz, commençait son attaque sur Pratzen. Soult marchait à sa droite, Berna- / dotte à sa gauche; ils étaient suivis par Murat, à la tête de la cavalerie, par Oudinot et Bessières, avec la garde. Napoléon s'était placé entre les corps destinés à l'attaque et ceux de la réserve. A peine Miloradowitch eut-il débouché du village de Pratzen, et commencé à gagner la plaine, qu'il aperçut ce déploiement formidable de l'ennemi.
- **translation**: This movement of our advance guard took place exactly when Napoleon, sure that our left wing was entering the defiles of Telnitz and Sokolnitz, began his attack on Pratzen. Soult marched on his right, Bernadotte on his left; they were followed by Murat at the head of the cavalry, by Oudinot and Bessières with the Guard. Napoleon had placed himself between the corps meant for the attack and those of the reserve. ### C3.22 `mikhailovsky1846.p259.guard-columns`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1846`, p. 259 (leaf n285); pp. 260-261 OCR only; images: https://archive.org/download/relationdelacam00dangoog/page/n285_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image, part on the text layer (see the source line)): Le grand-duc Constantin, ne recevant aucune nouvelle de la marche des affaires, se trouvait dans une position très-pénible : livré à ses propres forces, qui se composaient seulement de 6 bataillons et de 10 escadrons, sans aucune réserve [...]. il prit à gauche pour se joindre à notre centre, déjà culbuté, couvrant ce mouvement de flanc par des tirailleurs. Après avoir fait une faible marche dans cette direction, on aperçut, à droite, les colonnes françaises. La garde se forma, attaqua à la baïonnette, renversa les deux premières colonnes; mais nos troupes, élancées à leur poursuite, furent bientôt atteintes par les batteries et mises en désordre. Elles perdirent une pièce de canon. La cavalerie française attaqua le régiment de Séménowski, et fut repoussée. [...] Les mameloucks de la garde impériale française atteignirent le régiment de Préobragenski
- **translation**: Grand Duke Constantine, receiving no news of the course of things, was in a very difficult position: left to his own forces, only 6 battalions and 10 squadrons, without any reserve [...]. The Guard formed, attacked with the bayonet, overthrew the first two columns; but our troops, rushing in pursuit, were soon caught by the batteries and thrown into disorder. They lost a gun. The French cavalry attacked the Semenovsky regiment and was repulsed. [...] The Mamelukes of the French Imperial Guard reached the Preobrazhensky regiment
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: In M-D the Russian Guard overthrew 'les deux premières colonnes' of unnamed French columns, then was checked by batteries; the French Guard's Mamelukes and (p. 261 OCR) horse grenadiers and 'chasseurs-guides' fought it; no French Guard infantry and no I Corps unit is named in pp. 259-261.

- **does not settle**: Whose columns.

### C3.23 `mikhailovsky1844.p172.night-move`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, p. 172 (IIIF $182, hOCR k=183); the Russian original of step-2 §5.1; images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$182/full/1000,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image (the sentence's first words on p. 171 in the OCR)): корпуса Сульта и Бернадота черезъ ручей, поставилъ ихъ впереди Гиршковица, Пунтовица и Кобельница, и подвинулъ за ними резервы, назначая всѣ сіи войска ударить на нашъ центръ. Въ десять часовъ вечера кончилось передвиженіе, исполненное во мракѣ [...]. Единогласно признаютъ военные писатели однимъ изъ самыхъ смѣлыхъ дѣйствій Наполеона рѣшеніе поставить передъ сраженіемъ два корпуса впереди дефилей.
