- **translation**: [when dusk fell he moved] the corps of Soult and Bernadotte across the brook, placed them in front of Girschikowitz, Puntowitz and Kobelnitz, and moved the reserves up behind them, intending all these troops to strike our centre. At ten in the evening the movement, carried out in darkness, was finished [...]. Military writers agree in counting among Napoleon's boldest acts the decision to place two corps in front of the defiles before the battle.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed (Stutterheim pp. 55-56: the crossing at the time of Soult's attack; Barrès p. 75 and Savary p. 200: I Corps behind; no. 9535: to Caffarelli's ground at 07:00).

M-D continues (pp. 172-173, $183 read): 'Вымышленныя похвалы не нужны дивному полководцу. Сдѣланное имъ въ вечеру передвиженіе, и вообще распоряженія его къ Аустерлицкой битвѣ основывались на извѣстности ему предположеній нашихъ. (*)', note (*) Dolgorukov's report to the Emperor of 6 December ('nos dispositions étoient connues de l'ennemi'). The night move is M-D's own statement (B), its source not stated on these pages; the 10 p.m. end is M-D's hour.

### C3.29 `barres_etoile.p75-78.reserve-marched`

- **source**: `barres_etoile`, pp. 75-78 of the electronic re-edition (leaves n74-n77; page numbers read on the images); images: https://archive.org/download/jean-baptiste-barres-souvenirs-dun-officier-de-la-grande-armee-fr-1923-www.-etoile.-app/page/n74_w1000.jpg, https://archive.org/download/jean-baptiste-barres-souvenirs-dun-officier-de-la-grande-armee-fr-1923-www.-etoile.-app/page/n75_w1000.jpg, https://archive.org/download/jean-baptiste-barres-souvenirs-dun-officier-de-la-grande-armee-fr-1923-www.-etoile.-app/page/n76_w1000.jpg, https://archive.org/download/jean-baptiste-barres-souvenirs-dun-officier-de-la-grande-armee-fr-1923-www.-etoile.-app/page/n77_w1000.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image (of the modern re-edition)): toute la Garde, les grenadiers réunis, le 5ème corps qui était à notre gauche et en avant de nous, le 4ème à droite, ainsi que le 3ème plus loin et en avant, enfin, le 1er qui était à une demi-lieue en arrière, en firent autant. [...] L'Empereur, entouré de ses maréchaux et des généraux d'élite de son armée, était placé sur un mamelon dont j'ai parlé, distribuant des ordres pour la disposition de ses troupes et attendant que le brouillard se dissipât pour donner le signal de l'attaque. Il fut donné, et, peu de temps après, toute cette immense ligne fut en feu. Pendant ce temps là, le 1er corps, qui était derrière, se porta en avant, en passant à droite et à gauche du mamelon. [...] le maréchal Bernadotte en tête [...]. (p. 76) Après le passage du 1er corps, notre mouvement commença; nous formions la réserve : elle se composait de 20 bataillons d'élite, dont 8 de la Garde impériale, 2 de la garde royale italienne, et 10 de grenadiers et de voltigeurs réunis. Derrière nous, marchaient la cavalerie de la Garde et plusieurs bataillons de dragons à pied. [...] Dans cet ordre, nous franchîmes la plaine et gravîmes les hauteurs [...]. Parvenus sur le plateau que les Russes occupaient quelques instants auparavant, l'Empereur nous arrêta pour nous haranguer [...] « Chasseurs, mes gardes à cheval viennent de mettre en déroute la Garde impériale russe [...] » [...] Après un quart d'heure de repos, l'infanterie de la Garde fit un changement de direction, à droite, pour aller seconder le 4ème corps, en marchant sur les hauteurs. [...] (p. 78) Il n'y avait point d'action personnelle à citer, puisqu'on n'avait fait que marcher
- **translation**: the whole Guard, the united grenadiers, the 5th corps on our left and in front of us, the 4th on the right, the 3rd further off and in front, and lastly the 1st, half a league behind, did the same [lit torches, the night of 1 December]. [...] The Emperor, surrounded by his marshals and the chosen generals of his army, stood on a hillock I have mentioned, giving orders for the disposition of his troops and waiting for the fog to clear to give the signal of attack. It was given, and soon after the whole immense line was in fire. Meanwhile the 1st corps, which was behind, moved forward, passing right and left of the hillock [...] Marshal Bernadotte at its head [...]. After the 1st corps had passed, our movement began; we formed the reserve: 20 elite battalions, 8 of the Imperial Guard, 2 of the Italian royal guard and 10 of united grenadiers and voltigeurs. Behind us marched the Guard cavalry and several battalions of dismounted dragoons. [...] In this order we crossed the plain and climbed the heights [...]. Reaching the plateau the Russians had held a few moments before, the Emperor halted us to harangue us [...] 'Chasseurs, my horse guards have just routed the Russian Imperial Guard [...]' [...] After a quarter of an hour's rest the Guard infantry wheeled right to go and support the 4th corps, marching along the heights. [...] There was no personal action to tell of, since we had only marched
- **grade**: B (the memoir), read in a modern re-edition whose fidelity to the 1923 printing is not checked: the reading is 'uncertain' until checked on the 1923 page (Sokolov cites this passage as Barrès 1923 p. 56, his note 33). **label**: uncertain (as C3's source skeptic corrected it: read in a modern re-edition not checked against the 1923 page; the researcher gave "fact (what this text says)").
- **grade, as the source skeptic corrects it**: uncertain until the 1923 Plon page is seen (B for the memoir once checked); the reading's label 'uncertain'
- **settles**: In Barrès: I Corps half a league behind the Guard on the night of 1-2 December; after the signal it advanced past both sides of the Emperor's mound, Bernadotte at its head; then the reserve (8 Guard battalions, 2 of the Italian royal guard, 10 of grenadiers) followed, crossed the plain and climbed onto the plateau, arriving after the Guard cavalry had routed the Russian Guard; then the Guard infantry wheeled right to support IV Corps toward the lakes; it 'had only marched'.
- **does not settle**: Hours; which I Corps division; the 1923 wording.

[...] Bernadotte's troops in no way helped Soult on the Pratzen plateau, and by 10.30 had deployed in battle order south of Blasowitz between Caffarelli's troops and Vandamme's left flank.

### C3.31 `sokolov2006.p53.guard-drouet`

- **source**: `sokolov2006`, t. II, p. 53 (IIIF $52); endnotes 40, 41 on p. 73 ($72); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1799-1805-.-ii$52/full/1100,/0/default.jpg, https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1799-1805-.-ii$72/full/1100,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): В тот момент, когда началась атака русской гвардии, на плато поблизости почти не было французских полков. Французская пешая гвардия была еще на подходе, и в распоряжении императора не было ничего, кроме гвардейской кавалерии. Маршал Бессьер рассказывает в своем рапорте: «Император отдал мне приказ двинуться вперед с кавалерией, чтобы поддержать 4-й полк линейной пехоты и 24-й легкой пехоты, сильно атакованные неприятелем». [...]⁴⁰ [...] Тот факт, что французская кавалерия была опрокинута в начале боя между гвардиями, подтверждается воспоминаниями генерала Друэ: «Император... вызвал мою дивизию, чтобы поддержать центр. Чтобы скорее прийти на угрожаемый пункт, я пошел через болотистую местность и построил прямо на ходу мою дивизию в колонны пополубатальонно. Я атаковал русских. Их кавалерия устремилась вперед с большой решительностью и проскочила в интервалы между моей пехотой, чтобы атаковать гвардейских конных егерей, которые приводили себя в порядок после атаки позади моей дивизии»⁴¹. — Notes (p. 73): ⁴⁰ Цит. по: Revue d'histoire, 1907, № 80, p. 377. ⁴¹ Drouet d'Erlon J.-B. Vie militaire ecrite par lui-meme. Paris, 1844, p. 27.
- **the source skeptic's correction to the quotation**: the note mark after the Drouet quotation is printed '^' (a garbled superscript); that it is 41 is inferred from the sequence 40 ... 42 and the endnote list (41 = Drouet d'Erlon, Vie militaire, Paris 1844, p. 27)
- **translation**: At the moment the Russian Guard's attack began there were almost no French regiments nearby on the plateau. The French foot Guard was still coming up, and the Emperor had nothing at hand but the Guard cavalry. Marshal Bessières says in his report: 'The Emperor ordered me to advance with the cavalry to support the 4th of the line and the 24th light, heavily attacked by the enemy.' [...] That the French cavalry was overthrown at the start of the fight between the Guards is confirmed by General Drouet's memoirs: 'The Emperor ... called up my division to support the centre. To reach the threatened point sooner I crossed marshy ground and formed my division on the march in columns by half-battalions. I attacked the Russians. Their cavalry rushed forward with great resolution and passed through the intervals of my infantry to attack the Guard's chasseurs à cheval, who were re-forming behind my division after their charge.'
- **grade**: C as read (Sokolov's Russian translation of Drouet d'Erlon's Vie militaire, 1844, p. 27, and of Bessières's report as printed in the Revue d'histoire, 1907, no. 80, p. 377; neither original read: the French words are not known from this reading). **label**: fact (what Sokolov prints).
- **settles**: Drouet's memoir, as Sokolov renders it: his division called up by the Emperor 'to support the centre', crossing marshy ground, attacking the Russians, the Russian cavalry passing through his intervals to reach the Guard chasseurs re-forming behind his division; Sokolov: the foot Guard still coming up when the Russian Guard attacked; Bessières (as rendered) sent to support the 4e de ligne and 24e léger.
- **does not settle**: Drouet's own French; the hour; whether Drouet's 'centre' is the plateau or Blasowitz.

### C3.33 `lejeune1851.p89-90.bernadotte-guard`

- **source**: `lejeune1851`, pp. 89-90 (leaves n148-n149); images: https://archive.org/download/souvenirsdunoff00jeungoog/page/n148_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/souvenirsdunoff00jeungoog/page/n149_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Le maréchal Bernadotte occupait le centre, en face d'Austerlitz, sur les hauteurs de Kreznowitz. L'ennemi fit ici de très grands efforts et le combat s'y prolongea longtemps. Le corps de Bernadotte souffrait beaucoup et commençait à perdre du terrain, lorsque l'Empereur y envoya sa garde et vint en personne pour le soutenir. Il pouvait alors être une à deux heures. L'arrivée de la garde impériale permit à Bernadotte de reprendre l'offensive et sa première position. Les chevaliers gardes, corps d'élite de l'armée russe, chargèrent alors pour empêcher ce mouvement; mais le colonel Morland, à la tête de l'élite de notre armée, les chasseurs de la garde et les Mamelucks, depuis peu revenus d'Egypte, s'élancèrent sur les Russes, les renversèrent, ainsi que leur artillerie, dans le profond ravin du ruisseau de Kreznowitz [...]. Je m'y trouvais engagé aussi [...]. (p. 90) Ce brillant combat avait lieu sur le centre, tandis que notre droite était occupée à de plus rudes épreuves.
- **translation**: Marshal Bernadotte held the centre, facing Austerlitz, on the heights of Kreznowitz. The enemy made very great efforts here and the fight went on a long time. Bernadotte's corps suffered much and was beginning to lose ground when the Emperor sent his guard there and came in person to support it. It might then have been one or two o'clock. The arrival of the Imperial Guard allowed Bernadotte to take the offensive again and recover his first position. The Chevalier Guards, the Russian army's elite corps, then charged to prevent this movement; but Colonel Morland at the head of our army's elite, the Guard chasseurs and the Mamelukes, threw themselves on the Russians and overthrew them and their artillery into the deep ravine of the Kreznowitz brook [...]. I was engaged there too [...]. This brilliant fight took place in the centre, while our right was undergoing harder trials.
- **grade**: B (participant memoir, 1851; its topography is confused: inference). **label**: fact (what Lejeune says); disputed (Thiébault p. 466; the Materialien's note l).
- **settles**: In Lejeune I Corps was engaged at length in the centre and losing ground when the Emperor sent his Guard; 'il pouvait alors être une à deux heures'; the Guard's chasseurs and Mamelukes then charged the Chevalier Guard.
- **does not settle**: Which I Corps division; whether the Guard infantry engaged (the charge he describes is cavalry).
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.drouet.track[6]; FORMATIONS.c_gd.role; PHASES[6].lede; ANALYSIS[#guard].text.

Topography confused (agreed): B for what Lejeune says, weak for place. Not read by a person.

### C3.40 `materialien1806.p93.note-l`

Die aus 20 Bataillons Grenadieren bestehende Reserve, war auf den Höhen hinter den Divisionen St. Hilaire und Vandamme angekommen. ### C3.c1 `marbot1891.p260.centre-ravine`

- **quote** (read on: page image): Pendant que notre gauche remportait cet éclatant succès, le centre, formé par les troupes des maréchaux Soult et Bernadotte, placé par l'Empereur au fond du ravin de Goldbach où il était caché par un épais brouillard, s'élançait vers le coteau sur lequel est situé le village de Pratzen. Ce fut à ce moment que parut dans tout son éclat ce brillant soleil d'Austerlitz, dont Napoléon se plaisait tant à rappeler le souvenir.
- **translation**: While our left was winning this brilliant success, the centre, formed by the troops of Marshals Soult and Bernadotte, placed by the Emperor at the bottom of the Goldbach ravine where it was hidden by a thick fog, was dashing toward the slope on which the village of Pratzen stands. It was at that moment that the brilliant sun of Austerlitz, which Napoleon so liked to recall, appeared in all its splendour.

### C3.c2 `marbot1891.p262.guard-descends`

- **source**: `marbot1891`, t. I (27th ed.), p. 262 (leaf n281; header '262' read); images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugn01marbuoft/page/n281_w1200.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Pendant que les maréchaux Lannes, Soult, Murat, et la garde impériale, battaient le centre et la droite des Austro-Russes et les rejetaient au delà du village d'Austerlitz, la gauche des ennemis [...] se jeta sur le village de Telnitz [...]. L'Empereur, que nous avons laissé sur le plateau de Pratzen, débarrassé de la droite et du centre ennemis qui fuyaient derrière Austerlitz, l'Empereur, descendant alors des hauteurs de Pratzen avec les corps de Soult et toute sa garde, infanterie, cavalerie et artillerie, se précipite vers Telnitz, où il prend à dos les colonnes ennemies, que le maréchal Davout attaque de front.
- **translation**: While Marshals Lannes, Soult, Murat and the Imperial Guard were beating the centre and right of the Austro-Russians and driving them back beyond the village of Austerlitz, the enemy's left [...] threw itself on the village of Telnitz [...]. The Emperor, whom we left on the Pratzen plateau, rid of the enemy right and centre which were fleeing behind Austerlitz, the Emperor, then coming down from the heights of Pratzen with Soult's corps and all his guard, infantry, cavalry and artillery, rushes toward Telnitz, where he takes the enemy columns in the rear while Marshal Davout attacks them in front.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: In Marbot the Guard's infantry, cavalry and artillery came down from the Pratzen with Soult's corps toward Telnitz in the afternoon; Bernadotte is not named among those who beat the Allied centre and right.
- **does not settle**: Whether the Guard infantry fought (Marbot's 'battaient' is collective); any hour; Bernadotte's part (an omission is not a statement).
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.guard_inf.track[7]; FORMATIONS.c_gd.role; PHASES[7].lede; COMMAND.fr[7][1]; against segur1873.p470-472.guard-bernadotte (Bernadotte and the Guard on foot left on the plateau); FORMATIONS.c_i.role.
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

### C3.c3 `mikhailovsky1846.p255.rivaud-pratzen`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1846`, p. 255 (leaf n281 of relationdelacam00dangoog; the header is blurred, the page by the register's offset +26; the passage matches step-2 §5.6); second reading of step-2 §5.6; images: https://archive.org/download/relationdelacam00dangoog/page/n281_w1200.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Il ordonna aussitôt à une partie de la division Riveau, du corps de Bernadotte, envoyée à l'avance à Pratzen, et à deux divisions du corps de Lannes, celle de Cafarelli et la division de cavalerie de Kellermann, de marcher sur Blasowitz et d'occuper l'espace qui avait été réservé au prince Lichtenstein.
- **translation**: He at once ordered part of Rivaud's division of Bernadotte's corps, sent ahead to Pratzen, and two divisions of Lannes's corps, Caffarelli's and Kellermann's cavalry division, to march on Blasowitz and occupy the space that had been reserved for Prince Liechtenstein.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: In Mikhailovsky-Danilevsky part of Rivaud's division had been sent ahead to Pratzen before Napoleon turned it, with Caffarelli and Kellermann, toward Blasowitz.
- **does not settle**: Any hour; how much of the division; whether it went with or behind Soult's divisions; M-D calls Kellermann's cavalry a division 'du corps de Lannes'.

### C3.c4 `coignet1909.p122.second-line`

- **source**: `coignet1909`, p. 122 (leaf n153; header '122' read); images: https://archive.org/download/lescahiersducapi00coig/page/n153_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Lorsque les colonnes furent passées, l'Empereur nous fit suivre le mouvement. Nous étions vingt-cinq mille bonnets à poils, et des gaillards. Nos bataillons montèrent cette côte l'arme au bras et, arrivés à distance, ils souhaitèrent le bonjour à la première ligne par des feux de bataillon, puis la baïonnette croisée sur la première ligne des Russes, en battant la charge. [...] Du premier choc nos soldats enfoncèrent la première ligne, et nous, derrière les soldats, la seconde ligne. On perça le centre de leur armée et nous fûmes maîtres du plateau de Pratzen, mais notre aile droite souffrit beaucoup. [...] Mais on nous fit appuyer fortement à droite.
- **translation**: When the columns had crossed, the Emperor had us follow the movement. We were twenty-five thousand bearskins, and fine fellows. Our battalions climbed that slope with arms at the support and, once within range, greeted the first line with battalion volleys, then bayonets crossed onto the Russians' first line, beating the charge. [...] At the first shock our soldiers broke the first line, and we, behind the soldiers, the second line. The centre of their army was pierced and we were masters of the Pratzen plateau, but our right wing suffered much. [...] But we were made to bear hard to the right.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: Coignet, a grenadier of the foot Guard, has the Guard follow as soon as the columns had crossed and claims that 'we' (the Guard), behind the line's soldiers, broke the Russian second line on the Pratzen.
- **does not settle**: Whether 'nous' is the Guard in action or the army at large (the text is loose: 25,000 bearskins); any hour; it contradicts his own p. 123 ('nous croyions notre tour arrivé, mais ils battirent en retraite').
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.c_gd.role; FORMATIONS.guard_inf.track[6]; EVENTS[#guard-broken].forms; against schoenhals1873.p169.guard-infantry-not-engaged, corr11_1863.p451.guard-centre-reserve, barres_etoile.p75-78.reserve-marched.
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

### C3.c5 `segur1873.p467.nine-oclock`

- **source**: `segur1877`, t. II, p. 467 (leaf n488; header '467' read); images: https://archive.org/download/histoireetmmoir06sggoog/page/n488_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Le plateau, attaqué de front, et à revers de gauche à droite, fut escaladé au pas de course. [...] A neuf heures, et de notre côté, la bataille, péniblement sur la défensive en arrière à droite, déjà victorieuse en avant au centre, et menaçante à notre gauche, était engagée sur tout notre front. Vers onze heures tout avait réussi selon les prévisions de l'Empereur.
- **translation**: The plateau, attacked in front, and in reverse from left to right, was climbed at the double. [...] At nine o'clock, on our side, the battle, painfully on the defensive behind on the right, already victorious forward in the centre, and threatening on our left, was engaged along our whole front. Towards eleven everything had succeeded as the Emperor had foreseen.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: In Ségur the plateau was attacked in front and 'à revers de gauche à droite' and the centre was already victorious at nine; by about eleven all had succeeded.
- **does not settle**: Who attacked 'à revers de gauche à droite' (Bernadotte's oblique approach of p. 464 is the obvious reading: inference); Ségur's own later 'Vers midi' climb of Bernadotte (p. 467) is a separate scene.

- **translation**: In the distance one saw the remains of the Russian reserves leaving the central plateau to us, and the left of their army withdrawing in close ranks toward Austerlitz. ### C3.c7 `sokolov2006.p49.blasowitz-caffarelli`

- **source**: `sokolov2006`, t. II, p. 49 (IIIF $48; folio '49' read); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1799-1805-.-ii$48/full/1100,/0/default.jpg.

На поддержку ему были направлены два батальона 51-го линейного. 13-й легкий* под командованием полковника Кастекса устремился в атаку. Полковник, который сражался в первых рядах, был убит, но деревня была взята.
- **translation**: But at that moment Marshal Lannes, seeing Bernadotte's units approaching, went over to the attack. The 13th light regiment of Caffarelli's division was sent to retake Blasowitz. Two battalions of the 51st of the line were sent to support it. The 13th light, under Colonel Castex, rushed to the attack. The colonel, who fought in the front ranks, was killed, but the village was taken.

### C3.c8 `sokolov2006.p49.barres-mound`

- **source**: `sokolov2006`, t. II, p. 49 (IIIF $48), the quotation of Barrès with note 33 (Barrès 1923 p. 56); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1799-1805-.-ii$48/full/1100,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Велит гвардии Баррес вспоминал: «Первый корпус, который стоял позади нас, двинулся вперед, обойдя справа и слева холм (Зг/ран). Солдаты и офицеры кричали «Да здравствует Император!», подняв свои шляпы на шпагах, саблях и штыках.
- **translation**: Barrès, a vélite of the Guard, recalled: 'The First Corps, which stood behind us, moved forward, passing right and left of the mound (Zg/ran). Soldiers and officers shouted Long live the Emperor!, raising their hats on swords, sabres and bayonets.'
- **grade**: C. **label**: inference.
- **settles**: Sokolov glosses Barrès's mound in italics, printed '(Зг/ран)': apparently a garbled 'Žuráň' (inference; the print is damaged). If so, a modern specialist identifies the Emperor's mound, behind which I Corps stood, with the Žuráň.
- **does not settle**: The identification itself (Sokolov's gloss, not Barrès's word; the printed form is garbled); Barrès's French words (a Russian translation).

### C3.c9 `schoenhals1873.p164.girzikowitz-infantry`

- **source**: `schoenhals1873`, p. 164 (leaf n168; header '164' read); images: https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n168_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Während eine mörderische Kanonade Tod durch die Reihen der beiderseitigen Heere verbreitete, drangen die Franzosen immer ungestümer gegen die Höhen zwischen Blaziowitz und Pratze vor. Fürst Liechtenstein musste sich zur Deckung dieses Terrain's noch mehr links ziehen. Er liess die Regimenter Nassau und Lothringen-Cürassiere unter dem General Chramelly die feindliche Infanterie, die aus Girzikowitz hervorbrach und sich in den Weingärten zwischen diesem Orte und Pratze ausbreitete, mit Erfolg angreifen, wodurch sie in ihrem Vordringen eine Zeit lang aufgehalten ward. Unterdessen aber hatte sich der Kampf auf den Höhen von Pratze entschieden, und das heftige Feuer, das der Feind von dort her gegen die nun entblösste linke Flanke der Reiterei richtete, nebst dem Vordringen der Division Davoust gegen Krženowitz, zwang den Fürsten zum Rückzuge
- **translation**: While a murderous cannonade spread death through the ranks of both armies, the French pressed ever more impetuously toward the heights between Blasowitz and Pratze. Prince Liechtenstein had to draw further left to cover this ground. He had the Nassau and Lothringen cuirassier regiments under General Chramelly attack, with success, the enemy infantry which broke out of Girzikowitz and spread through the vineyards between that place and Pratze, so that it was held up in its advance for a while. Meanwhile, however, the fight on the heights of Pratze had been decided, and the heavy fire the enemy directed from there against the cavalry's now exposed left flank, together with the advance of the division Davoust toward Krženowitz, forced the prince to retreat
- **grade**: B. **label**: uncertain.
- **settles**: In Schönhals French infantry debouched from Girzikowitz into the vineyards toward Pratze and was held up for a time by Chramelly's cuirassiers, before the Guard fight on p. 164.
- **does not settle**: Whose infantry (I Corps by place: inference); any hour; 'der Division Davoust gegen Krženowitz' names Davout's division where Davout fought far to the south: a slip of the text or another unit, not interpreted here.
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.drouet.track[3]; FORMATIONS.rivaud.track[3]; FORMATIONS.drouet.track[6]; KNOW_OVERRIDE.al.drouet; KNOW_OVERRIDE.al.rivaud.
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

**The source skeptic's summary** (C3; its counts and "CRn" numbers are its working list's: the verdicts above are the record): Skeptic for C3, two attempts. The first attempt judged all 40 readings on fresh downloads of their page images (its verdicts kept verbatim in substance; C3-challenge.partial.md); the second attempt added counter-evidence and the proposal reviews, re-reading only the pages it quotes (Marbot pp. 260, 262; M-D 1846 p. 255; and crops already made of Coignet p. 122, Ségur pp. 467, 471-472, 206-207 (Savary), Sokolov p. 49, Schönhals p. 164). Verdicts: 40 readings, none rejected; 15 accepted with corrections. The corrections that matter for the synthesis: (1) Coignet p. 122 claims the Guard broke the Russian second line: the researcher's 'Guard infantry not engaged, every reading agrees' (proposals on c_gd.role and guard_inf@6, claim 'fact') must become 'disputed, the weight against engagement'. (2) The Guard's artillery was in action in Ségur, Thiébault, Marbot, Coignet and Barrès. (3) Thiébault p. 504's non-use of the 2nd division, grenadiers and Guard is his conjecture of Napoleon's boast, not an observation. (4) M-D 1844 p. 172's 'military writers' are credited with a judgement, not the night move; the night move is M-D's own statement. (5) Schönhals's 40 guns go with the grenadiers by grammar. (6) Barrès is read only in a modern re-edition: uncertain until the 1923 page is seen. (7) Ségur p. 470's 'Guard on foot at a fair distance' is the scene when Rapp returned, not during the charge. (8) The Materialien's lettered notes' attribution to the French officer rests on the heading plus note a) (not read on the image). Counter-evidence found: Marbot p. 260 (Bernadotte's troops with Soult's in the ravine, advancing at sunrise: against Sokolov's 10:00 and Ségur's noon); Marbot p. 262 (the Guard infantry with the wheel toward Telnitz; Bernadotte unnamed among the victors); M-D 1846 p. 255 (part of Rivaud's division 'envoyée à l'avance à Pratzen': against the researcher's 'no source has Rivaud follow Soult'); Sokolov p. 49 (Blasowitz retaken by Caffarelli's 13e léger, against Stutterheim's Bernadotte) and his gloss of Barrès's mound, printed '(Зг/ран)'; Ségur p. 467 (centre victorious at nine; plateau attacked 'à revers de gauche à droite'); Schönhals p. 164 (French infantry out of Girzikowitz into the vineyards, held by Chramelly's cuirassiers; the same page's 'Division Davoust gegen Krženowitz' is an oddity, not interpreted). Drouet's memoir as Sokolov quotes it (C) has the Emperor call his division up at the crisis: it bears against an 08:45 crossing for Drouet in particular and partly supports the current role's 'feeds Drouet onto the plateau at the crisis'. Code checks the researcher's effects missed: tools/stage2/chronology.js:490 accepts tm.basis only as 'source' or 'app narrative, unsourced' (the proposals to write readings into basis for drouet@3, rivaud@3, guard_inf@7, c_gren@7 would fail check:chronology; readings go in tm.note, which EXCLUDED_PATHS leaves to --times). Changing an act that a tm.ev quotes (rivaud@3 'Follows Soult onto the plateau') or that a REVIEW row cites (chronology.js ll. 231-238: rivaud@3, drouet@6, guard_inf@6) requires moving those cites. New texts naming Bernadotte, the French Guard or the grenadiers with an hour become timed statements (NAMES, ll. 70-90) needing REVIEW_CITES or ALLOW_TIMED rows. A split of PLANS.fr.cols[4] meets runtime-test.js:855 (four axis columns via routeAxis0, the column count) and redteam.js §5 (obj within 1.5 km of a feature). Searched, not found: Drouet d'Erlon, Vie militaire (1844), on archive.org (advancedsearch 'title:erlon', 'creator:erlon', 'title:(drouet)', 'drouet erlon'): no scan. Alombert and Colin t. IV (the first attempt's OCR download) holds no account of 2 December (three passing mentions of Austerlitz). Not done: the 1923 Barrès page (Gallica, unreachable); Materialien note a) on its image; the title leaves of stutterheim1806fr and lejeune1851.

**The researcher's notes** (C3): Cluster C3, second attempt (no first-attempt notes existed). Every quote returned was read on its page image (crops in work/C3-research2/crops); OCR was used only to find pages. Second readings of step-2 §1.2-§1.5, §2.3/§2.5/§2.7's pages, §4.7, §4.8, §4.10 were made on the images; §5.1, §5.4 were read in the 1844 Russian original. Main findings. (1) No source read calls I Corps the general reserve: the reserve is the Guard and Oudinot's grenadiers (Bulletin p. 450; Stutterheim p. 42; Schönhals p. 158; Barrès; M-D; Ségur). Question 157 can be settled for the role: 'the centre', with the dawn position and the commitment kept disputed. (2) The dispositions no. 9535 do not name the Guard at all and put the grenadiers under Lannes. (3) Whether an I Corps division fought the Russian Guard is disputed but leans 'yes, one division': Stutterheim (A), the Bulletin's three regiments (A), Savary, Ségur, Lejeune (B), Drouet's own memoir via Sokolov (C as read) against Thiébault and the Materialien's French officer (B). (4) The Guard infantry was not engaged in every reading that speaks of it; it moved up after the cavalry's charge. (5) The grenadiers were not engaged (c_gren@8 'attacking' is unsupported). Contradictions inside the map noticed: COMMAND.fr[4][1] is tagged DOCUMENTED but no order is read; c_gd.strengthNote's '24 guns' matches Thiébault's ice battery, not a gun count; rivaud@3 'Follows Soult' has no source. Hours for C1 (the Guard fight's hour, not C3's own): three French memoirs put the Russian Guard's attack about one o'clock: Rapp p. 60 'Il était déjà une heure', Ségur p. 468 'Il était une heure', Lejeune p. 89 'une à deux heures' (the Guard sent to Bernadotte); the Bulletin p. 451 'A une heure après midi la victoire était décidée'. The map's phase 6 is 11:15-12:45 with guard-attack t 660-780 and guard-broken 675-795. These readings were made for C3's items and are offered to C1's synthesizer, not proposed here as a retiming. Translation discrepancy: Stutterheim's French original (archive.org labatailledaust00stutgoog, reachable; step 2 had only Gallica) says the Bernadotte column appeared 'sur la droite de Pratzen' (p. 87); the 1806 German says 'links von Pratzen' (p. 62). The French is the original. The Materialien's lettered notes are headed 'Mit Anmerkungen eines französischen Officiers' (n10): step-2 §3 calls the additions the editor's; the lettered notes have this stated origin (the numbered Zusätze are the editor's). This bears on other clusters that cite Materialien notes. Barrès was read only in a modern electronic re-edition on archive.org (not the 1923 printing): its quotes are flagged; a skeptic should treat them as uncertain until a 1923 page is seen. Sokolov's quotations of Drouet d'Erlon (Vie militaire, 1844, p. 27) and of Bessières's report (Revue d'histoire 1907 no. 80 p. 377) are Russian translations: the French originals were not found (leads: the Ministry of Defence's digital library record, unreachable today; the Revue d'histoire 1907 volumes). Leads not followed: Czartoryski, Ermolov (Russian side; not C3's items); Thiers (grade C); Kutuzov's report (Materialien Zusatz 11; Kutuzov 1951 docs) for what the Allies saw of I Corps; the second French scan bub_gb_bLCe2qyA_D8C; Sokolov's 'Relation de la bataille d'Austerlitz gagnée le 2 décembre 1805' (his note on p. 72, an official French relation? not identified). New source keys used: barres_etoile (the re-edition; barres1923 stays the unread printing), lejeune1851 (as C2 used it). Registered keys used: corr11_1863, rapp1823, savary1828, segur1873, coignet1909, stutterheim1806de, stutterheim1806fr (read on archive.org), mikhailovsky1844, mikhailovsky1846, thiebault3_1894, schoenhals1873, sokolov2006, materialien1806.

## C4: the Zuran and the plan (question 160), the chapel battery (question 154), the ice and the sun of Austerlitz

### C4.1 `corr11.p441.proclamation`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9533 'A l'armée', 'Au bivouac, 10 frimaire an XIV (1er décembre 1805)', pp. 440-441; leaves n449 (p. 440, foot) and n450 (p. 441, head); images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n449_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n450_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Soldats, l'armée russe se présente devant vous pour venger l'armée / autrichienne d'Ulm. [...] Les positions que nous occupons sont formidables; et, pendant qu'ils marcheront pour tourner ma droite, ils me présenteront le flanc. [...] Moniteur du 25 frimaire an XIV. (En minute au Dépôt de la guerre.)
- **translation**: Soldiers, the Russian army comes before you to avenge the Austrian army of Ulm. [...] The positions we occupy are formidable; and while they march to turn my right, they will present their flank to me.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The proclamation of 1 December states in terms that the enemy will march to turn his right and so present its flank to him.
- **does not settle**: It does not say that the right was weakened on purpose, that he wished to invite the attack, or that the enemy would leave the plateau; it names no height and no hour.
- **bears on**: ANALYSIS[#deception].text; COMMAND.fr[0][4]; PLANS.fr.author.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 2 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept. Read fresh on n449 (p. 440 foot) and n450 (p. 441 head), with 'Moniteur du 25 frimaire an XIV. (En minute au Dépôt de la guerre.)'. The settles/does_not_settle are exactly as narrow as the words. Not read by a person.

### C4.2 `corr11.p440.grenadiers`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9532, to Talleyrand, heading 'Au bivouac à deux lieues en avant de Brünn, 9 frimaire an XIV (30 novembre 1805), 4 heures du soir' (p. 439, heading read in OCR only); the sentence on p. 440, n449; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n449_w1600.jpg.
- **locator, as the source skeptic gives it**: no. 9532, heading on p. 439 (n448) now read on the image: 'Au bivouac à deux lieues en avant de Brünn, 9 frimaire an XIV (30 novembre 1805), 4 heures du soir'; the sentence on p. 440 (n449)
- **quote** (read on: page image): Mandez à Paris que, bivouaquant depuis quatre jours au milieu de mes grenadiers, je n'écris que sur mes genoux, et ne puis dès lors guère écrire.
- **translation**: Send word to Paris that, bivouacking these four days among my grenadiers, I write only on my knees and so can hardly write.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Napoleon's own letter of 30 November: his bivouac had been among his grenadiers for four days.
- **does not settle**: Which height; the night of 1-2 December.
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.gqg.track[0]; FEATURES[#zuran].facts.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 2 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept. The heading the researcher had only in OCR is confirmed on the image. The heading's 'à deux lieues en avant de Brünn' is itself evidence on where the bivouac stood on 30 Nov (see counter reading corr11.p439.deux-lieues). Not read by a person.

### C4.3 `corr11.p448-449.bivouac`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9541, 30th Bulletin, 'Austerlitz, 12 frimaire an XIV (3 décembre 1805)', pp. 448-449; n457, n458; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n457_w1600.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n458_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Le 10, l'Empereur, du haut de son bivouac, aperçut, avec une indicible joie, l'armée russe commençant, à deux portées de canon de ses avant-postes, un mouvement de flanc pour tourner sa droite. [...] On fit tout pour confirmer l'ennemi dans cette idée. Le prince Murat fit avancer un petit corps de cavalerie dans la plaine; mais tout d'un coup il parut étonné des forces immenses de l'ennemi, et rentra à la hâte. [...] L'Empereur fit mettre à l'ordre la proclamation ci-jointe¹. Le soir, il voulut visiter à pied et incognito tous les bivouacs; mais à peine eut-il fait quelques pas qu'il fut reconnu. [...] Des fanaux de paille furent mis en un instant au haut de milliers de perches, et 80,000 hommes se présentèrent au-devant de l'Empereur [...] / (p. 449) L'Empereur dit, en entrant dans son bivouac, qui consistait en une mauvaise cabane de paille sans toit que lui avaient faite les grenadiers : « Voilà la plus belle soirée de ma vie [...] ». Footnote 1: 'Pièce nº 9533.'
- **translation**: On the 10th the Emperor, from the top of his bivouac, saw with inexpressible joy the Russian army beginning, two cannon-shots from his outposts, a flank march to turn his right. [...] Everything was done to confirm the enemy in that idea. Prince Murat sent a small body of cavalry forward into the plain, but suddenly appeared astonished at the enemy's immense forces and came back in haste. [...] The Emperor had the attached proclamation put in orders. In the evening he wished to visit all the bivouacs on foot and incognito, but had hardly gone a few steps when he was recognised. [...] Straw torches were set up in an instant on thousands of poles, and 80,000 men came before the Emperor [...]. The Emperor said, going into his bivouac, which was a poor roofless straw hut the grenadiers had made him: 'This is the finest evening of my life [...]'.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: On 1 December his bivouac stood on a height ('du haut de son bivouac') from which the flank march was seen; the Bulletin says 'everything was done to confirm the enemy in that idea'; the evening walk, the straw torches, the roofless straw hut made by the grenadiers.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: 'On fit tout pour confirmer l'ennemi dans cette idée' is used (here and in proposal 2b) as if 'cette idée' were the flank march to turn the right. On the page it follows 'Il n'avait qu'une crainte, c'était que l'armée française ne lui échappât': the idea is the enemy's fear that the French army would escape him (it 'paraissait ne pas oser sortir de sa position'); Murat's feigned retreat illustrates it. The sentence on the enemy's operation is the next one: 'Ainsi tout tendait à confirmer le général russe dans l'opération mal calculée qu'il avait arrêtée.'
- **does not settle**: Which height (no name); that it is the Zuran is an inference.
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.gqg.track[0]; FEATURES[#zuran]; ANALYSIS[#deception].text; COMMAND.fr[0][3].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 2 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept with corrections. Quote word for word on n457/n458. Correct 'settles' to: the Bulletin says everything was done to confirm the enemy in the belief that the French would escape him, and that 'tout tendait à confirmer le général russe dans l'opération mal calculée qu'il avait arrêtée'. Also on p. 449: 'L'Empereur fit sur-le-champ toutes ses dispositions de bataille' (after the evening of the 1st), which supports proposal 2a. Not read by a person.

### C4.4 `corr11.p450.reserve-one-oclock`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9541, 30th Bulletin, p. 450; n459 (the sunrise part re-reads register §1.4); images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n459_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): L'Empereur, avec son fidèle compagnon de guerre le maréchal Berthier, son premier aide de camp le colonel général Junot, et tout son état-major, se trouvait en réserve avec les dix bataillons de sa Garde et les dix bataillons de grenadiers du général Oudinot, dont le général Duroc commandait une partie. [...] A une heure du matin, l'Empereur monta à cheval pour parcourir ses postes, reconnaître les feux des bivouacs de l'ennemi, et se faire rendre compte par les grand'gardes de ce qu'elles avaient pu entendre des mouvements des Russes. [...] Le 11 frimaire, le jour parut enfin. Le soleil se leva radieux [...]. L'Empereur, entouré de tous les maréchaux, attendait pour donner ses derniers ordres que l'horizon fût bien éclairci. Aux premiers rayons du soleil les ordres furent donnés, et chaque maréchal rejoignit son corps au grand galop.
- **translation**: The Emperor, with his faithful companion in war Marshal Berthier, his first aide-de-camp Colonel-General Junot and all his staff, was in reserve with the ten battalions of his Guard and General Oudinot's ten battalions of grenadiers [...]. At one in the morning the Emperor mounted to ride round his posts, see the fires of the enemy's bivouacs and have the grand-guards report what they had heard of the Russians' movements. [...] On 11 frimaire the day broke at last. The sun rose radiant [...]. The Emperor, surrounded by all the marshals, waited until the horizon had cleared to give his last orders. At the first rays of the sun the orders were given, and each marshal rejoined his corps at full gallop.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: A clock hour, 01:00, for Napoleon mounting to ride round his posts and see the enemy's fires; Berthier and the staff with him 'en réserve' with the Guard and the grenadiers; all the marshals with him at daybreak; the last orders at the first rays of the sun.
- **does not settle**: The place where he waited at dawn (not named); when he came back from the round of the posts; the hour he took post on any mound; the clock hour of sunrise.
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.gqg.track[0]; PHASES[0].events[2]; COMMAND.fr[0][0]; PLANS.fr.author; PHASES[3].flash.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 1 freshly downloaded image: quotation exact, accept. Exact on n459. Caution for its use: 'se trouvait en réserve avec les dix bataillons de sa Garde...' stands in the Bulletin's description of the battle array (after the corps, before 'A une heure du matin'), not in a dawn scene; it is weak support for 'Napoleon with Berthier at dawn' (Thiébault p. 456 names Berthier among those gathered before eight, which is the better basis). Not read by a person.

### C4.5 `corr11.p441.butte-reread`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9534 'Ordres', 'Au bivouac en avant de Brünn, 10 frimaire an XIV (1er décembre 1805)', p. 441, n450 (second reading of register §1.1); images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n450_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Ordre aux grenadiers de se porter en avant de la butte, sur la droite de la route. Ordre à la division Suchet et à la division Caffarelli de se placer en avant à droite de la route, à la hauteur du Santon. Ordre au 17e régiment d'infanterie légère de prendre position au Santon. Ordre au quartier général de se transporter à la butte. Le maréchal Berthier, par ordre de l'Empereur. Dépôt de la guerre.
- **translation**: Order to the grenadiers to move in front of the mound, on the right of the road. Order to Suchet's and Caffarelli's divisions to place themselves forward, right of the road, level with the Santon. Order to the 17th light to take position at the Santon. Order to headquarters to move to the mound.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Register §1.1 confirmed as printed; the order relates 'la butte' to 'la route' (grenadiers in front of it, on the right of the road): the butte stands by the road.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: 'the butte stands by the road' is an inference: the order puts the grenadiers 'en avant de la butte, sur la droite de la route', which places the grenadiers, not the butte. Also the order moves headquarters to the butte on 1 December; it says nothing about 2 December.
- **does not settle**: That 'la butte' is the Zuran (inference, consistent with a mound by the road and with M-D's barrow by Schlapanitz); any hour on 2 December.
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.gqg.track[0]; FEATURES[#zuran]; PHASES[0].events[2].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 1 freshly downloaded image: quotation exact, accept with corrections. Register §1.1 confirmed on the image. Important for item 1: 'Ordre au quartier général de se transporter à la butte' is a move on 1 Dec, so the earlier headquarters bivouac (Thiébault's 'monticule derrière le village de Kritschen', set up on 29 Nov) and 'la butte' need not be the same place. Not read by a person.

### C4.7 `md1844.p177.kurgan-sun`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, p. 177, IIIF k188 (second reading of register §6.3, with the rest of the paragraph); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$188/full/1100,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Ноября 20-го, въ 8-мъ часу утра, первыя три колонны выступили съ ночлега: (*) [...] Наполеонъ стоялъ на курганѣ при Шлапаницѣ, окруженный своими маршалами, еще до зари къ нему созванными. Движенія наши были сперва скрыты отъ него туманомъ. Когда солнце озарило вершины горъ, Наполеонъ увидѣлъ, что занимаемыя нами высоты обнажались отъ войскъ и наши колонны приняли направленіе, совершенно согласное съ объявленнымъ имъ въ приказѣ по арміи. Очевидно убѣдясь въ томъ, отдалъ онъ маршаламъ окончательныя повелѣнія: 1), Бернадоту и Сульту, овладѣвъ Праценскими высотами, разрѣзать нашу армію на двѣ части, и начать атаку черезъ полчаса [...]
- **translation**: On 20 November, in the eighth hour of the morning, the first three columns set out from their night quarters [...]. Napoleon stood on the barrow by Schlapanitz, surrounded by his marshals, summoned to him even before dawn. Our movements were at first hidden from him by the fog. When the sun lit the tops of the hills, Napoleon saw that the heights we held were being stripped of troops and that our columns had taken a direction entirely in accord with the one he had announced in the order to the army. Plainly convinced of it, he gave his marshals the final orders: 1) to Bernadotte and Soult, having seized the Pratzen heights, to cut our army in two, and to begin the attack in half an hour [...]
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Napoleon on 'the barrow by Schlapanitz' with the marshals summoned before dawn; the sun on the hilltops before the final orders; the attack to start half an hour after them; M-D reads the 1 December proclamation as having announced the Allied direction.
- **does not settle**: That the barrow is the Zuran (inference); a clock hour for his taking post, for the sun on the hilltops or for the orders; M-D's own source for the French side here.
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.gqg.track[0]; FEATURES[#zuran]; PLANS.fr.author; PHASES[3].flash; COMMAND.fr[0][4].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 1 freshly downloaded image: quotation exact, accept. Exact on k188 (p. 177). M-D attributes the final orders to Bernadotte and Soult as 'having seized the Pratzen heights, to cut our army in two' (bears on proposal 2b: the attack on the Pratzen is attested in M-D, not only the map's reading). Same page: 'Бивачный дымъ и туманъ, покрывая долины, не позволяли намъ видѣть войскъ, собранныхъ Наполеономъ' (5b). Not read by a person.

### C4.8 `md1844.p203-204.guard-artillery`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, pp. 203-204, IIIF k215 (foot) and k216 (head); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$215/full/1100,/0/default.jpg, https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$216/full/1100,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Графъ Буксгевденъ и Графъ Ланжеронъ спѣшили достигнуть Аугеста [...]. Собравъ сіи войска, Дохтуровъ поворотилъ назадъ, поражаемый / гвардейскою артиллеріею Наполеона, лично имъ расположенною на Аугестскихъ высотахъ. [...] черезъ мостъ на Литавѣ, между Аугестомъ и Сачанскимъ озеромъ, и плотиною, между двумя озерами, на селеніе Сачанъ. Отъ тяжести орудій провалился мостъ. Наши кинулись на замерзшее озеро, но ледъ не вездѣ держался. [...] И здѣсь ледъ началъ проваливаться; озеро поглощало людей, лошадей и пушки, а между тѣмъ Наполеонъ продолжалъ губительный пушечный огонь.
- **translation**: Count Buxhowden and Count Langeron hurried to reach Augezd [...]. Gathering these troops, Dokhturov turned back, struck by Napoleon's Guard artillery, placed by him in person on the Augezd heights. [...] over the bridge on the Littawa between Augezd and the Satschan lake, and by the dam between the two lakes, to Satschan village. Under the weight of the guns the bridge gave way. Ours threw themselves onto the frozen lake, but the ice did not hold everywhere. [...] And here the ice began to give way; the lake swallowed men, horses and guns, while Napoleon kept up a murderous cannon fire.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: In the Russian official history the Guard artillery that fired on the retreat stood on 'the Augezd heights', placed by Napoleon in person; the ice gave way under men, horses and guns.
- **does not settle**: The chapel (not named); a gun count; IV Corps guns; a drowned number.

Not read by a person.

### C4.9 `stutfr2.p68-69.note7`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806fr2`, pp. 68-69, note (7) of the French officer; n71, n72; images: https://archive.org/download/bub_gb_bLCe2qyA_D8C/page/n6.jpg, https://archive.org/download/bub_gb_bLCe2qyA_D8C/page/n71.jpg, https://archive.org/download/bub_gb_bLCe2qyA_D8C/page/n72.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (7) Le Maréchal Bernadotte n'a été vu que le jour de la bataille. [...] C'est ainsi que tous les jours les divisions ont repassé les défilés, à mesure que les mouvemens de l'ennemi s'éclaircissoient, et que les fautes qu'il alloit commettre devenoient plus probables. Si d'ailleurs, au lieu de tourner la droite à / l'armée française, ce que le général français désiroit le plus, les Russes eussent engagé une affaire de montagne, en tournant la gauche de l'armée française, le bivouac du quartier-général derrière Kritchen que les Français ont toujours fait occuper, étoit la position d'où l'on pouvoit plus aisément, par un à gauche, se porter sur les mamelons de la gauche diagonalement en arrière du Santon.
- **translation**: (7) Marshal Bernadotte was seen only on the day of the battle. [...] So every day the divisions recrossed the defiles as the enemy's movements became clearer and the mistakes he was going to make more probable. If, moreover, instead of turning the right of the French army, which is what the French general most wished, the Russians had fought in the hills by turning the French left, the headquarters' bivouac behind Kritchen, which the French always kept occupied, was the position from which one could most easily, by a left turn, move onto the knolls on the left diagonally behind the Santon.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: An 1806 French note places the headquarters' bivouac 'derrière Kritchen' and says the French general most wished the Russians to turn his right.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: Used (with Thiébault p. 449) as a placing of the 2 December post. The note speaks of a bivouac 'que les Français ont toujours fait occuper' in the context of the days of manoeuvre before the battle ('tous les jours les divisions ont repassé les défilés'); it does not say Napoleon was there on the night of 1-2 Dec or the morning of the 2nd.
- **does not settle**: Who the officer was and whether he was present; where 'Kritchen' is (not identified here); that the bivouac is the Zuran.

The same note, omitted by the researcher, bears on the plan: p. 68 'il étoit résolu à se porter encore une journée en arrière, si l'ennemi manœuvroit sagement. Il ne vouloit point engager une bataille corps à corps contre une armée supérieure et qui seroit bien postée'; p. 69 'l'attaque qu'avoit projetée l'Empereur dépendoit spécialement de la promptitude de la marche du centre sur les hauteurs de Pratzen' (counter reading stutfr2.p68-69.centre-pratzen). ### C4.10 `stutfr2.p115-116.chapel-note11`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806fr2`, pp. 115-116, Stutterheim's text and note (11) of the French officer; n118, n119; images: https://archive.org/download/bub_gb_bLCe2qyA_D8C/page/n118.jpg, https://archive.org/download/bub_gb_bLCe2qyA_D8C/page/n119.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Alors déjà les Français, après leur succès sur le centre, avoient fait avancer leur réserve de vingt bataillons, et s'étoient étendus le long de la crête des hauteurs qu'avoient occupées les alliés le matin, depuis Pratzen jusqu'à la chapelle au dessus d'Aujest; mais l'ennemi n'étoit encore qu'en petit nombre; mais il étoit sans canon au-dessus de ce village(11). [...] (11) La première colonne ne pouvoit plus reprendre l'offensive. La réserve, composée de / vingt bataillons de grenadiers, étoit arrivée sur les hauteurs derrière les divisions Saint-Hilaire et Vandamme. (L'Empereur, déjà avec la cavalerie de la garde, descendoit à la chapelle Saint Antoine.) Cette colonne se trouvoit prise en queue par le Maréchal Davoust et le général Legrand.
- **translation**: By then the French, after their success in the centre, had brought up their reserve of twenty battalions and spread along the crest of the heights the Allies had held in the morning, from Pratzen to the chapel above Aujest; but the enemy was still few, and he had no guns above that village (11). Note (11): The first column could no longer resume the offensive. The reserve of twenty grenadier battalions had arrived on the heights behind the Saint-Hilaire and Vandamme divisions. (The Emperor, already with the Guard cavalry, was coming down to the chapel of Saint Anthony.) That column was taken in the rear by Marshal Davout and General Legrand.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The earliest placing of the Emperor at the chapel found: an 1806 French note has him coming down to the chapel of St Anthony with the Guard cavalry; Stutterheim's text names the chapel above Aujest and says the French at first had no guns above the village.
- **does not settle**: A battery at the chapel, its count or its corps; an hour.

### C4.11 `stut.p74-75.kapelle-teich`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, pp. 74-75, n77-n78; images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n77.jpg, https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n78.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Jetzt hatten die Franzosen, nach ihren Vortheilen im Centrum, schon ihre Reserve von 20 Bataillonen anrücken lassen, und hatten sich längst des Randes der Anhöhen, welche diesen Morgen von den Alliirten besetzt gewesen waren, von Pratzen bis zu der Kapelle ober Aujest ausgebreitet; doch war der Feind noch in geringer Anzahl, auch hatte er ober jenem Dorfe keine Kanonen. [...] Es war die Division Vandamme, welche an der äußersten Spitze rechts auf den Anhöhen bey Pratzen gestanden hatte, und welche nach und nach, so wie die Franzosen jene Anhöhe besetzten, sich auf die Anhöhe bey der Kapelle ober Aujest herüber gezogen hatte. [...] in und um Aujest wurden 4000 Mann gefangen, die auch ihre Kanonen verloren. Viele von den Flüchtigen warfen sich auf den Teich, der zwar gefroren war, aber nicht so stark, daß nicht einige darin umkamen. Der Feind, welcher unterdessen seine Artillerie erhalten hatte, verfolgte mit derselben sehr nachdrücklich die Flüchtigen, welche nachher durch Satschan gingen, und Abends zur Arrieregarde auf den Anhöhen bey Neuhof stießen.
- **translation**: By now the French had brought up their reserve of 20 battalions and spread along the edge of the heights the Allies had held that morning, from Pratzen to the chapel above Aujest; but the enemy was still few, and had no guns above that village. [...] It was Vandamme's division, which had stood at the far right on the heights by Pratzen and had gradually drawn over onto the height by the chapel above Aujest. [...] In and around Aujest 4,000 men were taken, who also lost their guns. Many fugitives threw themselves onto the pond, which was frozen, but not so hard that some did not perish in it. The enemy, who meanwhile had received his artillery, pursued the fugitives very vigorously with it; they then went through Satschan and in the evening joined the rearguard on the heights by Neuhof.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: An officer present, printed 1806: Vandamme on the height by the chapel above Augezd; at first no French guns above the village, then French artillery firing on the fugitives; the pond frozen but not hard enough, 'einige' (some) died in it.
- **does not settle**: Where the guns stood once they came; their number or corps; a drowned count beyond 'some'.

### C4.16 `schoen.p169-170.tellnitz-pfarrer`

- **source**: `schoenhals1873`, pp. 169-170, n173-n174; images: https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n173.jpg, https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n174.jpg.

Allerdings brach das Eis hie und da unter der Last der Fliehenden, im Ganzen aber waren die Teiche sehr fest gefroren, und die Zahl der ertrunkenen Russen war gewiss sehr unbedeutend; nach der Aus- / sage des Tellnitzer Pfarrers dürften sie auf 60 bis höchstens 100 Mann anzunehmen sein.
- **translation**: Neither the French Guard infantry nor Oudinot's grenadier division had been engaged. The drowning of so many Russians in the ponds is one of the French exaggerations. ### C4.17 `sokolov.p71.suchet-drain`

- **source**: `sokolov2006`, p. 71, n70; endnote 88 on p. 74, n73; images: https://archive.org/download/1799-1805-.-ii/page/n70.jpg, https://archive.org/download/1799-1805-.-ii/page/n73.jpg.
- **locator, as the source skeptic gives it**: pp. 70-71 (the cut sentence begins on p. 70, n69: 'Однако после'), n69-n70; note 88 on p. 74, n73
- **quote** (read on: page image): [...] после публикаций документов местных архивов, которые в начале XX века были осуществлены краеведом А. Словаком, а также подробного разбора обстоятельств этих событий, который произвел Колен, сомнений быть не может — эпизод с гибелью тысяч людей в озере является вымыслом. Через несколько дней после битвы под наблюдением генерала Сюше, назначенного к этому времени губернатором Брюнна, был произведен слив воды из прудов. Вот что он докладывал в документе, датированном 16 декабря 1805 г.: «Исполняя приказ Вашего Величества, я осуществил спуск воды из пруда поблизости от Ауэзда. Эта операция заняла 5 дней... Уже в первые дни понижение уровня воды позволило извлечь 12 русских пушек, и общее количество тех, которые были найдены в пруду, составило 36. 138 конских трупов и 3 человеческих тела — вот что нам удалось обнаружить»⁸⁸. Note 88 (p. 74): 'Цит. по: Alombert P.-C, Colin J. La campagne de 1805 en Allemagne. Paris, 2002, t. 5, p. 297.'
- **translation**: [...] after the local historian A. Slovák's publications of documents from local archives in the early twentieth century, and Colin's detailed examination, there can be no doubt: the episode of thousands dying in the lake is a fiction. A few days after the battle, under the supervision of General Suchet, by then governor of Brünn, the ponds were drained. This is what he reported in a document dated 16 December 1805: 'Carrying out Your Majesty's order, I have let the water out of the pond near Augezd. The operation took 5 days... Already in the first days the fall of the water let us pull out 12 Russian guns, and the total found in the pond came to 36. 138 horse carcasses and 3 human bodies: that is what we were able to find.' Note 88: quoted from Alombert and Colin, La campagne de 1805 en Allemagne, Paris 2002 [reprint], t. 5, p. 297.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: A report of General Suchet dated 16 December 1805, as Sokolov quotes it in Russian: the pond near Augezd drained over 5 days; 36 Russian guns (12 in the first days), 138 horse carcasses, 3 human bodies. The only record of the draining found in this reading. Sokolov names A. Slovák (local archives, early 20th century) and Colin as his grounds for calling the mass drowning a fiction.
- **does not settle**: The French text of Suchet's report (not read; grade A only once read in print); which pond (near Augezd: Satschan, by inference); whether other ponds were drained; the map's figures 'as usually given' (38 guns, about 130 horses, two men), which differ from this quotation (36, 138, 3) and were not found in any source read.
- **bears on**: FEATURES[#satschan].facts; PHASES[8].lede; EVENTS[#ice].why; ANALYSIS[#collapse].text; SOURCE_NOTE.refs[2].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 3 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept with corrections. Quote and note 88 exact. Sokolov writes 'Paris, 2002, t. 5, p. 297'; 'reprint' is the researcher's word. The archive.org item la-campagne-de-1805-en-allemagne-vol.-4 searched by the researcher is 'TOME QUATRIÈME' covering late October to early November (its maps: 28 octobre to 3 novembre; 3 OCR mentions of Austerlitz), so its silence does not test Sokolov's citation. The quotation reaches us third-hand (Suchet's French > the 2002 edition > Sokolov's Russian); B is the ceiling. On p. 70 Sokolov also says the pond was a fish pond in which those who fell through stood at most chest-deep and that the horses died of cold rather than drowning (counter reading sokolov.p70.fishpond, grade C: no source named). Not read by a person.

### C4.18 `ujezd2025.kaple`

- **source**: `ujezd2025`, the stop's text.
- **quote** (read on: text; the text layer only): V květnu 1703 byla kaple dokončena a byla zasvěcena sv. Antonínu Paduánskému. [...] Roku 1785 Josef II. nakázal, aby Církev kapli odevzala k účelům všedním. Kříž, který stál před kaplí byl umístěn na hřbitov před sokolovnou a od té doby kaple chátrala. V pondělí 2. prosince 1805 zazněl od kaple výstřel z děla, který započal bitvu tří císařů. Tou dobou kaple přišla o střechu a roku 1814 z ní nezbylo ani obvodové zdivo. [...] Dne 5. července 1863 byla kaple slavnostně posvěcena. [...] text byl volně přepsán z kroniky církevních staveb, r. v. kolem 1900.
- **translation**: In May 1703 the chapel was completed and consecrated to St Anthony of Padua. [...] In 1785 Joseph II ordered the Church to give the chapel up to profane use. The cross before the chapel was moved to the cemetery, and from then on the chapel decayed. On Monday 2 December 1805 a cannon shot from the chapel began the Battle of the Three Emperors. At that time the chapel lost its roof, and by 1814 not even its outer walls remained. [...] On 5 July 1863 the chapel was solemnly consecrated. [...] The text was freely rewritten from the chronicle of church buildings, written about 1900.
- **grade**: C. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The town's own text: completed May 1703; closed 1785; roofless 'at that time'; walls gone by 1814; reconsecrated 5 July 1863. It says nothing of a battery, of 24 guns, of IV Corps or of Napoleon at the chapel, and does not say the chapel burned.
- **does not settle**: Anything about guns or command posts; the local tradition of the 'first shot' from the chapel is unsupported by the period sources read (label uncertain).
- **bears on**: FEATURES[#chapel].facts; FORMATIONS.heightguns.track[8].act; FORMATIONS.heightguns.strengthNote.

'Tou dobou kaple přišla o střechu' is 'at that time the chapel lost its roof' (a loss about 1805, not 'roofless and decaying' before it): proposal 3c's 'roofless at the time of the battle' should read 'lost its roof about the time of the battle'. The text opens 'Hned nad Újezdem se tyčí pahorek zvaný Na Kříbech'. Not read by a person.

### C4.20 `turistika.kaple`

- **source**: `turistika_kaple`, the place description.
- **quote** (read on: text; the text layer only): Původně středověká poutní svatyně vyhořelala v roce 1805 při bitvě Tří císařů, kdy zde měla stanoviště francouzská baterie s 24 děly Napoleonovy gardy a IV. armádní sbor, který odtud podnikl závěrečný útok na spojenecká vojska. Stávající kaple zde byla opětovně postavena v roce 1863.
- **translation**: Originally a medieval pilgrimage shrine, it burned in 1805 in the Battle of the Three Emperors, when a French battery with 24 guns of Napoleon's Guard and the IV Army Corps, which made the final attack on the Allied troops from here, had its post here. The present chapel was rebuilt here in 1863.
- **grade**: C. **label**: disputed.

It rose on the Russians' side, showed us to their fire and dazzled us.

### C4.24 `savary.p203-205.night-seven`

- **source**: `savary1828`, t. II, pp. 203-205, n214-n216; images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresduducder04savagoog/page/n214.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoiresduducder04savagoog/page/n215.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoiresduducder04savagoog/page/n216.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): je trouvai l'empereur couché sur la paille et dormant profondément sous une baraque que les soldats lui avaient faite [...] il fut reconnu par les soldats, qui allumèrent spontanément des torches de paille [...] L'empereur rentra très-tard [...]. Il était éveillé et debout à la pointe du jour, pour faire prendre en silence les armes à toute l'armée. Il y avait un brouillard très-épais, qui enveloppait tous nos bivouacs au point de ne pouvoir distinguer à dix pas. [...] / A mesure que le jour arrivait, le brouillard paraissait se disposer à remonter. [...] Je revins en rendre compte. Il était à peu près sept heures du matin; le brouillard était déjà assez remonté pour que je n'eusse plus besoin de suivre la ligne des troupes pour ne pas m'égarer (on était à deux cents toises des Russes). L'empereur voyait toute son armée, l'infanterie et la cavalerie formées en colonnes par divisions. Tous les maréchaux étaient près de lui et le tourmentaient pour commencer : il résista à leurs instances jusqu'à ce que l'attaque des / Russes se fût plus prononcée à sa droite [...]. Lorsque l'empereur jugea à la vivacité du feu que l'attaque était sérieuse, il fit partir tous les maréchaux et leur ordonna de commencer.
- **translation**: I found the Emperor lying on straw, fast asleep under a shelter the soldiers had made him [...] he was recognised by the soldiers, who spontaneously lit straw torches [...]. The Emperor came back very late [...]. He was awake and up at the break of day, to have the whole army take arms in silence. There was a very thick fog over all our bivouacs, so that one could not see ten paces. [...] As the day came on, the fog seemed ready to lift. [...] I came back to report. It was about seven in the morning; the fog had already lifted enough that I no longer needed to follow the line of troops to keep my way (we were two hundred toises from the Russians). The Emperor could see his whole army, infantry and cavalry in columns by divisions. All the marshals were near him, pressing him to begin: he resisted until the Russians' attack on his right had become more pronounced [...]. When he judged by the liveliness of the fire that the attack was serious, he sent off all the marshals and ordered them to begin.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Napoleon's aide-de-camp: Napoleon slept under a straw shelter the soldiers made; up at daybreak; thick fog; about seven the fog had lifted enough, the whole army in view, all the marshals with him; he held them until the attack on his right was pronounced, then sent them off.
- **does not settle**: The place (no mound named); the hour he took post; disputed against Thiébault ('le jour ne venait qu'à huit heures', §4.2) and Lejeune (sun on the horizon at eight).
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.gqg.track[0]; PHASES[0].events[2]; PLANS.fr.author; PHASES[3].events[0]; PHASES[3].flash.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 3 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept. Exact. The straw-shelter sleep is on the evening of 1 Dec, before he rode out along his line and was recognised; '(on était à deux cents toises des Russes)' is Savary's own place at the extreme right, not the Emperor's. Not read by a person.

### C4.26 `lejeune.p35-36.sun-eight`

- **source**: `lejeune1895`, t. I, pp. 35-36, n54-n55; images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugnrale00lejegoog/page/n54.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugnrale00lejegoog/page/n55.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Après cette mémorable soirée, après cette belle nuit d'hiver, nous vîmes se lever le beau soleil d'Austerlitz. Le 2 décembre 1805, jour anniversaire du couronnement de l'Empereur, il était huit heures du matin lorsqu'il apparut sur l'horizon de la Moravie, pur et radieux comme aux plus beaux jours du printemps. / Une légère vapeur adoucissait les teintes, et nous permettait cependant de voir distinctement cent vingt mille baïonnettes qui luisaient au soleil et qui s'avançaient lentement vers nous
- **translation**: After that memorable evening and fine winter night, we saw the fine sun of Austerlitz rise. On 2 December 1805, the anniversary of the Emperor's coronation, it was eight in the morning when it appeared on the horizon of Moravia, pure and radiant as on the finest spring days. A light haze softened the colours, yet let us see distinctly a hundred and twenty thousand bayonets glittering in the sun and advancing slowly towards us.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: A participant's clock hour for the sunrise, eight in the morning, and his phrase 'le beau soleil d'Austerlitz' for the rising sun; a light haze in his view.
- **does not settle**: The hour of the Pratzen climb; the clock's basis (decision 69). Agrees with Thiébault's day at eight (§4.2); against Savary's fog lifted about seven.

### C4.27 `lejeune.p39-40.augezd-ice`

- **source**: `lejeune1895`, t. I, pp. 39-40, n58-n59; images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugnrale00lejegoog/page/n58.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugnrale00lejegoog/page/n59.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Cette petite scène se passait sur un tertre élevé au-dessus du village (de Augesd), en face des lacs, grands étangs formés par la digue de Tellnitz. Dans ce même temps, le corps austro-russe [...] chercha à se sauver par la digue de Tellnitz [...]; mais déjà la hauteur était occupée par notre artillerie de la garde, et la cavalerie seule put en hasarder le / passage en traversant au galop sous le feu de la mitraille. L'infanterie [...] n'entrevit de salut qu'en essayant de franchir sur la glace les larges étangs [...]. Quelques hommes, en effet, purent passer; mais [...] une masse de plus de six mille hommes [...] disparut en deux secondes sous les glaçons brisés, sans qu'un seul homme pût s'échapper en nageant à la surface. [...] Une artillerie considérable des Russes resta attelée sur le bord du lac ou renversée dans l'eau [...]. Au moment où les eaux engloutissaient les derniers débris de cette armée [...], le soleil descendit sous l'horizon en se cachant derrière d'épais nuages, la neige tomba aussitôt
- **translation**: This little scene took place on a knoll above the village (of Augezd), facing the lakes, large ponds formed by the Tellnitz dyke. At the same time the Austro-Russian corps tried to escape by the Tellnitz dyke; but the height was already held by our Guard artillery, and only the cavalry could risk the passage at the gallop under grapeshot. The infantry saw no safety but in crossing the wide ponds on the ice. A few men got across; but a mass of more than six thousand men vanished in two seconds under the broken ice, not one escaping by swimming. [...] A considerable Russian artillery remained harnessed at the lake's edge or overturned in the water [...]. As the waters swallowed the last remnants, the sun went down behind thick clouds and snow fell at once.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: Lejeune (with the Emperor) puts Napoleon on 'un tertre élevé au-dessus du village (de Augesd), en face des lacs' at the end, and the Guard artillery on 'la hauteur'.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: 'the Guard artillery on la hauteur' is right, but the proposals (3a, 3b) turn it into 'the Guard artillery holding the height above Augezd': in Lejeune 'la hauteur' is the height that barred the escape 'par la digue de Tellnitz'; that it is the tertre above Augezd is an inference. Proposal 3d says Lejeune has the Emperor 'with Berthier and Soult' there: neither is named on pp. 38-40 (n57-n59).
- **does not settle**: The chapel by name; a gun count; IV Corps guns. His 'more than six thousand' drowned is disputed (Stutterheim 'einige', Schönhals 60-100, Suchet's 3 bodies as quoted by Sokolov).
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.gqg.track[8]; FORMATIONS.heightguns.track[8]; FEATURES[#chapel].facts; FEATURES[#satschan]; EVENTS[#ice].

### C4.28 `marbot.p258.grange`

- **source**: `marbot1891`, t. I (27th ed.), p. 258, n277 (also the image reading of register §8.1, which was OCR); images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugn01marbuoft/page/n277.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image, part on the text layer (see the source line)): Le 1er décembre, veille de la bataille, Napoléon, ayant quitté Brünn dès le matin, employa toute la journée à examiner les positions, et fit établir le soir son quartier général en arrière du centre de l'armée française, sur un point d'où l'œil embrassait les bivouacs des deux partis, ainsi que le terrain qui devait leur servir de champ de bataille le lendemain. Il n'existait d'autre bâtiment en ce lieu qu'une mauvaise grange : on y plaça les tables et les cartes de l'Empereur, qui s'établit de sa personne auprès d'un immense feu, au milieu de son nombreux état-major et de sa garde. [...] Il n'y avait point de lune, et l'obscurité de la nuit était augmentée par un épais brouillard qui rendait la marche fort difficile.
- **translation**: On 1 December, the eve of the battle, Napoleon, having left Brünn in the morning, spent the day examining the positions, and in the evening had his headquarters set up behind the centre of the French army, at a point from which the eye took in the bivouacs of both sides and the ground of the next day's battle. The only building there was a poor barn: the Emperor's tables and maps were put in it, and he settled by a huge fire amid his staff and his guard. [...] There was no moon, and the darkness was increased by a thick fog that made marching very difficult.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: Marbot's headquarters of 1 December: behind the centre, on a commanding point, a poor barn, a fire; no moon, thick fog (register §8.1 now read on the image).
- **does not settle**: Which point (no name); it differs in detail from the Bulletin (straw hut), Savary (straw shelter), Lejeune (fire by his carriage).
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.gqg.track[0]; FEATURES[#zuran].

### C4.29 `marbot.p259.trap`

- **source**: `marbot1891`, t. I, p. 259, n278; images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugn01marbuoft/page/n278.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Le lendemain 2 décembre, le canon se fit entendre au point du jour. Nous avons vu que l'Empereur avait montré peu de troupes à sa droite; c'était un piège qu'il tendait aux ennemis, afin qu'ils eussent la possibilité de prendre facilement Telnitz, d'y passer le ruisseau de Goldbach et d'aller ensuite à Gross-Raigern s'emparer de la route de Brünn à Vienne, afin de nous couper ainsi tout moyen de retraite. - **translation**: Next day, 2 December, the guns were heard at daybreak. We have seen that the Emperor had shown few troops on his right; it was a trap he set for the enemy, so that they could easily take Telnitz, cross the Goldbach and go on to Gross-Raigern to seize the Brünn-Vienna road and so cut our retreat. The Austro-Russians fell right into the snare.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
