### C4.30 `thieb.p449.monticule-reread`

- **source**: `thiebault3_1894`, t. III, p. 449, n460 (second reading of register §4.1); images: https://archive.org/download/mmoires03thieuoft/page/n460.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): et la réserve de grenadiers, ainsi que la garde impériale, quittèrent Brünn pour camper autour du bivouac de l'Empereur, qui fut placé sur un monticule derrière le village de Kritschen. Ainsi prêt à se reployer ou à combattre, mais plus disposé à une retraite qu'à une bataille si l'ennemi ne faisait pas la faute sur laquelle il comptait, telle fut la position dans laquelle l'Empereur résolut d'attendre les événements.
- **translation**: and the grenadier reserve and the Imperial Guard left Brünn to camp around the Emperor's bivouac, which was placed on a hillock behind the village of Kritschen. Thus ready to fall back or to fight, but more inclined to a retreat than a battle if the enemy did not make the mistake he counted on, such was the position in which the Emperor resolved to await events.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Register §4.1 confirmed; Thiébault's 'monticule derrière le village de Kritschen' agrees with the 1806 French note's 'bivouac du quartier-général derrière Kritchen' (stutfr2.p68-69.note7): two placings behind Kritschen.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: The passage is dated by p. 448 (n459): Napoleon 'dicta des ordres de manœuvre plutôt que de retraite, d'après lesquels, le 29, un peu avant le jour' Lannes and Soult fell back; the grenadiers and Guard then came from Brünn to camp round the Emperor's bivouac 'sur un monticule derrière le village de Kritschen'. That is the bivouac from 29 November. Order 9534 of 1 December moves headquarters 'à la butte'. So 'two placings behind Kritschen' are not placings of the 2 December post, and cannot support gqg@0.
- **does not settle**: Where Kritschen is (not in GEOREF; not identified here); that the hillock is the Zuran.
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.gqg.track[0]; FEATURES[#zuran].

The Materialien 1806 (p. 131, n140, read) puts a part of the French army 'bei Kritschen und Lesch', i.e. Kritschen lies with Lesch (Líšeň) towards the French left rear; still unidentified. Not read by a person.

### C4.32 `thieb.p508.augezd-two`

- **source**: `thiebault3_1894`, t. III, p. 508, n521; images: https://archive.org/download/mmoires03thieuoft/page/n521.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Or il résulte des déclarations formelles de cet officier que, le 2 décembre, à la pointe du jour, le maréchal Soult est arrivé à la division Vandamme et l'a mise en mouvement [...]; qu'il n'a reparu à la division Vandamme que lorsque vers deux heures après midi, et tous les grands combats terminés, elle entrait à Augezd. C'était le moment où les réserves avançant sur le terrain conquis (et beaucoup trop tard pour avoir une action), la garde impériale, le maréchal Lannes, avec une de ses divisions et une du premier corps, notre cavalerie, ainsi que l'Empereur, se trouvaient sur les hauteurs de Pratzen, par conséquent, au moment où la bataille allait finir. Le maréchal Soult n'apparut là que pour transmettre l'ordre de se porter au bord du lac de Satschan et de barrer sur ce point la retraite aux Russes
- **translation**: Now it follows from that officer's [General Vincent's] formal statements that at daybreak on 2 December Marshal Soult came to Vandamme's division and set it moving [...]; that he reappeared with it only when, towards two in the afternoon and all the great fighting over, it was entering Augezd. That was the moment when the reserves were advancing over the conquered ground (far too late to act), and the Imperial Guard, Marshal Lannes with one of his divisions and one of the first corps, our cavalry, and the Emperor were on the heights of Pratzen, at the moment the battle was about to end. Soult appeared there only to pass on the order to go to the edge of the Satschan lake and bar the Russians' retreat.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Via Vandamme's aide Vincent: Vandamme's division entering Augezd towards 14:00; the Emperor then still on the Pratzen heights.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: 'the Emperor then still on the Pratzen heights' is read as distinct from the chapel; Stutterheim (p. 74) describes one crest 'von Pratzen bis zu der Kapelle ober Aujest', so 'les hauteurs de Pratzen' may include the chapel height. It is not evidence against the chapel post.
- **does not settle**: When Napoleon reached the chapel (a sequence Pratzen then chapel agrees with Schönhals p. 167, by inference); the clock's basis.
- **bears on**: FORMATIONS.gqg.track[8]; PHASES[8].lede; FORMATIONS.heightguns.track[8].moveMin.

Exact. Hearsay via Vincent inside Thiébault's polemic against Soult; B is the ceiling. Not read by a person.

### C4.33 `davout.p202-206.report-ice`

- **source**: `davout1885`, no. 127 'A l'Empereur et Roi', 'Presbourg, 5 nivôse' [an XIV, 26 Dec. 1805], pp. 202-206, n267, n270, n271; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n267.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n270.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n271.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 202) Sire, Votre Majesté m'a ordonné, par sa lettre du 22 de ce mois, de lui adresser un rapport plus circonstancié de tout ce qu'ont fait les troupes que je commandais le jour de la bataille d'Austerlitz. [...] (p. 205) Pendant ce mouvement, les troupes de la division Legrand parurent sur les crêtes en arrière; un des régiments de cette division et le 8e régiment de hussards arrivèrent à portée de l'ennemi, dont la colonne entière mit bas les armes, après quelques coups de fusil. La glace du lac sur lequel cette colonne fut jetée venait d'être rompue par les chevaux des officiers qui s'étaient sauvés; d'ailleurs l'arrivée des troupes françaises de l'autre côté de ce lac ôtait à l'ennemi tout espoir de salut. Ce fut à ce moment que s'engagea une forte canonnade / (p. 206) sur les hauteurs au delà de Telnitz. [...] A la hauteur de Telnitz, la brigade du général Heudelet atteignit une forte colonne qui se retirait dans le plus grand désordre et fit sur elle, tout en la poursuivant, un feu très-vif de mousqueterie et d'artillerie, qui lui tua encore beaucoup de monde; cette brigade se trouvait alors en potence avec des troupes du maréchal Soult : elle eut avec elles le spectacle des Russes se submergeant dans le lac, par leur précipitation à s'échapper.
- **translation**: Sire, Your Majesty ordered me by your letter of the 22nd of this month to send a more detailed report of all that the troops I commanded did on the day of the battle of Austerlitz. [...] During this movement the troops of Legrand's division appeared on the crests behind; one of its regiments and the 8th hussars came within reach of the enemy, whose whole column laid down its arms after a few shots. The ice of the lake onto which this column was thrown had just been broken by the horses of officers who had escaped; besides, French troops arriving on the far side of the lake took away all hope of escape. At that moment a heavy cannonade began on the heights beyond Telnitz. [...] Level with Telnitz, Heudelet's brigade caught up a strong column retreating in great disorder and, pursuing it, kept up a very lively musket and artillery fire that killed many more; this brigade was then at right angles to Marshal Soult's troops: with them it had the spectacle of the Russians plunging into the lake in their haste to escape.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: A report to the Emperor dated 26 December 1805: Russians seen plunging into the lake near Telnitz in their haste to escape; a column thrown onto a lake whose ice fleeing officers' horses had broken (by Sokolnitz, by inference); 'une forte canonnade sur les hauteurs au delà de Telnitz' at that moment.
- **does not settle**: A number drowned; the chapel; whose guns; the lake's name.
- **bears on**: EVENTS[#ice]; FEATURES[#satschan]; ANALYSIS[#collapse].text; FORMATIONS.heightguns.track[8].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 3 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept. Exact. The heading prints only 'Presbourg, 5 nivôse'; the year and 26 Dec 1805 are the editor's context (footnote: the Emperor's request of 22 frimaire an XIV, 13 Dec 1805). The p. 205 lake (the column by Sokolnitz 'acculé sur des lacs') and the p. 206 lake (seen 'à la hauteur de Telnitz') may be different waters; do not merge them with Satschan by default. Not read by a person.

### C4.c1 `corr11.p442.soult-0730`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9535 'Dispositions générales pour la journée du 11', 'Au bivouac en avant de Brünn, 10 frimaire an XIV (1er décembre 1805), 8 heures et demie du soir', p. 442, n451; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n451_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): M. le maréchal Soult donnera les ordres pour que ses trois divisions soient placées au delà du ravin, à sept heures du matin, de manière à être prêtes à commencer la manœuvre de la journée, qui doit être une marche en avant par échelons, l'aile droite en avant. M. le maréchal Soult sera de sa personne, à sept heures et demie du matin, près de l'Empereur, à son bivouac.
- **translation**: Marshal Soult will give orders for his three divisions to be placed beyond the ravine at seven in the morning, ready to begin the day's manoeuvre, which is to be an advance in echelons, right wing forward. Marshal Soult will be in person at half past seven in the morning near the Emperor, at his bivouac.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The written dispositions of 1 Dec, 20:30: Soult's divisions placed beyond the ravine at 07:00; Soult in person at the Emperor's bivouac at 07:30 (besides the general summons of the marshals on p. 443); the day's manoeuvre an echelon advance, right wing forward.
- **does not settle**: Where the bivouac was; whether the hours were kept; the morning's last orders.
- **bears on**: PLANS.fr.author; PHASES[0].events[2]; FORMATIONS.gqg.track[0].act.
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

### C4.c2 `corr11.p448.cette-idee`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9541, 30th Bulletin, p. 448, n457; images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n457_w1600.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Il défilait par une marche de flanc, sur une ligne de quatre lieues, en prolongeant l'armée française, qui paraissait ne pas oser sortir de sa position. Il n'avait qu'une crainte, c'était que l'armée française ne lui échappât. On fit tout pour confirmer l'ennemi dans cette idée. Le prince Murat fit avancer un petit corps de cavalerie dans la plaine; mais tout d'un coup il parut étonné des forces immenses de l'ennemi, et rentra à la hâte. Ainsi tout tendait à confirmer le général russe dans l'opération mal calculée qu'il avait arrêtée.
- **translation**: He filed past in a flank march on a line of four leagues, along the French army, which seemed not to dare leave its position. He had only one fear: that the French army would escape him. Everything was done to confirm the enemy in that idea. Prince Murat sent a small body of cavalry forward into the plain, but suddenly appeared astonished at the enemy's immense forces and came back in haste. So everything tended to confirm the Russian general in the ill-calculated operation he had decided on.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: In the Bulletin 'cette idée' is the enemy's fear that the French army would escape; the Bulletin separately says everything tended to confirm the Russian general in his operation.
- **does not settle**: That the right was weakened on purpose; the plateau.
- **bears on**: ANALYSIS[#deception].text; COMMAND.fr[0][3]; COMMAND.fr[0][4].
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

### C4.c4 `stutfr2.p68-69.centre-pratzen`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806fr2`, pp. 68-69, note (7) of the French officer; n71, n72; images: https://archive.org/download/bub_gb_bLCe2qyA_D8C/page/n71.jpg, https://archive.org/download/bub_gb_bLCe2qyA_D8C/page/n72.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): car, ne voulant se battre qu'autant que l'ennemi feroit de très-grandes fautes, il étoit résolu à se porter encore une journée en arrière, si l'ennemi manœuvroit sagement. Il ne vouloit point engager une bataille corps à corps contre une armée supérieure et qui seroit bien postée. [...] / [...] car l'attaque qu'avoit projetée l'Empereur dépendoit spécialement de la promptitude de la marche du centre sur les hauteurs de Pratzen.
- **translation**: for, wishing to fight only if the enemy made very great mistakes, he was resolved to fall back another day if the enemy manoeuvred wisely. He did not wish to engage a pitched battle against a superior and well-posted army. [...] for the attack the Emperor had planned depended especially on the speed of the centre's march onto the heights of Pratzen.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: An 1806 French note: the planned attack depended on the centre's rapid march onto the Pratzen heights; Napoleon would have fallen back if the enemy manoeuvred wisely.
- **does not settle**: That the right was weakened to draw the enemy off the plateau (the 'leaving the plateau' step remains an inference); who the officer was.

### C4.c13 `materialien1806.p121.zusatz15`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, Zusätze Nro. 15, p. 121, n130 (keyed from the translated Stutterheim, p. 70, n79, note 15); images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n130.jpg, https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n79.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Dieser Umstand ward in den französischen officiellen Berichten anfänglich mit sehr hohen Farben geschildert, inzwischen man ihn von andrer Seite geradezu abläugnete. So steht in dem 30sten Bulletin der großen Armee: „Le corps de l'ennemi, qui avoit été cerné et chassé de toutes les hauteurs,“ (es ist hier von der ersten und zweiten Colonne die Rede) „se trouvoit dans un bas-fond et acculé à un lac. L'Empereur s'y porta avec vingt pièces de canon. Ce corps fut chassé de position en position, et l'on vit un spectacle horrible tel qu'on l'avoit vu à Aboukir, vingt mille hommes se jetant dans l'eau et se noyant dans les lacs“. Der Vf. unsers Aufsatzes wird wohl der Wahrheit am treuesten geblieben seyn.
- **translation**: This circumstance was at first painted in very lurid colours in the French official reports, while from another side it was flatly denied. Thus the 30th Bulletin says: [...] 'twenty thousand men throwing themselves into the water and drowning in the lakes'. The author of our essay [Stutterheim, 'einige'] has no doubt kept closest to the truth.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: Already in 1806 the Weimar editor judged the Bulletin's 20,000 drowned an exaggeration, noted a flat denial 'from another side', and preferred Stutterheim's 'einige'; the Bulletin's 'vingt pièces de canon' is quoted (register §1.6).
- **does not settle**: A count; who denied it.
- **bears on**: FEATURES[#satschan]; PHASES[8].lede; EVENTS[#ice].why; ANALYSIS[#collapse].text; SOURCE_NOTE.refs[4].
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

### C4.c14 `sokolov.p70.fishpond`

- **source**: `sokolov2006`, p. 70, n69; images: https://archive.org/download/1799-1805-.-ii/page/n69.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Но дело в том, что Зачан был не озером а огромным прудом для разведения рыбы. Его глубина была такова, что провалившиеся под лед оказались максимум по грудь в воде. Что касается артиллерийских упряжек, то они застряли в иле, и брошенные артиллеристами пушки остались в ледяной воде вместе с барахтающимися почти намертво прикрепленными к ним лошадьми. Последние, действительно, погибли, но не от того, что утонули, а от того, что замерзли
- **translation**: But the point is that Satschan was not a lake but a huge fish pond. Its depth was such that those who fell through the ice were at most chest-deep in water. As for the gun teams, they stuck in the mud, and the guns abandoned by the gunners stayed in the icy water with the struggling horses almost inseparably harnessed to them. The horses did die, not by drowning but of cold.
- **grade**: C. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Sokolov's own judgment (no source named in this passage): a shallow fish pond, chest-deep; the horses died of cold.
- **does not settle**: The pond's depth in 1805 from a document; any count.
- **bears on**: FEATURES[#satschan].story; ANALYSIS[#collapse].text; EVENTS[#ice].why.
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

**The source skeptic's summary** (C4; its counts and "CRn" numbers are its working list's: the verdicts above are the record): Every reading's page images were downloaded fresh and read (or the web pages re-fetched). All quotations are on their pages; no reading is rejected. The main corrections: (1) Thiébault's 'monticule derrière le village de Kritschen' is dated by p. 448 to the bivouac set up from 29 November, and order 9534 moved headquarters 'à la butte' on 1 December; neither Thiébault nor the 1806 French note places the 2 December post, so the 'two placings behind Kritschen' must not support gqg@0 (B still stands). The Materialien 1806 p. 131 puts Kritschen with Lesch and Bellowitz (French left rear); it remains unidentified. (2) The Bulletin's 'On fit tout pour confirmer l'ennemi dans cette idée' refers to the enemy's fear that the French would escape, not to the flank march; proposal 2b mis-splices it. (3) The 1806 French note (p. 69) and M-D (p. 177) attest that the planned attack was the centre's march on the Pratzen heights, so 'the plateau' is not merely the map's reading; only the link from the thin right to emptying the plateau is inference. (4) The Materialien's ice note is 15, not 25; Zusatz 15 (p. 121) was found and read: the 1806 editor already doubted the Bulletin's 20,000 and sided with Stutterheim. (5) Lejeune names neither Berthier nor Soult on the tertre above Augezd; his 'la hauteur' commands the Tellnitz dyke. (6) Stutterheim p. 78 and Schönhals p. 168 give French guns by Tellnitz besides the height above Augezd. (7) heightguns is a IV Corps child; an act naming Guard guns describes guns it does not own. (8) The ice event's cf A fits fire on the retreat, not fire on the ice. (9) Ségur grade B, not C. (10) The archive.org Alombert-Colin t. IV ends in early November and cannot test Sokolov's 't. 5, p. 297 (Paris, 2002)'. (11) lejeune1895 needs a src_new (archive.org metadata: Mémoires du général Lejeune, t. I, Firmin-Didot, 1895; title page not read by me). Not re-read here: register §1.6 (Bulletin p. 451, beyond its quotation in Zusatz 15), §4.8 (Thiébault p. 466) and §8.3; the Materialien chapel sentence (c. p. 93) on its image. **The researcher's notes** (C4): 1) The map's 'Újezd local history' for the chapel battery is, on the evidence found, a tourist website (turistika.cz, user 'pasatur', updated 2.12.2023: '24 děly Napoleonovy gardy a IV. armádní sbor', 'vyhořela v roce 1805'), not the town's history: the municipality's own text (after a parish chronicle of c. 1900) has 1703, 1785, roofless, ruined by 1814, reconsecrated 5 July 1863, and no battery. The corps attribution 'IV. Corps und der Garde' is in Schönhals 1873 p. 168 (no count); the count 24 is Thiébault's (Guard only). CHANGELOG lines 367-368 and the data's 'Újezd local history' should be re-attributed (data task). 2) The drained count: the map's 38 / about 130 / two was not found; Sokolov 2006 p. 71 quotes Suchet's report of 16 Dec. 1805: 36 guns, 138 horse carcasses, 3 human bodies, from 'the pond near Augezd', drained over 5 days; the French text is unread (A once read). Also: the Satschan pond was let out for good in 1834 (town text, C). 3) Drowning estimates now on record, each with its source: the Bulletin 20,000 (A, claim); Davout's report, Russians 'se submergeant dans le lac' (A, no number); Stutterheim 'einige' (A); Thiébault 3,000-4,000 (B); Marbot thousands (B); Lejeune more than 6,000 (B); Schönhals 60 to at most 100, on the Telnitz priest's statement (B); Suchet as quoted by Sokolov, 3 bodies (B via a modern quotation). 4) Hours for the chronology cluster (not this cluster's to set): the Bulletin's 01:00 ride; Savary's about 07:00 with the fog lifting and the marshals with him; Lejeune's sun at 08:00; Thiébault's day at 08:00 and Vandamme entering Augezd towards 14:00 (via Vincent, p. 508) with the Emperor then on the Pratzen heights; Schönhals's 14:30 for the whole left wing past the Satschan defile (p. 169), against the map's ice event at 15:00 (t:900) and phase 8 from 14:30; Davout's start 05:30-06:00 (p. 202). 5) Where Napoleon slept differs by source: a roofless straw hut made by the grenadiers (Bulletin, A), a straw shelter (Savary), a fire by his carriage (Lejeune), a barn and a fire behind the centre (Marbot), a hillock behind Kritschen (Thiébault; the 1806 note); the map does not draw the night, so no text needs it, but 'Kritschen' is worth identifying before anyone writes that the bivouac was on the Zuran. 6) The Bulletin puts the Emperor 'en réserve' with the Guard and grenadiers, and has 'On fit tout pour confirmer l'ennemi dans cette idée': the deception text can rest on the Bulletin (A) for the intention as the army's own account, on the proclamation only for the expectation. 7) Question 160's measurement (headquarters' confidence patch at opening-1 and opening-end) was not made: per the cluster brief, only the grade is reported. 8) The first attempt left no notes file; every quote above was read on its image in this attempt except those marked OCR or text.

Cavallerie-Batterien / Sesajew-Kosaken — — 5 500 500 / Melentiew-Kosaken — — 5 500 500 / Summa . 5 2 32 3/4 6340 2440
- **translation**: Advance guard, FML Baron Kienmayer: GM Carneville's brigade, Vienna Jäger 2 companies 300; Brod Grenz 1 bn 500; 2nd Szekler 2 bn 1,300; 1st Szekler 2 bn 1,300; GM Stutterheim, O'Reilly chevau-légers 8 sq 900, Merveldt uhlans 1/4 sq 40; GM Count Nostitz, Schwarzenberg uhlans 1/2 sq 100, Hessen-Homburg hussars 8 sq 300; GM Prince Moritz Liechtenstein, Szekler hussars 6 sq 600, 2 Austrian cavalry batteries, Sysoev and Melentiev Cossacks 5 sq and 500 each; total 5 bn, 2 coys, 32 3/4 sq, 6,340 men, 2,440 horses.

Weber | Nassau-Cürassier — — 8 300 300 / Lothringen-Cürassier — — 8 300 300 / Kaiser-Cürassier — — 8 500 500 / Summa — — 24 1100 1100 / GL. Essen | GM. [...] Avantgarde des FML. Baron Kienmayer 5 2 32 3/4 6340 2940 [...] 5. Colonne — — 69 5600 5600 / Corps des Fürsten Bagration 15 — 45 13000 4500 / Kaiserl. russ. Garden 10 1 17 9380 2900 / Summa 114 10 170 3/4 82575 16565.
- **translation**: Cavalry corps or 5th Column, FML Prince Liechtenstein: under FML Prince Hohenlohe, GMs Caramelli and Weber, Nassau, Lorraine and Emperor cuirassiers, 24 sq, 1,100; under Lt-Gen Essen, GM Shepelev ('Czepelow'): Gordeev Cossacks 5 sq 500, Grand Duke Constantine uhlans 10 sq 1,000, St Petersburg dragoons 5 sq 500, Life Cuirassiers 5 sq 500; under Lt-Gen Uvarov, GM Penitsky ('Penitzky'): Kharkov and Chernigov dragoons 5 sq 500 each, horse artillery, Elisavetgrad hussars 10 sq 1,000; Russian sum 45 sq 4,500; corps total 69 sq, 5,600. Recapitulation: Kienmayer 6,340 (2,940 horses); 5th Column 5,600; Bagration 13,000; Guards 9,380; total 82,575 men, 16,565 horses.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.

The Life Cuirassiers and the St Petersburg dragoons are in the V Column under Essen/Shepelev. Uwarov" covers only GM Penitzky's brigade (Charchow 500, Czernikow 500, reitende Artillerie, Elisabethgrad 1000 = 2,000); Leib-Cürassier and Petersburg-Dragoner sit under Essen/Czepelow. The column without Uvarov is 3,600 (derived), not 3,100; the researcher's 2,500 is an arithmetic error.

"Summa der Garden" prints 4380 on the page and 9380 in the recap, the lines add to 9,730: the table has misprints. ### C5.3 `md1844.p166-169.columns`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, pp. 166-169 (IIIF k175-k178); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$175/full/1400,/0/default.jpg, https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$176/full/1400,/0/default.jpg, https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$177/full/1400,/0/default.jpg, https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$178/full/1400,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Ввечеру сдѣлали у насъ новое росписаніе полковъ по колоннамъ, послѣ чего составъ, въ которомъ войска выступили на другое утро къ Аустерлицу, и потомъ дѣйствовали въ сраженіи, былъ слѣдующій: [...] Впереди Дохтурова отрядъ Кинмейера. 2 полка казаковъ, 3 полка слабыхъ Венгерской конницы, 5 батальоновъ Кроатовъ. [...] 5-я колонна, Князя Лихтенштейна. Полки: Елисаветградскій гусарскій. Драгунскіе: Харьковскій, / Черниговскій, Уланскій Его Высочества, 32 эскадрона Австрійцовъ. Отрядъ Князя Багратіона. Гусарскіе: Павлоградскій, Маріупольскій. Драгунскіе: С.-Петербургскій, Тверской, Лейбъ-кирасирскій Ея Величества.
- **translation**: In the evening a new distribution of the regiments into columns was made, after which the composition in which the troops set out next morning toward Austerlitz and fought in the battle was as follows: [...] Ahead of Dokhturov, Kienmayer's detachment: 2 regiments of Cossacks, 3 weak regiments of Hungarian cavalry, 5 battalions of Croats. [...] 5th column, Prince Liechtenstein's: the Elisavetgrad hussars; the Kharkov and Chernigov dragoons; His Highness's uhlans; 32 squadrons of Austrians. Prince Bagration's detachment: Pavlograd and Mariupol hussars; St Petersburg and Tver dragoons; Her Majesty's Life Cuirassiers.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: the original lists Kienmayer's cavalry as '3 полка слабыхъ' (the 1846 translation '2 faibles'); no column strengths; the Empress's Life Cuirassiers and the St Petersburg dragoons with Bagration (p. 169), disputed against Schönhals p. 178.
- **does not settle**: numbers of men
- **bears on**: item 1 Kienmayer; item 8 Empress's cuirassiers; COMPOSITION.lich.note; COMPOSITION.kienmayer.note; item 3 lich.

### C5.15 `corr11.9537-9538-9541.losses-3dec`

- **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9537 (proclamation, p. 444, leaf n453), no. 9538 (to Joseph, pp. 444-445, n453-n454), no. 9541 (30th Bulletin, p. 452, n461); images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n453.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n454.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n461.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (9537) Une armée de 100,000 hommes, commandée par les empereurs de Russie et d'Autriche, a été, en moins de quatre heures, ou coupée ou dispersée. Ce qui a échappé à votre fer s'est noyé dans les lacs. Quarante drapeaux, les étendards de la garde impériale de Russie, cent vingt pièces de canon, vingt généraux, plus de 30,000 prisonniers, sont le résultat de cette journée à jamais célèbre. (9538) Leur armée était forte de 80,000 Russes et de 30,000 Autri- / chiens. Je leur ai fait à peu près 40,000 prisonniers, parmi lesquels une vingtaine de généraux russes, quarante drapeaux, cent pièces de canon, tous les étendards de la garde impériale de Russie. [...] L'ennemi a laissé au moins 12,000 ou 15,000 hommes sur le champ de bataille. Je ne connais pas encore ma perte; je l'évalue à 8 ou 900 hommes tués, et le double blessés. (9541) Les résultats de cette journée sont quarante drapeaux russes, parmi lesquels sont les étendards de la garde impériale, un nombre considérable de prisonniers (l'état-major ne les connaît pas encore tous; on avait déjà la note de 20,000); 12 ou 15 généraux, au moins 15,000 Russes tués, restés sur le champ de bataille. Quoiqu'on n'ait pas encore les rapports, on peut, au premier coup d'œil, évaluer notre perte à 800 hommes tués et à 15 ou 1,600 blessés.
- **translation**: (Proclamation, 3 Dec) An army of 100,000 men commanded by the emperors of Russia and Austria has in under four hours been cut up or dispersed; what escaped drowned in the lakes. Forty colours, the standards of the Russian Imperial Guard, 120 guns, 20 generals, more than 30,000 prisoners. (To Joseph, 3 Dec) Their army was 80,000 Russians and 30,000 Austrians. I took about 40,000 prisoners, some twenty Russian generals, forty colours, a hundred guns, all the Russian Imperial Guard's standards. The enemy left at least 12,000 or 15,000 men on the field. I put my loss at 8 or 900 killed and twice that wounded. (30th Bulletin, 3 Dec) Forty Russian colours with the Imperial Guard's standards; prisoners not yet all known, 20,000 already noted; 12 or 15 generals; at least 15,000 Russians killed; our loss at first glance 800 killed and 1,500 or 1,600 wounded.
- **grade**: A. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: Napoleon's claims dated 3 December: 40 colours; 120 or 100 guns; 20,000 to 40,000 prisoners; 12,000-15,000 Russian dead; French 800-900 killed and 1,500-1,800 wounded; the Allied army 100,000 or 80,000 + 30,000.
- **does not settle**: the losses themselves (claims of the victor written the next day, disagreeing among themselves)
- **bears on**: item 5 losses; PHASES[9].lede; TOUR[8].x.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 4 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept with corrections. Every quoted sentence exact. Missed on the same pages: 9537 "Ce qui a échappé à votre fer s'est noyé dans les lacs."; 9538 (source line "Mémoires du roi Joseph") "Mon armée sur le champ de bataille a été moins nombreuse que la sienne"; 9541 "L'Empereur s'y porta avec vingt pièces de canon" (Guard guns at the meres: 20, against 24 in Thiébault and Sokolov; see CR15), "Deux colonnes, chacune de 4,000 Russes, mettent bas les armes", and "105,000 hommes, dont 80,000 Russes et 25,000 Autrichiens" (against 9538's 80,000 + 30,000). p. 450 re-read: "quarante pièces de canon servies par les canonniers de la Garde" in the intervals of the reserve: confirmed. - **source**: `corr11_1863`, no. 9544 (to the Elector of Bavaria, p. 455, n464), no. 9545 (to the Elector of Württemberg, p. 456, n465), no. 9546 (31st Bulletin, pp. 457-458, n466-n467); images: https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n464.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n465.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n466.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondancede11napouoft/page/n467.jpg.

(9545) elle a perdu ses dra- / peaux, ses canons, 36,000 prisonniers et 20,000 morts. Ainsi a péri une armée de 80,000 Russes, belle et bonne. (9546, 31e BULLETIN DE LA GRANDE ARMÉE, Austerlitz, 14 frimaire an XIV (5 décembre 1805)) La perte des Russes a été immense. [...] Ce n'est pas 120 pièces de canon qu'on a prises, mais 150. ### C5.18 `md1844.p209-210.losses`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, pp. 209-210 (IIIF k221, k222; p. 209 a chapter opening without its number printed); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$221/full/1400,/0/default.jpg, https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$222/full/1400,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Подъ Аустерлицомъ Русская армія лишилась до 21,000 убитыми, ранеными и плѣнными, (*) и потеряла 133 орудія. (**) У Австрійцовъ выбыло изъ строя 5,922 человѣка. (***) Число утраченныхъ ими пушекъ намъ неизвѣстно, за необнародованіемъ о томъ свѣдѣній. По словамъ Французскихъ писателей / въ арміи Наполеона убито и ранено до 8,000, и безъ вѣсти пропало 760 человѣкъ, но Маршалъ Бернадотъ—въ послѣдствіи Шведскій Король—возвращаясь отъ Аустерлица къ Рейну, сказывалъ, что потеря Французовъ простиралась до 12,000 человѣкъ. (*) [...] Footnotes: (*) Строевые рапорты Кутузова. (**) Вѣдомость, поданная начальникомъ артиллеріи, Богдановымъ, Графу Аракчееву, на третій день послѣ Аустерлицкаго сраженія. (***) Oesterreichische Militair-Zeitschrift, 1822 p. 229. [p. 210] (*) Донесеніе Русскаго Посланника изъ Берлина, отъ 18-го Декабря.
- **translation**: At Austerlitz the Russian army lost up to 21,000 killed, wounded and prisoners (Kutuzov's field returns) and 133 guns (the statement of the chief of artillery Bogdanov to Arakcheev on the third day after the battle). By French writers, Napoleon's army had up to 8,000 killed and wounded and 760 missing, but Bernadotte, on his way back to the Rhine, said the French loss reached 12,000 (the Russian envoy at Berlin, 18 December).

### C5.19 `schoenhals1873.p156.french-strength`

Die meisten Generäle des verbündeten Heeres empfiengen erst gegen Früh die Disposition zum Angriff des Feindes. Sie beruhte auf der Annahme, dass Napoleon mit seinem linken Flügel in dem waldigen Gebirge links von der Strasse stehe, seinen rechten an die rückwärtigen Teiche gegen Kobelnitz und Sokolnitz lehne.

Так как потери австрийцев, по данным австрийских архивов, составили 5 922 человека, результирующая цифра потерь союзников при Аустерлице, писал историк, 27 тыс. человек. [...] Согласно архивным источникам, исследованным Коленом, общее количество взятых в плен русских и австрийских солдат составило 9 767 и 1 686 человек соответственно. [...] Курского полка— 736 человек, Галицкого —874 Бутырского—791 Нарвского— 954 Подольского— 477 Азовского— 482 Новгородского —250 Гвардейцев — 110 (p. 78) Кроме этого, французы захватили 160 русских пушек и 37 австрийских, а также около 300 зарядных ящиков и фур. [...] По французским данным, Великая Армия захватила в качестве трофеев 45 знамен (29 русских и 16 австрийских), и это число обычно упоминается в европейских исторических произведениях [...] По подсчетам генерала Андоленко [...] армия Наполеона захватила от 14 до 17 «полных» знамен, т.е. тех, где присутствует и древко, и полотнище. [...] Согласно рапортам, представленным императору, армия потеряла 1 305 убитых, 573 пленных (скорее, без вести пропавших) и 6 940 раненых, итого 8 818 человек. [...] ** 5 знамен Подольского полка, 3 — Азовского, 2 (5?) — Курского, 2 — Нарвского, 1 — Архангелогородского, 1 — Пермского.
- **translation**: (p. 75) M-D gave Russian irrecoverable losses of 21,000 and, with the Austrians' 5,922, an Allied total of 27,000 (Sokolov objects that the two figures are of different kinds). Colin, from the archives: 9,767 Russian and 1,686 Austrian prisoners; by regiment, Kursk 736, Galicia 874, Butyrsk 791, Narva 954, Podolia 477, Azov 482, Novgorod 250, Guards 110. (p. 78) The French took 160 Russian and 37 Austrian guns and about 300 caissons and carts. By French data, 45 colours (29 Russian, 16 Austrian), the figure usually cited; Andolenko counts 14-17 complete colours (staff and cloth). By the reports submitted to the Emperor the French lost 1,305 killed, 573 prisoners (rather missing) and 6,940 wounded, 8,818 in all. Footnote: 5 colours of Podolia, 3 of Azov, 2 (5?) of Kursk, 2 of Narva, 1 of Archangelgorod, 1 of Perm.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: the map's '1,300 dead, 7,000 wounded and 600 missing' rounds the French reports Sokolov cites (1,305 / 6,940 / 573; note 9 'Revue d'histoire, 1907, № 80, р. 409', OCR); 45 colours = 29 Russian + 16 Austrian in the French data; guns 160 Russian + 37 Austrian (197, derived); Kursk regiment's prisoners 736 (Colin, through Sokolov).
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: Settles takes the French reports' 1,305 / 6,940 / 573 as the figure; Sokolov himself on p. 79 puts the French killed and died of wounds at 1,800-1,900 and the total at 9-9.5 thousand: the French figure is disputed within the same source.
- **does not settle**: the French reports themselves (not read); Sokolov's source for the guns
- **bears on**: item 5 losses; PHASES[9].lede; TOUR[8].x; item 9 Kursk.

### C5.24 `stutterheim1806de.p10-11-20-21.allied-knowledge`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, pp. 10-11 (leaves n13, n14 crop top), p. 20 (n23, middle crop), p. 21 (n24, crop top); images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n13.jpg, https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n14.jpg, https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n23.jpg, https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n24.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Diese bey Brünn stehende Armee war 8 Divisionen stark, wovon jede ungefähr 7000 Mann in sich hielt. [...] Die Russischen Vorposten hatten ganz und gar keine Nachrichten über die Stellung und die Stärke des Feindes [...]. Nach den eingegangenen Berichten schien es indessen, daß die Französische Macht nur in gerin- / ger Anzahl bey Brünn concentrirt sey, und mehrere Generale von der combinirten Armee thaten zu Wischau den Vorschlag, sogleich offensive zu agiren. [...] Die coalisirte Armee war seit dem 19. November stärker als jene des Feindes [...] (p. 20) Man hat gesehen, wie unvollständig Herr von Kutusoff, zur Zeit da es entschieden wurde, daß er den Krieg wieder offensive führen sollte, von den Bewegungen und der Stärke des Feindes unterrichtet war. [...] Die erste Disposition zu dem Manöver, das man machte, um vorwärts zu marschiren, gründete sich also weder auf eine genaue Kenntniß von der Stellung des Feindes, noch von der Macht, mit der man zu kämpfen hatte (p. 21) Der Feind benützte diese Verzögerung: noch am Abend vor der Schlacht, wie ich schon oben erwähnt habe, langte der Marschall Bernadotte und ein Theil von dem Corps des Marschall Davoust an, und verstärkten den Kaiser Napoleon.
- **translation**: This army at Brünn was 8 divisions of about 7,000 each. The Russian outposts had no news at all of the enemy's position and strength [...]. From the reports received it seemed that the French force was concentrated at Brünn in small numbers only, and several generals proposed at Wischau to attack at once. [...] From 19 November the coalition army was stronger than the enemy's. [...] Kutuzov was incompletely informed of the enemy's movements and strength when the offensive was decided; the first disposition rested on no exact knowledge of the enemy's position or of the force to be fought. [...] The enemy used the delay: on the evening before the battle Bernadotte and part of Davout's corps arrived and reinforced Napoleon.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: the Allied staff believed the French at Brünn 'nur in geringer Anzahl' (no figure) and planned without exact knowledge of their strength; Bernadotte and part of Davout's corps arrived the evening before.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: Context: pp. 10-11 describe the moment the armies united at Wischau (c. 19 November: "Die coalisirte Armee war seit dem 19. November stärker als jene des Feindes"); p. 20's "Die erste Disposition zu dem Manöver, das man machte, um vorwärts zu marschiren" is the disposition of the advance from Olmütz (24 November), the sentence continuing "sondern war einzig und allein dem Terrain zwischen Ollmütz und Wischau angepaßt" (cut). It is not the Austerlitz disposition of 1 December. And p. 10 gives a number the researcher's searched_not_found denies: "Diese bey Brünn stehende Armee war 8 Divisionen stark, wovon jede ungefähr 7000 Mann in sich hielt" with a reserve of 15,000 under Duroc (CR3): Stutterheim's own statement, not attributed to the Allied staff.
- **does not settle**: a numerical Allied estimate (none on these pages); whether the Allies knew of the reinforcement before the battle
- **bears on**: item 6 Allied estimate; PLANS.al.assumed; COMMAND.al[0][2].

### C5.30 `md1844.p188-189.kamensky-kursk`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, pp. 188-189 (IIIF k199, k200); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$199/full/1400,/0/default.jpg, https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$200/full/1400,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 188) Дѣйствія Фанагорійскаго и Ряжскаго полковъ въ правый флангъ непріятелей удерживали довольно долго часть Сультова корпуса отъ наступленія противъ центра, (**) но не могли остановить его. [...] (**) Секретное донесеніе Кутузова Государю, отъ 28-го Декабря. (p. 189) Едва отступилъ Кутузовъ съ бригадою Графа Каменскаго, на то самое мѣсто, гдѣ она сражалась, пришелъ Графъ Ланжеронъ съ Курскимъ полкомъ. Слѣдующая причина привела его сюда: подходя къ Гольдбаху, Графъ Ланжеронъ получилъ отъ Графа Каменскаго 1-го донесеніе, что Французы подымаются на Праценскія высоты, и что въ слѣдствіе сего движенія, онъ остановилъ полки Фанагорійскій и Ряжскій, и не можетъ идти съ ними къ Сокольницу за второю колонною. Графъ Ланжеронъ усомнился было въ справедливости донесенія, но вскорѣ слыша пальбу въ бригадѣ Графа Каменскаго, поѣхалъ къ нему, желая лично удостовѣриться въ происходившемъ. Убѣдясь въ истинѣ донесенія Графа Каменскаго, Графъ Ланжеронъ повелъ на помощь ему изъ Сокольницъ Курскій полкъ, но поспѣлъ на мѣсто, гдѣ была бригада Графа Каменскаго, уже по отступленіи съ нею Кутузова. Французы атаковали Курскій полкъ, встрѣтившійся такимъ образомъ лицомъ къ лицу съ непріятелемъ, несравненно превосходнѣйшимъ въ силахъ. Курскій полкъ былъ совершенно разбитъ, потерявъ до 1600 человѣкъ.
- **translation**: (p. 188) The action of the Fanagoria and Ryazhsk regiments against the enemy's right held part of Soult's corps for a fairly long time from advancing on the centre (Kutuzov's secret report to the Emperor, 28 December), but could not stop it. (p. 189) Hardly had Kutuzov withdrawn with Kamensky's brigade when Langeron arrived with the Kursk regiment on the ground where it had fought. Nearing the Goldbach, Langeron had received from Kamensky I a report that the French were climbing the Pratzen heights and that he had therefore halted the Fanagoria and Ryazhsk regiments and could not follow the second column to Sokolnitz. Langeron doubted it, but hearing firing at the brigade rode there; convinced, he led the Kursk regiment from Sokolnitz to help, but arrived after Kutuzov had withdrawn with the brigade. The French attacked the Kursk regiment, alone against an enemy incomparably superior; it was utterly beaten, losing up to 1,600 men.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: the original's account: Kamensky halted the brigade himself and reported to Langeron; Langeron brought the Kursk regiment (no battalion count) too late; 'потерявъ до 1600 человѣкъ' (up to 1,600, in this action; the 1846 translation: 'perdit à cette seule bataille 1600 hommes').
- **does not settle**: a clock hour; the regiment's strength
- **bears on**: item 9 Kamensky; item 9 Kursk; COMMAND.al[4][0]; FORMATIONS.lang.role; EVENTS[#kursk].why.

### C5.c3 `stutterheim1806de.p10.french-at-bruenn`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, p. 10 (leaf n13); images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n13.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): die eine Reserve von 15000 Mann unter Commando des General Duroc ausmachten. Diese bey Brünn stehende Armee war 8 Divisionen stark, wovon jede ungefähr 7000 Mann in sich hielt.
- **translation**: ... which formed a reserve of 15,000 men under General Duroc. This army standing at Brünn was 8 divisions strong, each of about 7,000 men.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: An Austrian officer's 1806 statement of the French army at Brünn about 19 November: 8 divisions of about 7,000 and a reserve of 15,000 (about 71,000, derived). It refutes the researcher's "only qualitative statements" in Stutterheim pp. 10-21.

### C5.c4 `sokolov2006.p75.gross-error`

- **source**: `sokolov2006`, p. 75 (leaf n74, crop 44-66%); images: https://archive.org/download/1799-1805-.-ii/page/n74.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): результирующая цифра потерь союзников при Аустерлице, писал историк, 27 тыс. человек. [...] Нужно сразу отметить, что уже с точки зрения формальной в этом подсчете заложена грубая ошибка, так как 21 тыс. — это безвозвратные потери, т.е. убитые и пропавшие без вести, а 6 тыс. (в округленном подсчете) — это убитые, раненые и пленные австрийские солдаты.
- **translation**: the resulting figure of the Allies' losses at Austerlitz, the historian [Mikhailovsky-Danilevsky] wrote, was 27 thousand. [...] It must be noted at once that even formally this count contains a gross error, since the 21 thousand are irrecoverable losses, i.e. killed and missing, while the 6 thousand (rounded) are killed, wounded and captured Austrian soldiers.
- **grade**: C. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: Sokolov attributes an Allied total of 27,000 to M-D and rejects the sum; he reads M-D's 21,000 as killed and missing, against M-D's own words "убитыми, ранеными и плѣнными" (1844 p. 209).

### C5.c5 `sokolov2006.p76.russian-returns`

- **source**: `sokolov2006`, p. 76 (leaf n75, crop 25-47%); notes 3-7 (OCR only: Kutuzov, Sbornik dokumentov t. 2); images: https://archive.org/download/1799-1805-.-ii/page/n75.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image, part on the text layer (see the source line)): Согласно ведомости от 25 декабря 1805 г. (6 января 1806 г.) « Об убитых и без вести пропавших в 20-е число ноября воинских чинах и строевых лошадях», пехота и кавалерия русской армии потеряла при Аустерлице 19 311 человек (убитыми и без вести пропавшими). [...] при Аустерлице было убито и без вести пропало 815 русских артиллеристов. Наряду с этим имеется другая ведомость по потерям артиллерии, которая указывает на урон в 2 024 человека. [...] безвозвратных потерь русской гвардии при Аустерлице — 871 человек.
- **translation**: According to the return of 25 December 1805 (6 January 1806) "Of the ranks and troop horses killed and missing on the 20th of November", the infantry and cavalry of the Russian army lost at Austerlitz 19,311 men (killed and missing). [...] 815 Russian artillerymen killed and missing. Besides this there is another return of artillery losses giving 2,024. [...] irrecoverable losses of the Russian Guard at Austerlitz: 871.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: A Russian return dated 25 December 1805 O.S., quoted by a modern historian: 19,311 infantry and cavalry killed and missing; artillery 815 or 2,024; Guard 871 (a modern count).
- **does not settle**: the wounded and the prisoners counted separately
- **bears on**: item 5 PHASES[9].lede; TOUR[8].x.

[...] можно оценить совокупные потери союзников при Аустерлице в 30—35 тыс. человек*. [...] Союзники потеряли если не половину армии, то, как минимум, 40 % ее состава, а также две трети пушек
- **translation**: one may suppose that the Russian troops lost 25-28 thousand men. As mentioned, the Austrians lost about 6 thousand. ### C5.c7 `sokolov2006.p79.french-killed`

- **source**: `sokolov2006`, p. 79 (leaf n78, crop 3-42%); images: https://archive.org/download/1799-1805-.-ii/page/n78.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Таких (вместе со смертельно раненными) оказалось 1 537 человек. Из них 1 генерал, 109 офицеров и 1428 унтер-офицеров и солдат. [...] Великая Армия потеряла в этом сражении порядка 1800—1900 человек убитыми и умершими от ран. [...] французская армия потеряла при Аустерлице около 9—9,5 тыс. человек убитыми, ранеными и пропавшими без вести. - **translation**: Such (with the mortally wounded) were 1,537: 1 general, 109 officers and 1,428 NCOs and men. [...] the Grande Armée lost in this battle some 1,800-1,900 killed and died of wounds. [...] the French army lost at Austerlitz about 9-9.5 thousand killed, wounded and missing. One general (Valhubert) died of wounds; one battalion eagle (of the 4th of the line) was lost.

### C5.c11 `stutterheim1806de.p64.kamensky-order`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, p. 64 (leaf n67); register §2.6 (second reading); images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n67.jpg.

### C5.c13 `materialien1806.p114-119.official-report-losses`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, p. 114 (leaf n123, crop top 45%) and p. 119 (leaf n128, crop lower 65%; page number on a header crop); images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n123_w1200.jpg, https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n128_w1200.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 114) Ein officieller, ungedruckter Bericht verdient hier mit der nun folgenden Erzählung des Vfs. verglichen zu werden [...] „Die Kaiserl. Russischen Garden unter dem Commando des Großfürsten Constantin, 10,000 Mann stark, (incl. der noch einen Marsch zurück befindlichen, 3 Bataillons Grenadier und 5 Escadrons Chevalier-Garde) [...] (p. 119) „Der Verlust der kaiserl. östreichischen und russischen Armee war ohngefähr / 3 bis 4000 Todte und Blessirte / 8 bis 10000 Gefangene / 80 Piecen verschiednen Calibers. / „Erobert wurde ein einziger Adler und gegen 200 Gefangene gemacht. — Der Feind verlor gewiß an Todten 6 bis 8000 Mann, in Brünn lagen nach der Bataille 14,000 Blessirte und kranke Franzosen.“
- **translation**: (p. 114) An official, unprinted report deserves to be compared here with the author's narrative that follows [...] "The Imperial Russian Guards under the Grand Duke Constantine, 10,000 strong (including 3 grenadier battalions and 5 squadrons of the Chevalier Guard still a march behind) [...] (p. 119) "The loss of the Imperial Austrian and Russian army was about 3,000 to 4,000 dead and wounded, 8,000 to 10,000 prisoners, 80 pieces of various calibres. "One eagle was taken and about 200 prisoners made. The enemy certainly lost 6,000 to 8,000 dead; in Brünn after the battle lay 14,000 wounded and sick Frenchmen."
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: An unprinted official report (author and date not stated) printed in 1806: Allied losses 3-4,000 killed and wounded, 8-10,000 prisoners, 80 guns; one French eagle taken (as Sokolov p. 79: the 4e de ligne's); French dead 6-8,000 (a claim). The lowest Allied figures read; missed by the researcher.
- **does not settle**: its author (presumably Allied, from its "the enemy"); its date
- **bears on**: item 5 PHASES[9].lede; TOUR[8].x.
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

### C5.c14 `materialien1806.p121.bulletin-twenty-guns`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, p. 121 (leaf n130); the 30th Bulletin no. 9541 in Correspondance t. XI pp. 449-452 (read by attempt 1, page of this line not re-located); images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n130_w1200.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Dieser Umstand ward in den französischen officiellen Berichten anfänglich mit sehr hohen Farben geschildert, inzwischen man ihn von andrer Seite geradezu abläugnete. So steht in dem 30sten Bulletin der großen Armee: „Le corps de l'ennemi, qui avoit été cerné et chassé de toutes ses hauteurs,“ [...] „se trouvoit dans un bas-fond et acculé à un lac. L' Empereur s'y porta avec vingt pièces de canon. [...] vingt mille hommes se jetant dans l'eau et se noyant dans les lacs“.
- **translation**: This circumstance was at first painted in very bright colours in the French official reports, while from another side it was flatly denied. Thus the 30th Bulletin: "The enemy corps, surrounded and driven from all its heights, found itself in a hollow with its back to a lake. The Emperor went there with twenty guns [...] twenty thousand men throwing themselves into the water and drowning in the lakes."
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: The Bulletin's twenty guns at the meres (against Thiébault's and Sokolov's 24), reprinted 1806; the 1806 editor records the drowning story as denied by the other side (with Schönhals p. 169).
- **does not settle**: the number of guns
- **bears on**: item 4 heightguns.strengthNote; c_gd.strengthNote; item 5.
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

How: none dated 2 December; Sokolov states that no returns of the morning of 2 December exist (p. 193).
- `C5.n10`: Kursk and 'two battalions' in Thiébault and Stutterheim. Where: Thiébault t. III text layer pp. 449-504; Stutterheim German hOCR. How: grep -i 'koursk|kursk|kourski' 0 hits in both; 'zwey Bataillon' hits in Stutterheim concern Wischau, Telnitz and Sokolnitz garrisons, not Kursk.
- `C5.n11`: the words 'used to 1806' or a 1805 date for the Austrian colours. Where: Dolleczek 1896 (text layer, whole) and pp. 158-160 on images. How: grep '1804|1805|1806': the only 'bis 1806' concerns cavalry standards made in the older way; '1804' concerns the navy flag.

- Russkaya Starina 1895 (Langeron's notes, the attempt-1 downloads russkajastarinag261895t83kn46apr27, t84kn1012o86): searched in the text layer only (Курск, Ланжерон, Аустерлиц): the hits are not on Kursk at Austerlitz; pages not read on the images; the 1895 Langeron text located (t. 83, near line 21806) is a letter, not the battle.

- Oesterreichische Militair-Zeitschrift 1822 p. 229 (Austrian 5,922): not searched for a scan in this attempt.
- Duffy 1977, Smith 1998 (the 'Duffy/Smith' figures and Gladkov): modern works in copyright: cited, not read (brief).
- Stutterheim 1806 French original: Gallica: HTTP 403.

**The source skeptic's summary** (C5; its counts and "CRn" numbers are its working list's: the verdicts above are the record): All 32 readings judged (30 in attempt 1, Czartoryski and Dolleczek in attempt 2); none rejected: every quotation was found on its cited page, with corrections to locators, settles and overreach as given. Strength is not text only: app.js subUnits() draws strength/1150 battalions (cap 7) or strength/950 squadrons (cap 6), the figure kit and standards follow the drawn battalions, counters size and the map layer ranks by strength. Proposals 1, 2 and 3 must say so; only proposal 2 (sthilaire 6 to 4, vandamme 6 to 7) changes drawn battalions. Schönhals's order of battle (pp. 177-178) is undated by his own word (p. 156) and has misprints (Kienmayer Summa 2440 vs recap 2940; Guards 4380 vs 9380, lines 9,730). The Materialien's unprinted official report (pp. 113-121) is a source not used by the researcher: losses (CR14) and the Russian Guard at 10,000 including 3 grenadier battalions and 5 Chevalier Guard squadrons still a march behind (bears on the Russian Guard, outside this cluster). Sokolov p. 187: Legrand fought under Davout "после прибытия маршала на поле боя около 9 часов утра" (an hour for another cluster). The 6B record grades Thiébault's Varé statement A; under this brief a memoir published 1894 is B. Downloads in work/C5-skeptic/img (attempt 1) and img/stut2, img/mat2, img/mat3, crops (attempt 2); OCR text layers in ocr/ and ocr2/ used only to find pages.

**The researcher's notes** (C5): Attempt 2 of C5. The first attempt left downloads and a transcript digest but no recorded readings; every quotation here was read on its page image in this attempt. Contradictions inside the map noticed: (1) legrand.staff 'Merle · Féry · Levasseur': Féry/Ferey is Vandamme's brigadier in Alombert (28 Oct) and Sokolov (22 Nov); Legrand's brigades are Merle and Levasseur (Sokolov) or Levasseur, Merle, Brouard (Alombert). (2) heightguns 'Soult's corps had 35 guns': Sokolov's Rhine-crossing table gives IV Corps 36 guns (September). (3) M-D 1846 leaf offsets: pp. 224-226 are n246-n248 (page + 22), not page + 24. (4) Schönhals p. 169's '15.000' Russian prisoners reads '16.000' in the text layer (the image is the reading); Thiébault p. 465 '10 colonels, 150 caissons' read '40', '450' in the text layer. Sokolov's French tables: grade B for the 22 Nov figures (he names the archive, SHD C2 606; the returns themselves not read), C for his 2 Dec estimates. His Allied tables give no source on the pages read: C. Kursk timing: no source read gives a clock hour for the Kursk regiment's action; the kursk event's t:630 (app narrative) is unaffected. Thiébault p. 452 (OCR only) has the Allies imagining 'nos 75,000 à 80,000 hommes aux prises avec leurs 134,000' (OCR '13-4,000'), which concerns the coalition's campaign plan in Germany, not Austerlitz; p. 460 (register §4.3 neighbourhood) has 'avec 75,000 hommes contre 104,000' as Thiébault's own figures. Not read on the images; not used. Losses lede: the current French figures (1,300 / 7,000 / 600) match the reports Sokolov cites (1,305 / 6,940 / 573); TOUR[8]'s 'near nine thousand' matches their sum 8,818.

## C6: the language left for step 4, Blasowitz (question 158), Turas, the Posoritz post house and Kologrivov

### C6.1 `thiebault3_1894.p474.turas-comparable`

- **source**: `thiebault3_1894`, t. III, p. 474, leaf n485; images: https://archive.org/download/mmoires03thieuoft/page/n485_w1200.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): lorsque la brigade Kamenski, renforcée par les régiments de Fanagorski et Riajski, eut rétabli le combat contre nous, elle s'était trouvée rejointe par ces débris reformés. Alors elle avait repris l'offensive avec acharnement, et, malgré notre précédent succès, notre position ne laissait pas d'être encore très critique. En face de nous et marchant sur nous, nous voyions arriver quatre ou cinq fois plus d'ennemis que nous n'étions de combattants, et sur notre droite et jusqu'à Turas, c'est-à-dire nous enveloppant aux trois quarts, cinquante-cinq bataillons russes des 1re, 2e et 3e colonnes et 3 ou 4,000 chevaux. Jamais Morand et moi (et la dernière fois au Palais-Royal, peu de mois avant sa mort), nous ne nous sommes revus sans rappeler cette situation : « J'ai bien fait la guerre, me disait-il; j'ai eu le bonheur de me trouver, depuis l'armée d'Égypte, à toutes les grandes batailles livrées par l'Empereur, mais je n'ai rien vu de comparable à cette position. » [...] Enfin, après une mêlée effroyable, une mêlée de plus de vingt minutes, nous venions d'obtenir un temps d'arrêt;
- **translation**: when Kamensky's brigade, reinforced by the Fanagorski and Riajski regiments, had restored the fight against us, it had been joined by these re-formed remnants. Then it had resumed the offensive with fury, and despite our earlier success our position was still very critical. In front of us and marching on us we saw coming four or five times more enemies than we had fighting men, and on our right as far as Turas, enveloping us on three sides, fifty-five Russian battalions of the 1st, 2nd and 3rd columns and 3 or 4,000 horse. Never did Morand and I meet again (the last time at the Palais-Royal, a few months before his death) without recalling this situation: 'I have made war well,' he would tell me; 'I have had the luck to be, since the army of Egypt, at all the great battles the Emperor fought, but I have seen nothing comparable to that position.' [...] At last, after a frightful mêlée of more than twenty minutes, we had just won a pause;
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: An attributable judgement of the fight on the Pratzeberg: Morand, in Thiébault, 'je n'ai rien vu de comparable à cette position'; Thiébault's own 'quatre ou cinq fois plus d'ennemis'; Russian battalions on his right 'jusqu'à Turas'.
- **does not settle**: That it was the day's hardest fighting (no comparison with Telnitz or any other sector); the numbers (his own estimate).
- **bears on**: PHASES[4].lede; FEATURES[#pratzeberg].story; world.js VILLAGES Turas.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 1 freshly downloaded image: quotation exact, accept. p. 474 confirmed on the header; quotation exact with its cut (superscripts 1re/2e/3e as printed). Morand's comparison is with 'toutes les grandes batailles livrées par l'Empereur', not with other sectors of Austerlitz, as 'does_not_settle' says. Not read by a person.

### C6.2 `thiebault3_1894.p475-476.pouzet`

- **source**: `thiebault3_1894`, t. III, pp. 475-476, leaves n486-n487; images: https://archive.org/download/mmoires03thieuoft/page/n486_w1200.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoires03thieuoft/page/n487_w1200.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): le feu le plus vif et presque à bout portant avait de nouveau succédé à la baïonnette, lorsque le général Saint-Hilaire réunit à la hâte Morand et moi, et nous dit : « Ceci devient intenable, et je vous propose, messieurs, de prendre en arrière de nous une position que nous pussions défendre. » Il avait à peine proféré ce dernier mot que le colonel Pouzet (1), commandant le 10e léger [...] s'écria : « Nous retirer, mon général... Si nous faisons un pas en arrière, nous sommes perdus. Nous n'avons qu'un moyen de sortir d'ici avec honneur, c'est de foncer tête baissée sur tout ce qui nous fera face, et, par-dessus tout, de ne pas laisser à nos ennemis le temps de nous compter. » [...] Par ce prodige de ténacité, après une demi-heure, ayant perdu le colonel Mazas et mon pauvre Richebourg (2), [...] ayant un grand nombre de blessés et parmi eux le / général Saint-Hilaire et le colonel Houdar de Lamotte, après avoir eu deux chevaux tués sous moi, nous pûmes exécuter enfin, à notre tour, une charge décisive en ce qu'elle rompit les lignes de l'ennemi, nous fit enlever trois nouvelles batteries [...] et nous rendit irrévocablement maîtres de cette partie des hauteurs de Pratzen (1). [...] J'ai dit qu'au moment de cette terrible lutte nous étions sans nouvelles du quartier général
- **translation**: the hottest fire, almost point-blank, had again followed the bayonet when General Saint-Hilaire hurriedly gathered Morand and me and said: 'This is becoming untenable, and I propose, gentlemen, that we take up a position behind us that we could defend.' He had hardly spoken when Colonel Pouzet, commanding the 10th light, cried: 'Retire, General... If we take one step back we are lost. We have only one way out of here with honour: to charge headlong at everything in front of us and, above all, not to leave our enemies time to count us.' [...] By this prodigy of tenacity, after half an hour, having lost Colonel Mazas and my poor Richebourg [...] with many wounded, among them General Saint-Hilaire and Colonel Houdar de Lamotte, after two horses had been killed under me, we could at last make in our turn a charge, decisive in that it broke the enemy's lines, took three more batteries [...] and made us irrevocably masters of that part of the Pratzen heights. [...] at the moment of this terrible struggle we were without news from headquarters
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The source of FEATURES[#pratzeberg].story's 'proposed withdrawing to better ground': Thiébault p. 475; in his telling Pouzet's objection decided it, the brigades held half an hour and then made their own charge (p. 476); he calls it 'cette terrible lutte'. He names no Levasseur in this crisis.
- **does not settle**: Levasseur's arrival (Schönhals p. 161); a clock hour; a ranking against other sectors. The anecdote is a memoir anecdote Thiébault says he told 'plus de cent fois' (note 1, p. 475).
- **bears on**: FEATURES[#pratzeberg].story; PHASES[4].lede.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 2 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept. pp. 475 (header 'LE COLONEL POUZET') and 476 confirmed; quotation exact with cuts; note (1) p. 475 'J'ai cité plus de cent fois le trait' read. No Levasseur in Thiébault's crisis on these pages. Not read by a person.

### C6.3 `schoenhals1873.p161.levasseur`

- **source**: `schoenhals1873`, p. 161, leaf n165; images: https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n165_w1200.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): St. Hilaire liess seinen Tirailleurs die rechte Flügel-Brigade folgen, während die andere deren Rücken deckte. Gegen die Brigade Kamenskoi, — welche General Langeron, der des Feindes Vordringen gegen die Höhe bemerkte, der vierten Colonne zu Hilfe sandte, — liess Soult die in Reserve gehaltene Brigade Levasseur; durch eine Directions-Veränderung rechts, nach Zurückwerfung der Russen, St. Hilaire's rechten Flügel unterstützen, welcher General, obgleich verwundet, den Befehl fortbehielt und später mit seiner ganzen Division eine Frontveränderung rechts vornahm,
- **translation**: St. Hilaire had the right-wing brigade follow his skirmishers while the other covered its rear. Against Kamensky's brigade, which General Langeron, noticing the enemy's advance on the height, sent to help the fourth column, Soult had Levasseur's brigade, held in reserve, support St. Hilaire's right wing by a change of direction to the right after the Russians had been thrown back; this general, though wounded, kept the command and later wheeled his whole division to the right,
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: An archive-based history for Levasseur's brigade supporting Saint-Hilaire's right against Kamensky's brigade.
- **does not settle**: That Levasseur's arrival 'settled' the crisis (Schönhals does not say so); an hour; who sent Kamensky (disputed: M-D 1844 p. 187, register §6.5).
- **bears on**: FEATURES[#pratzeberg].story; PHASES[4].lede.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 1 freshly downloaded image: quotation exact, accept. p. 161 confirmed; exact. The leaf's signature line reads 'Österr. militär. Zeitschrift. 1874. (Feldzug 1805.)': the text was printed in the ÖMZ; the registered key's date is kept as registered. Schönhals does not say Levasseur 'settled' anything. Not read by a person.

### C6.4 `stutterheim1806de.p50-51.telnitz-moerderisch`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, pp. 50-51, leaves n53-n54; images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n53_w1200.jpg, https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n54_w1200.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 50) das Musketen-Feuer fing an, und wurde auf diesem Puncte sehr lebhaft. Die Franzosen vertheidigten sich mit Wuth, und die Oesterreicher, denen man ein Bataillon zur Unterstützung nachgeschickt hatte, griffen mit Heftigkeit an. [...] diese benutzten den Vortheil des Terrains, das aus Weingärten und Gräben rings um das Dorf bestand [...] Das zweyte Bataillon von Szeckler-Infanterie war vorgerückt, um das erste zu verstärken, welches die Anhöhe angriff, und schon über die Hälfte seiner Mannschaft verloren hatte. (p. 51) Der Feind hatte in Tellnitz und in den Weingärten um das Dorf das dritte Linien-Regiment und zwey Bataillonen Plänkler. Diese Truppen vertheidigten ihren Posten mit Muth. [...] Jetzt begann ein sehr mörderisches Musketen-Feuer. Die Natur selbst hatte um dieses Dorf eine Art von Verschanzung formirt, denn die Weingärten waren mit einem breiten Graben umgeben, worin sich die Franzosen postirt hatten [...] Das Szeckler-Infanterie-Regiment focht wüthend, es hatte zwey Drittheile an Todten und Verwundeten. Dieses Gefecht dauerte schon über eine Stunde, und noch sah man die Spitze der ersten Colonne nicht ankommen
- **the source skeptic's correction to the quotation**: (p. 50) das Musketen-Feuer fing an, uud wurde auf diesem Puncte sehr lebhaft. [rest as given]
- **translation**: the musket fire began and became very lively at this point. The French defended themselves with fury, and the Austrians, to whom a battalion had been sent in support, attacked with violence. [...] these used the advantage of the ground, which consisted of vineyards and ditches all round the village [...] The second Szekler battalion had come forward to reinforce the first, which attacked the height and had already lost over half its men. The enemy had in Telnitz and in the vineyards round the village the third line regiment and two battalions of skirmishers. These troops defended their post with courage. [...] Now a very murderous musket fire began. Nature itself had formed a kind of entrenchment round this village, for the vineyards were surrounded by a broad ditch in which the French had posted themselves [...] The Szekler infantry regiment fought furiously; it had two thirds dead and wounded. This fight had already lasted over an hour and still the head of the first column was not seen arriving
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Stutterheim's words for Telnitz, attributable: 'sehr mörderisches Musketen-Feuer', the French 'mit Wuth', the Szeklers 'wüthend' with two thirds dead and wounded; the vineyards' ditch 'eine Art von Verschanzung'.
- **does not settle**: A ranking: the same author uses as strong or stronger words elsewhere (p. 63 'äußerst mörderisch' on the Pratzen; p. 68 'sehr hartnäckig' and 'äußerst lebhafte und mörderische Kanonade' at Blasowitz), so 'among the hardest of the day' is not his comparison.
- **bears on**: PHASES[1].events[0]; FEATURES[#telnitz].story; FEATURES[#telnitz].facts.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 2 freshly downloaded images: quotation minor differences, accept with corrections. pp. 50-51 confirmed. p. 50 prints the misprint 'uud wurde' where the quotation writes 'und wurde'; otherwise exact. Not read by a person.

### C6.6 `stutterheim1806de.p68.blasowitz-hartnaeckig`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, p. 68, leaf n71 (second reading of step-2 §2.5's p. 68, extended); images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n71_w1000.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): war der Großfürst Constantin ebenfalls in ein sehr hartnäckiges Gefecht verwickelt. Das Dorf Blasowitz, welches er, wie schon erwähnt worden, hatte besetzen lassen, wurde von den Truppen des Corps des Marschall Bernadotte angegriffen und genommen. Der Großfürst wollte den Feind aufhalten, verließ die dominirenden Anhöhen, worauf er stand, und rückte in Linie gegen die feindlichen Massen an. Gleich anfangs entstand ein sehr lebhaftes Musketen-Feuer; die Französischen Plänkler, welche vor ihren Massen standen, machten ihr Terrain streitig: endlich wurden sie durch einen Angriff mit dem Bajonette, den der Großfürst machen ließ, zum Rückzug gezwungen; hierauf begann auf diesem Puncte eine äußerst lebhafte und mörderische Kanonade. Das Kartätschenfeuer richtete große Verwüstungen an; aber in dem Augenblicke, da sich dieser Prinz den damahls schon in Linie stehenden Franzosen näherte, hieb die Cavallerie der Garden, welche von dem Marschall Bessieres commandirt war, und in den Zwischenräumen der Infanterie stand, in die Russische Linie ein, die somit, weil sie ohne Unterstützung war, nach einer tapferen Gegenwehr weichen mußte.
- **translation**: the Grand Duke Constantine was likewise engaged in a very stubborn fight. The village of Blasowitz, which he had had occupied, as already mentioned, was attacked and taken by the troops of Marshal Bernadotte's corps. The Grand Duke, wishing to stop the enemy, left the commanding heights on which he stood and advanced in line against the enemy masses. At once a very lively musket fire arose; the French skirmishers in front of their masses disputed the ground; at last they were forced back by a bayonet attack the Grand Duke had made; then a most lively and murderous cannonade began at this point. The canister did great havoc; but at the moment this prince approached the French, already in line, the Guard cavalry under Marshal Bessières, standing in the intervals of the infantry, cut into the Russian line, which, without support, had to give way after a brave resistance.
- **grade**: A. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: In Stutterheim: the defender of Blasowitz is the Grand Duke's Guard (the occupier, p. 56); the attackers Bernadotte's corps; the village falls BEFORE the Russian Guard's advance in line and its rout by Bessières's Guard cavalry; Bagration is not named in this fight.
- **does not settle**: A clock hour; which I Corps division.
- **bears on**: EVENTS[#blasowitz]; FEATURES[#blasowitz].facts; ANALYSIS[#north].
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 1 freshly downloaded image: quotation exact, accept. p. 68 confirmed; exact; the order (village taken, then the Grand Duke leaves the heights) is as printed. The occupation by the Guard is also on p. 56 (read here: 'Der Großfürst ließ eilig das Dorf Blasowitz durch das Jäger-Bataillon der Garde besetzen'). Not read by a person.

### C6.7 `stutterheim1806de.p49.seven`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, p. 49, leaf n52 (second reading of step-2 §2.2, extended); images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n52_w1000.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Um 7 Uhr Morgens setzte sich die combinirte Armee in Bewegung, und verließ die Anhöhen bey Pratzen, um auf ihre bestimmten Puncte vorzurücken. Jede der 4 Infanterie-Colonnen konnte vom Feinde beobachtet werden, dem es nicht entging, daß die Richtung ihres Marsches große Zwischenräume zwischen denselben verursachte, so wie sich die Spitzen dieser Colonnen dem Thale bey Tellnitz, Sokolnitz und Kobelnitz näherten. Das Gefecht fing auf dem linken Flügel der Alliirten an. Das Corps des General Kienmayer, welches vorwärts von Aujest stand, wie wir gesehen haben, war dem Feinde am nächsten, und bestimmt durch das Defilee von Tellnitz zu brechen, um für die erste Colonne reine Bahn zu machen. [...] zwischen 7 und 8 Uhr rückten einige Escadronen Husaren vor, um den Feind zu recognosciren.
- **translation**: At 7 in the morning the combined army set itself in motion and left the heights by Pratzen to advance on its appointed points. Each of the 4 infantry columns could be observed by the enemy, who did not miss that the direction of their march opened great gaps between them as the heads of the columns approached the valley by Tellnitz, Sokolnitz and Kobelnitz. The fighting began on the Allied left. Kienmayer's corps, standing in front of Aujest, was nearest the enemy and was to break through the Tellnitz defile to clear the way for the first column. [...] between 7 and 8 some squadrons of hussars came forward to reconnoitre the enemy.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Stutterheim's 7 o'clock for the whole army leaving the Pratzen heights toward the villages; Kienmayer was to clear Telnitz for the I Column.
- **does not settle**: When the I Column itself reached Telnitz (p. 51: over an hour into Kienmayer's fight its head was still not seen).
- **bears on**: TOUR[3].x; PHASES[1].events; FEATURES[#telnitz].why[1].

### C6.8 `mikhailovsky1844.p177-178.eighth-hour-telnitz`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, pp. 177-178, IIIF k188-k189 (second reading of step-2 §6.3, extended to p. 178); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$188/full/900,/0/default.jpg, https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$189/full/900,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 177) Ноября 20-го, въ 8-мъ часу утра, первыя три колонны выступили съ ночлега: (*) Дохтуровъ пошелъ къ Тельницу; на правомъ крылѣ его слѣдовалъ Графъ Ланжеронъ; Пржибышевскій спускался отъ Працена къ Сокольницу. (p. 178) Первые ружейные выстрѣлы раздались на нашемъ лѣвомъ крылѣ, гдѣ ближе всѣхъ къ Французамъ, у Аугеста, находился отрядъ Кинмейера [...] Селеніе Тельницъ, окруженное виноградниками и рвами, давало непріятелю сильную оборону. [...] Кинмейеръ два раза врывался въ селеніе, но не могъ утвердиться въ немъ. Черезъ часъ появился Графъ Буксгевденъ съ колонною Дохтурова [...] Кинмейеръ и Левизъ атаковали и взяли Тельницъ.
- **translation**: On 20 November, in the eighth hour of the morning [between seven and eight], the first three columns set out from their bivouac: Dokhturov went toward Telnitz; on his right followed Count Langeron; Przybyszewski was descending from Pratzen toward Sokolnitz. The first musket shots rang out on our left wing, where Kienmayer's detachment, by Augezd, was nearest the French [...] The village of Telnitz, surrounded by vineyards and ditches, gave the enemy a strong defence. [...] Kienmayer twice broke into the village but could not hold it. An hour later Count Buxhowden appeared with Dokhturov's column [...] Kienmayer and Lewis attacked and took Telnitz.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: M-D: the three columns set out between seven and eight; the I Column arrived at Telnitz an hour after Kienmayer's first attacks; Telnitz 'давало непріятелю сильную оборону' (a strong defence).
- **does not settle**: The clock's basis; any ranking of the day's fighting.
- **bears on**: TOUR[3].x; PHASES[1].events[0]; FEATURES[#telnitz].story; FEATURES[#sokolnitz].why[0].

### C6.10 `stutterheim1806de.p58.p70.posthaus`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, p. 58, leaf n61 (the name read on a crop of the full-size image); p. 70, leaf n73; images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n61_w1000.jpg, https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n61.jpg, https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n73_w1000.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 58) Dieser General war endlich von dem Pesorsitzer Posthause vorgerückt, um sich dem linken Flügel des Marschall Lannes, der sich an Covalowitz lehnte, entgegen zu stellen; der Fürst Bagration hatte die Dörfer Krug und Holubitz durch den General Ulanius mit 3 Bataillonen Jäger besetzen lassen. (p. 70) Der Fürst Bagration war während dieser Gefechte auf seiner linken Seite, wie schon gesagt, von dem Posorsitzer-Posthause vorwärts gerückt, und hatte die Anhöhen bey Dwaroschna zu besetzen gesucht; der General-Lieutenant Uwarow stand mit seiner Cavallerie auf dem rechten Flügel desselben bey Holubitz, welches Dorf, wie auch das Dorf Krug, besetzt war
- **translation**: (p. 58) This general [Bagration] had at last advanced from the Posoritz post house to face the left wing of Marshal Lannes, which rested on Kowalowitz; Bagration had had the villages of Krug and Holubitz occupied by General Ulanius with 3 jäger battalions. (p. 70) During these fights on his left Prince Bagration had, as already said, advanced from the Posoritz post house and tried to occupy the heights by Dwaroschna; Lieutenant-General Uvarov stood with his cavalry on its right wing by Holubitz, which village, like the village of Krug, was occupied
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.

### C6.11 `stutterheim1806de.p71.bagration-posorsitz`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, p. 71, leaf n74; images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n74_w1000.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Nachdem sich der Fürst Bagration lange bey Posorsitz gehalten hatte, zog er sich auf die Anhöhen bey Rausnitz, zu eben der Zeit, da die Russischen Garden die Anhöhen hinter Blasowitz verlassen hatten, und Abends erhielt er Befehl, nach Austerlitz zu marschiren, wodurch die Straße nach Wischau gänzlich bloßgegeben wurde [...] Der General-Lieutenant Uwarow deckte diesen Rückzug mit seiner Cavallerie. Um 6 Uhr Abends stellte sich der Fürst Bagration hinter Austerlitz
- **translation**: After Prince Bagration had long held on at Posorsitz, he withdrew onto the heights by Rausnitz, at the very time the Russian Guards had left the heights behind Blasowitz, and in the evening received orders to march to Austerlitz, by which the road to Wischau was left wholly uncovered [...] Lieutenant-General Uvarov covered this retreat with his cavalry. At 6 in the evening Prince Bagration took post behind Austerlitz
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.

- **does not settle**: That Blasowitz's fall levered Bagration off the army (no causal word); a clock hour for Blasowitz.
