### C6.12 `schoenhals1873.p163-165.blaziowitz-kruch-caffarelli`

- **source**: `schoenhals1873`, pp. 163-165, leaves n167-n169; images: https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n167_w1100.jpg, https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n168_w1100.jpg, https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n169_w1100.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 163) Kaum auf der Höhe zwischen Kruch und Blaziowitz erschienen, ward er von Kellermann's leichter Reiterei und den Tirailleurs der Division Rivaud angefallen und liess deshalb in Eile das Dorf Blaziowitz von den Garde-Jägern besetzen. In diesem Augenblick langte auch die Colonne des Fürsten Liechtenstein bei Blaziowitz an; er entsendete schnell das Huszaren-Regiment Elisabethgrod unter dem General Uwarow gegen Kruch, um die durch das rasche Vordringen der feindlichen Reiterei bedrohte linke Flanke des Fürsten Bagration zu decken. [...] (p. 164) Während dieser Reiter-Gefechte ward auch der Grossfürst in ein allgemeines Gefecht verwickelt. Der Feind hatte Blaziowitz angegriffen und das Garde-Jäger-Bataillon, welches es vertheidigte, herausgeworfen. Der Grossfürst rückte von der Höhe herab dem Feind entgegen, um ihm das verlorene Terrain wieder zu entreissen. [...] In vollkommener Ordnung setzte dieses ausgezeichnete Corps seinen Rückzug nach / (p. 165) Křenowitz fort, wo es, gedeckt von den österreichischen Cürassieren unter Fürst Hohenlohe, das Defilé übersetzte, nachdem es in diesem hartnäckigen Kampfe grossen Verlust an Todten und Verwundeten, doch nur sehr geringen an Gefangenen erlitten hatte. [...] Bagration hatte die Dörfer Hollubitz und Kruch durch 3 Bataillone des 6. Jäger-Regiments und durch Kosaken unter General Ulanius besetzt. [...] Mehrere Stunden dauerte nun schon der Kampf ohne Entscheidung, als gegen Mittag, nachdem die Höhen von Pratze verloren, und die 5. Colonne nebst den Garden zum Weichen gebracht worden waren, die feindliche Division Caffarelli in Colonnen gegen den linken Flügel Bagration's vordrang.
- **translation**: (p. 163) Hardly had he [the Grand Duke] appeared on the height between Kruch and Blaziowitz when he was attacked by Kellermann's light horse and the skirmishers of Rivaud's division, and so in haste had the village of Blaziowitz occupied by the Guard jägers. At that moment Liechtenstein's column too arrived at Blaziowitz; he quickly sent the Elisabethgrad hussars under General Uvarov toward Kruch to cover Bagration's left flank [...] (p. 164) During these cavalry fights the Grand Duke too became engaged in a general fight. The enemy had attacked Blaziowitz and thrown out the Guard jäger battalion that defended it. The Grand Duke came down from the height to retake the lost ground. [...] In perfect order this distinguished corps continued its retreat to Křenowitz, where, covered by the Austrian cuirassiers under Prince Hohenlohe, it crossed the defile, having suffered great loss in dead and wounded in this stubborn fight but very little in prisoners. [...] Bagration had occupied the villages of Hollubitz and Kruch with 3 battalions of the 6th jäger regiment and Cossacks under General Ulanius. [...] The fight had lasted several hours without decision when, toward noon, after the heights of Pratze had been lost and the 5th column and the Guards driven back, the enemy division Caffarelli advanced in columns against Bagration's left wing.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: (1) Blasowitz's defenders: the Russian Guard's jäger battalion; its attackers unnamed ('der Feind'; Rivaud's skirmishers and Kellermann's horse engaged on the height); (2) the village falls before the Grand Duke's counter-attack; (3) Caffarelli's attack on BAGRATION comes 'gegen Mittag', after the Guard's repulse: a separate action; (4) the Russian Guard retreated 'in vollkommener Ordnung' (against FORMATIONS.bag.role's 'The only'); (5) Kruh ('Kruch') a village beside Blasowitz and Hollubitz.
- **does not settle**: A clock hour for Blasowitz; the French corps that took it.
- **bears on**: EVENTS[#blasowitz]; FEATURES[#blasowitz].facts; FORMATIONS.bag.role; world.js VILLAGES Krug.

Regiments zusammen und nahm seinen Adler'; Napoleon 'seiner Reserve voraus, bei Blaziowitz'. p. 165: 'Der rechts im Posoržitzer-Thale aufgestellte General Czaplitz'. Not read by a person.

### C6.13 `schoenhals1873.p166.posthaus-suchet`

- **source**: `schoenhals1873`, p. 166, leaf n170; images: https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n170_w1100.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): General Ulanius ward nach tapferer Gegenwehr ebenso aus Kruch und Hollubitz geworfen. Ein Angriff en muraille der Cürassier-Division d'Hautpoult brachte die russischen Bataillone in Unordnung, in der sie sich gegen das Thal hinter dem Posthause von Posoržitz zurückzogen [...] Marschall Lannes, die Trennung der russischen Infanterie benützend, liess die Division Suchet und einen Theil der Caffarelli's ihren rechten Flügel vornehmen, und durchbrach damit die russische Linie abermals [...] Indessen gelang es dem Fürsten Bagration, seine zerstreuten Bataillone auf der rechts von der Brünner Strasse zwischen Rausnitz und dem Posoržitzer Posthause liegenden Anhöhe wieder einigermassen zu sammeln, als ein erneuerter Angriff Suchet's das Corps gänzlich zu zerstreuen drohte. [...] Nun gewann das vom Feinde nicht weiter angegriffene Corps Zeit, sich mehr zu ordnen, und trat Abends gegen 6 Uhr seinen Rückzug, gedeckt von der Reiterei unter Uworow, über Alt-Rausnitz gegen Austerlitz an.
- **translation**: General Ulanius was likewise thrown out of Kruch and Hollubitz after a brave resistance. A charge 'en muraille' by d'Hautpoul's cuirassier division threw the Russian battalions into disorder, in which they fell back toward the valley behind the post house of Posořitz [...] Marshal Lannes, using the separation of the Russian infantry, had Suchet's division and part of Caffarelli's bring forward their right wing and broke the Russian line again [...] Meanwhile Bagration managed to gather his scattered battalions somewhat on the height right of the Brünn road between Rausnitz and the Posořitz post house, when a renewed attack by Suchet threatened to scatter the corps completely. [...] The corps, no longer attacked, gained time to reorder itself, and toward 6 in the evening began its retreat, covered by Uvarov's cavalry, by Alt-Rausnitz toward Austerlitz.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Schönhals: 'das Posthaus von Posoržitz' on the Brünn road with Rausnitz beyond it; Bagration's corps in disorder at points and re-formed before retreating toward six; Suchet's division and part of Caffarelli's broke Bagration's line.
- **does not settle**: The post house's exact place; 'in good order' for the day as a whole.

### C6.14 `thiebault3_1894.p462.lannes-bagration-blazowitz`

- **source**: `thiebault3_1894`, t. III, p. 462, leaf n473; p. 463 note (1), leaf n474 (second reading of step-2 §4.6, extended); images: https://archive.org/download/mmoires03thieuoft/page/n473_w1100.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoires03thieuoft/page/n474_w1100.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): A notre extrême gauche, et en avant du Santon, le maréchal Lannes avait brillamment combattu le corps du prince Bagration; il l'avait chassé de Pozorzitz et forcé de se rejeter vers Holubitz, ainsi que Koutousow en convient lui-même dans son rapport. A la droite du maréchal Lannes, notre cavalerie, commandée par Murat, avait enlevé plusieurs batteries à l'ennemi et, malgré cet avantage, ne fit cependant que lutter de succès et de revers avec l'immense cavalerie des alliés, jusqu'au moment où le général Kellermann décida la victoire par une heureuse et destructive embuscade. La 1re division du cinquième corps avait disputé Blazowitz à la réserve du grand-duc Constantin, qu'elle abîma dans plusieurs chocs successifs et à laquelle elle prit ses drapeaux et ses canons.
- **translation**: On our extreme left, in front of the Santon, Marshal Lannes had fought Prince Bagration's corps brilliantly; he had driven it from Pozorzitz and forced it back toward Holubitz, as Kutuzov himself admits in his report. On Lannes's right our cavalry under Murat had taken several batteries [...] until General Kellermann decided the victory by a fortunate and destructive ambush. The 1st division of the fifth corps had contested Blasowitz with the Grand Duke Constantine's reserve, which it shattered in several successive clashes, taking its colours and its guns.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: In Thiébault the fight with Bagration (driven from Pozorzitz toward Holubitz) and the fight for Blasowitz (Lannes's 1st division against the Grand Duke's reserve) are two separate actions; 'Pozorzitz' names Bagration's position.
- **does not settle**: Which division was 'la 1re division du cinquième corps' on 2 December (the footnote on p. 463 does not say; register §7.4, §7.6); an hour.
- **bears on**: EVENTS[#blasowitz]; FEATURES[#blasowitz].facts; FEATURES[#posthouse].

### C6.15 `davout1885.no123.p192-193.plus-belle-resistance`

- **source**: `davout1885`, t. I, no. 123 'AU MINISTRE DE LA GUERRE, major général', 'A Menitz, 11 frimaire an XIV (2 décembre 1805)', pp. 192-193, leaves n257-n258 (read on resized crops); images: https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n257.jpg, https://archive.org/download/correspondanced03davogoog/page/n258.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 193) Le village, que le 3e régiment avait été obligé de quitter après la plus belle résistance, a été attaqué et emporté par le général Heudelet, à la tête du 108e régiment et de quelques compagnies du 15e régiment d'infanterie légère; mais des forces supérieures de l'ennemi l'ont repris de nouveau. / L'ennemi a manœuvré pour m'envelopper en débouchant avec de l'artillerie par Sokolnitz, que j'ai fait attaquer tout de suite par les cinq régiments de la division par échelons. Ce village a été pris et repris plusieurs fois [...] / J'ai eu à combattre pendant la grande majorité de la journée sur mon front et sur mes flancs des colonnes extrêmement fortes. / Tous les corps ont manœuvré avec sang-froid, sous un feu très-vif, et se sont mêlés plusieurs fois avec l'ennemi; ils ont beaucoup souffert. — Editor's note (1), pp. 192-193: La division Friant arrivait à marches forcées, et la division Gudin, la plus éloignée, ne pouvait arriver tout au plus que pendant l'action
- **translation**: The village [Telnitz], which the 3rd regiment had been forced to leave after the finest resistance, was attacked and carried by General Heudelet at the head of the 108th and some companies of the 15th light; but superior enemy forces retook it. The enemy manoeuvred to envelop me, debouching with artillery by Sokolnitz, which I had attacked at once by the division's five regiments in echelon. This village was taken and retaken several times [...] For the great majority of the day I had to fight, on my front and on my flanks, extremely strong columns. All the corps manoeuvred coolly under a very lively fire and closed with the enemy several times; they suffered much. — Editor: Friant's division was arriving by forced marches, and Gudin's, the furthest, could arrive at best during the action
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Davout's own judgements, attributable: the 3e de ligne left Telnitz 'après la plus belle résistance'; he fought 'des colonnes extrêmement fortes' most of the day; Sokolnitz 'pris et repris plusieurs fois'. The 1885 editor says only 'à marches forcées' (grade C for the note).
- **does not settle**: That Telnitz was the day's hardest fight; a number for the odds; a reputation of the march.
- **bears on**: PHASES[1].events[0]; FEATURES[#telnitz].story; FORMATIONS.c_iii.role; FEATURES[#viennaroad].story; FEATURES[#sokolnitz].why[0].

Grade A for the dated report, C for the 1885 editor's note: correct. 'pris et repris plusieurs fois' is Sokolnitz. Not read by a person.

### C6.16 `thiers1845.p296.marche-etonnante`

- **source**: `thiers1847`, t. VI, livre XXIII, p. 296, leaf n305 (read on a resized copy); images: https://archive.org/download/histoireduconsul06thieuoft/page/n305_w1100.jpg.
- **locator, as the source skeptic gives it**: Thiers, Histoire du Consulat et de l'Empire t. VI, Paris, Paulin, 1847 (key thiers1847), livre XXIII, p. 296, leaf n305
- **quote** (read on: page image): Il avait ordonné au maréchal Davout de porter la division Friant, et, s'il était possible, la division Gudin, vers l'abbaye de Gross-Raigern, placée sur la route de Vienne à Brünn, à la hauteur des étangs. [...] Le général Friant, seul averti à temps, parce que le général Gudin se trouvait plus loin vers Presbourg, était parti sur-le-champ, et, en quarante-huit heures, avait parcouru les trente-six lieues qui séparent Vienne de Gross-Raigern. Les soldats tombaient quelquefois sur la route, épuisés de fatigue; [...] Le 1er décembre au soir, ils bivouaquaient, par un froid rigoureux, à Gross-Raigern, à une lieue et demie du champ de bataille. Jamais troupe à pied n'a exécuté une marche aussi étonnante, car c'est une marche de dix-huit lieues par journée, pendant deux jours de suite.
- **translation**: He had ordered Marshal Davout to bring Friant's division and, if possible, Gudin's toward the abbey of Gross-Raigern on the Vienna-Brünn road, level with the ponds. [...] General Friant, alone warned in time, since Gudin was further off toward Pressburg, had left at once and in forty-eight hours had covered the thirty-six leagues between Vienna and Gross-Raigern. The soldiers sometimes fell on the road, exhausted [...] On the evening of 1 December they bivouacked in bitter cold at Gross-Raigern, a league and a half from the battlefield. Never did troops on foot make so astonishing a march: eighteen leagues a day for two days running.
- **grade**: C. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: An account that makes the judgement on the Vienna march: Thiers, 'Jamais troupe à pied n'a exécuté une marche aussi étonnante'.
- **does not settle**: The distance and time (Thiers: 36 lieues in 48 hours; the map: about 113 km in 40-46 hours, 'sources vary'); that the march was 'celebrated' (a reputation no page read records).
- **bears on**: FEATURES[#viennaroad].story; FEATURES[#viennaroad].facts; PHASES[1].events[2].

### C6.17 `thiers1845.p310.seven-eight-thousand`

- **source**: `thiers1847`, t. VI, p. 310, leaf n319; images: https://archive.org/download/histoireduconsul06thieuoft/page/n319_w1100.jpg.

En effet la division Friant, par la marche de trente-six lieues qu'elle avait exécutée, était réduite à 6 mille hommes au plus, et avec le 3e de ligne ne faisait pas plus de 7 à 8 mille combattants. Indeed Friant's division, after its march of thirty-six leagues, was reduced to 6 thousand at most and with the 3rd of the line made no more than 7 to 8 thousand combatants. [...] During this fierce fight on our right

### C6.18 `thiers1845.p317-318.blaziowitz-levered`

- **source**: `thiers1847`, t. VI, pp. 317-318, leaves n326-n327; images: https://archive.org/download/histoireduconsul06thieuoft/page/n326_w1100.jpg, https://archive.org/download/histoireduconsul06thieuoft/page/n327_w1100.jpg.
- **locator, as the source skeptic gives it**: thiers1847, t. VI (1847), pp. 317-318, leaves n326-n327
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 317) On marche ensuite sur le village de Blaziowitz, qui était à droite de la plaine, là où le terrain commence à s'élever vers Pratzen. Ce village, comme tous ceux du pays, profondément encaissé dans un ravin, ne se faisait voir que par la flamme qui le dévorait. Un détachement de la garde impériale russe l'avait occupé le matin, en attendant la cavalerie du prince de Lichtenstein. Lannes ordonne au 13e léger de s'en emparer. Le colonel Castex, qui commandait le 13e, s'avance avec le premier bataillon, en colonne d'attaque, et tandis / (p. 318) qu'il arrive sur le village, est frappé d'une balle au front. Le bataillon s'élance, et venge à coup de baïonnette la mort de son colonel. On s'empare de Blaziowitz, et on y ramasse quelques centaines de prisonniers [...] Blaziowitz pris, Lannes fait enlever Holubitz et Kruch, villages placés le long de la route d'Olmütz, et parvient à joindre l'infanterie de Bagration. En ce moment il rompt la ligne formée par ses deux divisions. Il porte la division Suchet obliquement à gauche, la division Caffarelli obliquement à droite. Par ce mouvement divergent, il sépare l'infanterie de Bagration de la cavalerie du prince de Lichtenstein, rejette la première à la gauche de la route d'Olmütz, la seconde à la droite vers les pentes du plateau de Pratzen.
- **translation**: Then they march on the village of Blaziowitz, on the right of the plain where the ground begins to rise toward Pratzen. This village, deep in a ravine like all those of the country, showed itself only by the flames consuming it. A detachment of the Russian imperial guard had occupied it in the morning, awaiting Liechtenstein's cavalry. Lannes orders the 13th light to take it. Colonel Castex, commanding the 13th, advances with the first battalion in attack column and, as he reaches the village, is struck in the forehead by a ball. The battalion charges and avenges its colonel with the bayonet. Blaziowitz is taken, with a few hundred prisoners [...] Blaziowitz taken, Lannes has Holubitz and Kruch, villages along the Olmütz road, carried, and reaches Bagration's infantry. He then breaks the line formed by his two divisions, carrying Suchet's obliquely to the left and Caffarelli's obliquely to the right. By this divergent movement he separates Bagration's infantry from Liechtenstein's cavalry, throws the first to the left of the Olmütz road, the second to the right toward the slopes of the Pratzen plateau.
- **grade**: C. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: The narrative behind EVENTS[#blasowitz] as the map has it ('Blasowitz falls and Bagration is levered off the army'; forms caffarelli, suchet, bag, lich) is Thiers's sequence (1845): Lannes takes Blasowitz (the 13e léger; that it was Caffarelli's division is an inference), then Holubitz and Kruch, then splits Bagration from Liechtenstein with Suchet and Caffarelli.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: 'The narrative behind EVENTS[#blasowitz] ... is Thiers's sequence (1845)': should read 'matches Thiers's sequence (1847)'.
- **does not settle**: The hour; the earlier accounts (A, B) do not tie Blasowitz's fall to Bagration's separation (Stutterheim p. 71; Schönhals p. 165).
- **bears on**: EVENTS[#blasowitz]; FEATURES[#blasowitz].facts; ANALYSIS[#north]; world.js VILLAGES Krug.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 2 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept with corrections. pp. 317-318 confirmed; exact. Source date: the title page of histoireduconsul06thieuoft (leaf n8, read) prints 'TOME SIXIÈME / PARIS / PAULIN, LIBRAIRE-ÉDITEUR / 60, RUE RICHELIEU / 1847'; its verso (n7) 'Ce volume a été déposé au Ministère de l'Intérieur ... le 4 janvier 1847'. Key thiers1847, not thiers1845; grade C unchanged. 'settles' overreaches in saying the map's event 'is Thiers's sequence': it matches Thiers's sequence; that the map took it from Thiers is not shown. Same page 317: Valhubert wounded and 'le groupe de tambours du premier régiment de Caffarelli' just before Blaziowitz (Caffarelli's division in the action is Thiers's; he does not name the 13e léger's division). ### C6.19 `materialien1806.p102-103.disposition-turas`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, Zusatz 7 (Weyrother's disposition), pp. 102-103, leaves n111-n112; images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n111_w1000.jpg, https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n112_w1000.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 102) besonders wenn wir die Dörfer Kobelnitz und Sokolnitz im Besitze haben, wodurch wir dem Feinde zugleich in die Flanke fallen und ihn auf der Fläche zwischen Schlapanitz und dem Turasser Walde verfolgen können, indem wir den Defileen von Schlapanitz und Bellowitz ausweichen, welche die feindliche Fronte decken. / „Zu diesem Endzwecke ist es nöthig, die Attake so früh als möglich gegen den rechten Flügel anzufangen. [...] eben so die Cavallerie unter Fürst Liechtenstein, die eine vortheilhafte Stellung zwischen Kruh und Schlapanitz auf beiden Seiten der großen Straße nehmen muß. [...] „Die erste Colonne marschirt links ab von dem Dorfe Aujest über Tellnitz. Nachdem sie sich in den Besitz des letzteren Dorfs gesetzt hat, commandirt sie ein Bataillon ab, welches sich jenseits des Defilees zur Deckung ihres linken Flügels stellt. Die Colonne selbst marschirt rechts vor gegen die Teiche, bis die Tete der zweiten Colonne in gleicher Höhe ist. (p. 103) „Dergestalt formiren die vier Colonnen eine große Fronte, wobei die erste Colonne mit drei Bataillons den Turasschen Wald besetzt, inzwischen die übrigen Bataillons mit Entschlossenheit den feindlichen rechten Flügel attakiren. [...] „Da der glückliche Erfolg der Schlacht von dem entschlossenen Angriffe unsers linken Flügels auf den rechten des Feindes abhängt
- **translation**: especially if we hold Kobelnitz and Sokolnitz, by which we fall at once on the enemy's flank and can pursue him on the plain between Schlapanitz and the Turas wood, avoiding the defiles of Schlapanitz and Bellowitz, which cover the enemy front. To this end the attack on the right wing must begin as early as possible. [...] likewise the cavalry under Prince Liechtenstein, which must take an advantageous position between Kruh and Schlapanitz on both sides of the high road. [...] The first column marches off by the left from the village of Aujest over Tellnitz. Having taken that village, it details a battalion to stand beyond the defile covering its left flank. The column itself advances to the right toward the ponds until the head of the second column is level. In this way the four columns form one great front, the first column occupying the Turas wood with three battalions while the other battalions attack the enemy's right wing with resolution. [...] Since the battle's happy outcome depends on the resolute attack of our left wing on the enemy's right
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: In the disposition Telnitz is the I Column's first village (taken, then the column turns right toward the ponds); the objective beyond is the Turas WOOD ('den Turasschen Wald', three battalions of the I Column) and the plain between it and Schlapanitz, not the village of Turas; Kruh (Krug) beside Schlapanitz on the high road is Liechtenstein's ground; Bellowitz is a defile beside Schlapanitz's.
- **does not settle**: Where the Turas wood lay; 'the first objective of the entire Allied plan' in those words.
- **bears on**: PLANS.al.cols[1].ord; FEATURES[#telnitz].why[1]; world.js VILLAGES Turas, Krug, Bellowitz.
- **status**: read by the cluster's researcher (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader) on 2 freshly downloaded images: quotation exact, accept. pp. 102-103 confirmed; exact ('Turasschen', 'Turasser' as printed). p. 102 also has 'formiren sich vier Colonnen, wie gestern'. Not read by a person.

### C6.20 `materialien1806.p105.retraite-kobelnitz`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, Zusatz 7, p. 105, leaf n114; images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n114_w1000.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): so müssen die drei Bataillons, die den Turasschen Wald besetzt haben, ihn nicht verlassen, damit er im Fall eines nicht glücklichen Erfolgs zur Retraite dienen könne, wenn man gezwungen wäre sich durch Kobelnitz und Puntowitz in unsere erste Position zu retiriren. / „Wenn der Fürst Bagration die Annäherung unsers linken Flügels bemerkt, so muß er suchen, den feindlichen rechten Flügel zu schlagen und eine Communication mit den übrigen Colonnen herzustellen. Die Vereinigung der ganzen Armee ist vor dem Dorfe Latein zwischen Lesch und Rennowitz. Die Cavallerie des Fürsten Liechtenstein benutzt die Reinigung der Defileen von Schlapanitz, Bellowitz und Kritschen
- **translation**: the three battalions occupying the Turas wood must not leave it, so that in case of an unhappy outcome it may serve for the retreat, if we were forced to retire through Kobelnitz and Puntowitz to our first position. If Prince Bagration notices the approach of our left wing he must try to beat the enemy's right wing and open communication with the other columns. The whole army unites before the village of Latein between Lesch and Rennowitz. Liechtenstein's cavalry uses the clearing of the defiles of Schlapanitz, Bellowitz and Kritschen
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The only retreat the disposition orders for the left is through the Turas wood, Kobelnitz and Puntowitz to the first position: not over the Augezd defile. 'The only ordered line of retreat' / 'the only ordered way out' at Augezd is contradicted by the order read.
- **settles, as the source skeptic limits it**: 'The only retreat the disposition orders for the left' omits the army's worst-case line on p. 106; 'contradicted by the order read' should be 'not supported by the disposition read'.
- **does not settle**: Whether orders given during the day named Augezd (none read).

'settles' overreaches: the same disposition (p. 106, read) orders a second retreat, 'Im allerübelsten Fall retirirt die Armee auf Niemtschan, Hodjegitz und Herspitz'. Correct: the disposition names two retreats (the left's through the Turas wood, Kobelnitz and Puntowitz to the first position; the whole army's worst case on Niemtschan, Hodjegitz and Herspitz); neither names Augezd. 'The only ordered line of retreat ... Augezd' is not supported by the disposition; 'contradicted' holds for the disposition only, not for orders given during the day (none read; M-D's Kutuzov order to Buxhöwden 'отступать съ первыми тремя колоннами' names no route, OCR only). ### C6.21 `materialien1806.p106.reserve-guard-retreat`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, Zusatz 7, p. 106, leaf n115; images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n115_w1000.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): — Aus der zweiten Colonne zwischen dem Turasser Walde und Schlapanitz muß er vier kleine Colonnen hinter der zweiten Linie formiren, welche während der ganzen Schlacht die Reserve bilden. / „Das Corps des Großfürsten Constantin Pawlowitz, kais. Hoh. besetzt bei Tages-Anbruche die Position hinter Blasowitz und Kruh und dient zur Verstärkung der Cavallerie des Fürsten Liechtenstein und des linken Flügels der Avantgarde des Fürsten Bagration. [...] „Im allerübelsten Fall retirirt die Armee auf Niemtschan, Hodjegitz und Herspitz. [...] „Alle fünf Colonnen rücken um 7 Uhr aus
- **translation**: From the second column, between the Turas wood and Schlapanitz, he must form four small columns behind the second line, which form the reserve throughout the battle. The corps of the Grand Duke Constantine occupies at daybreak the position behind Blasowitz and Kruh and serves to reinforce Liechtenstein's cavalry and the left wing of Bagration's advance guard. [...] In the worst case the army retires on Niemtschan, Hodjegitz and Herspitz. [...] All five columns march off at 7
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: (1) The disposition makes the Guard a support for Liechtenstein and Bagration's left, behind Blasowitz and Kruh, and names a separate 'Reserve' of four small columns from the 2nd column: 'The only Allied reserve' is not the disposition's wording (Stutterheim p. 56 calls the Guard 'die Reserve des rechten Flügels', register §2.7); (2) the worst-case retreat is east, 'auf Niemtschan, Hodjegitz und Herspitz'; (3) Kruh lies beside Blasowitz.
- **does not settle**: Whether the four small reserve columns were formed on the day.

### C6.22 `materialien1806.p118-119.einzige-trophaee`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, Zusatz 13 ('ein officieller, ungedruckter Bericht'), pp. 118-119, leaves n127-n128; images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n127_w1000.jpg, https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n128_w1000.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 118) bei welcher Gelegenheit die beiden Escadrons einen Adler (die einzige Trophäe des Tags) des 4ten Linien-Regiments eroberten. — Kaum waren die Garden auf der anbefohlnen Anhöhe von Krzenowitz aufmarschirt, als der Feind mit dreifacher Uebermacht gegen die Fronte und beide Flanken anrückte [...] (p. 119) [...] die sich Zurückziehenden fanden daher sehr glücklich diese formirte Truppe, schlossen sich an selbe an und nahmen eine Stellung zwischen Krzenowitz und Austerlitz, an welcher sich links die kais. östreichische Cavallerie und rechts Fürst Bagration anschloß. — Der Feind marschirte auf den gegen über liegenden Anhöhen auf und formirte eine Linie von dem Rausnitzer Posthause bis Klein-Hostjeradek. [...] „Erobert wurde ein einziger Adler und gegen 200 Gefangene gemacht.
- **translation**: on which occasion the two squadrons [of the Garde à cheval] took an eagle (the only trophy of the day) of the 4th line regiment. Hardly had the Guards deployed on the ordered height of Krzenowitz when the enemy advanced with threefold superiority against the front and both flanks [...] the retreating troops were glad to find this formed body, joined it and took a position between Krzenowitz and Austerlitz, with the Austrian cavalry on the left and Bagration on the right. The enemy deployed on the heights opposite and formed a line from the Rausnitz post house to Klein-Hostjeradek. [...] A single eagle was taken and about 200 prisoners.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: An Allied report counts one eagle, of the 4th line, 'die einzige Trophäe des Tags', 'ein einziger Adler', taken by two squadrons of the Garde à cheval: a source for 'the only eagle' in the Allied count, and for the Horse Guard's credit (with Stutterheim p. 69 and Schönhals p. 164). It also names a 'Rausnitzer Posthaus' at the north end of the French afternoon line (whether the same building as the Posoritz post house is not said: uncertain).
- **does not settle**: The French count of eagles lost (not read); whether the 24e léger also lost one (Schönhals p. 164: 'nahm seinen Adler', singular).

(*) [...] (*) Въ составѣ гвардіи были полки: Преображенскій . . . 2 батал. Семеновскій . . . 2 — Измайловскій . . . 2 — Гвардейскихъ егерей . . . 1 — Лейбъ-гренадерскій . . . 3 — Итого . . 10 батал. (p. 145) Кавалергардскій . . . 5 эскадр. Лейбъ-гвардіи Конный . . . 5 — — Гусарскій . . . 5 — — Казачій . . . 2 — батальонъ артиллеріи. - **translation**: Two days later the Guard arrived at Olmütz under the Tsesarevich Constantine Pavlovich, 8,500 men under arms. ### C6.c1 `kavalergard1851.p56-57.malutin-blazovits`

- **source**: `kavalergard1851`, pp. 56-57, leaves n66-n67; images: https://archive.org/download/istorijakavalergardovikavalergardskogoee45/page/n66_w800.jpg, https://archive.org/download/istorijakavalergardovikavalergardskogoee45/page/n67_w800.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 56) Настало роковое утро 20-го Ноября. Въ 8-мъ часу утра, когда долины были еще покрыты туманомъ, Кинмейеръ первый началъ наступленіе. [...] (p. 57) Переночевавъ съ частію своего отряда впереди Аустерлица и получивъ приказаніе составлять резервъ Князя Лихтенштейна, Цесаревичъ Константинъ Павловичъ выступилъ въ назначенный по диспозиціи часъ, со всею своею пѣхотою, кромѣ Лейбъ-гренадеровъ, и съ двумя полками Гвардейской кавалеріи: Коннымъ и Гусарскимъ. Лейбъ-Гренадерскій полкъ, Кавалергарды, Лейбъ-Казаки и часть Гвардейской Пѣшей Артиллеріи, составлявшіе особый отрядъ, подъ начальствомъ Генералъ-Лейтенанта Малютина, оставались на мѣстахъ своего ночлега и не помышляли быть участниками сраженія. [...] Пока происходило движеніе Гвардіи, Наполеонъ воспользовался неприбытіемъ Лихтенштейна, и въ оставленный для него промежутокъ послалъ часть своей пѣхоты и кавалеріи, которыя и заняли безпрепятственно это пространство, вмѣстѣ съ лежащимъ на немъ селеніемъ Блазовицемъ.
- **translation**: (p. 56) The fateful morning of 20 November came. In the eighth hour of the morning, while the valleys were still covered in fog, Kienmayer was the first to begin the advance. [...] (p. 57) Having spent the night with part of his detachment in front of Austerlitz and been ordered to form Prince Liechtenstein's reserve, the Tsesarevich Constantine Pavlovich set out at the hour set by the disposition with all his infantry except the Life Grenadiers, and with two regiments of Guard cavalry: the Horse [Guard] and the Hussars. The Life Grenadier regiment, the Chevalier Guards, the Life Cossacks and part of the Guard foot artillery, forming a separate detachment under Lieutenant-General Malyutin, remained at their bivouac and did not think to take part in the battle. [...] While the Guard was moving, Napoleon used Liechtenstein's failure to arrive and sent into the gap left for him part of his infantry and cavalry, which occupied that space without hindrance, together with the village of Blasowitz lying in it.
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: In the Chevalier Guard's regimental history: (1) on 2 December the Guard cavalry marching with the Tsesarevich was the Horse Guard and the Guard Hussars; the Chevalier Guard, Life Cossacks and Life Grenadiers were a separate detachment under Malyutin; (2) the French occupied Blasowitz 'безпрепятственно' (without hindrance): a version against Stutterheim p. 68 and Schönhals p. 164 (the Guard jägers held and lost it); (3) Kienmayer began 'въ 8-мъ часу утра' (between seven and eight).
- **does not settle**: Who commanded the Horse and Hussar regiments' column on the day (Kologrivov is not named on these pages); the hour of Blasowitz.
- **bears on**: C6 item 8: FORMATIONS.rg_cav.commander, strengthNote, track[6].act ('The Chevalier Guard's charge'); SOURCE_NOTE.body[5]; C6 item 4: EVENTS[#blasowitz], FEATURES[#blasowitz].facts, ANALYSIS[#north]; C6 items 1.1/5: PHASES[1].events[0], TOUR[3].x.
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

### C6.c2 `kavalergard1851.p58-59.horse-guard-eagle-depreradovich`

- **source**: `kavalergard1851`, pp. 58-59, leaves n68-n69 (p. 58 foot read on a crop); images: https://archive.org/download/istorijakavalergardovikavalergardskogoee45/page/n68_w800.jpg, https://archive.org/download/istorijakavalergardovikavalergardskogoee45/page/n69_w800.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 58) Конная Гвардія бли- / (p. 59) стательною атакою освободила пѣхоту и взяла одинъ изъ Наполеоновыхъ орловъ. [...] Отрядъ Генералъ-Лейтенанта Малютина, имѣвшій въ своемъ составѣ Кавалергардскій полкъ, стоялъ, какъ выше упомянуто, за Аустерлицемъ, и ожидалъ смотра. Услышавъ пушечные выстрѣлы, онъ выступилъ съ мѣста ночлега, и прошедъ Аустерлицъ, получилъ приказаніе Цесаревича подкрѣпить сражавшуюся Гвардію. По невозможности поспѣть всему отряду одновременно къ мѣсту боя, до котораго оставалось еще семь верстъ, Малютинъ послалъ впередъ Депрерадовича, со всѣми пятью эскадронами Кавалергардовъ и съ тремя взводами Лейбъ-Казаковъ
- **translation**: The Horse Guard by a brilliant attack freed the infantry and took one of Napoleon's eagles. [...] Lieutenant-General Malyutin's detachment, which included the Chevalier Guard regiment, stood, as mentioned above, beyond Austerlitz awaiting a review. Hearing the cannon it left its bivouac and, having passed Austerlitz, received the Tsesarevich's order to support the fighting Guard. As the whole detachment could not reach the field together, still seven versts away, Malyutin sent Depreradovich ahead with all five squadrons of Chevalier Guards and three platoons of Life Cossacks
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: The Chevalier Guard's own history credits the eagle to the Horse Guard ('Конная Гвардія ... взяла одинъ изъ Наполеоновыхъ орловъ'), against Marbot p. 260's 'chevaliers-gardes et cuirassiers'; the Chevalier Guard came up under Depreradovich, sent by Malyutin, not under Kologrivov.
- **does not settle**: 'The only eagle' (it says 'one of'); the hour of the charges.
- **bears on**: C6 item 3a: PHASES[6].lede, FEATURES[#vinohrady].story, ANALYSIS[#guard].text, EVENTS[#guard-attack].why, FORMATIONS.rg_cav.track[6].act; C6 item 8: FORMATIONS.rg_cav.commander, strengthNote.

### C6.c3 `materialien1806.p118-119.malutin-chevaliergarde`

- **source**: `materialien1806`, Zusatz 13, pp. 118-119, leaves n127-n128; images: https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n127_w1200.jpg, https://archive.org/download/10807964bsb/page/n128_w1200.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 118) „Generallieutenant Malutin, der während dem Marsche der Garden von der russischen Gränze an, die 3te aus 3 Bataillons Grenadier und 5 Escadrons / (p. 119) Chevalier-Garde bestehende Colonne commandirte, war am Tage vor der Schlacht in Butschowitz und bei dem Rückzuge durch das Dorf Krzenowitz so eben bei diesem Orte angekommen; die sich Zurückziehenden fanden daher sehr glücklich diese formirte Truppe, schlossen sich an selbe an
- **translation**: Lieutenant-General Malyutin, who from the Russian frontier on had commanded the 3rd column of the Guards' march, of 3 grenadier battalions and 5 squadrons of Chevalier Guard, was on the day before the battle at Butschowitz and had just arrived at Krzenowitz at the time of the retreat through that village; the retreating troops were therefore glad to find this formed body and joined it
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: The Allied report printed in 1806 puts the Chevalier Guard's five squadrons in Malyutin's column, arriving at Krzenowitz only during the retreat; with Kavalergard 1851 pp. 57-59 and Sokolov p. 200 (Malyutin's 2nd Guard column), the Chevalier Guard was not under Kologrivov on the day.
- **does not settle**: How this agrees with the same report's p. 118 '2 Escadrons, nämlich 2 von Chevaliers und 2 Gardes à cheval' in action before Krzenowitz (an internal tension of the report: keep both); who commanded the Horse Guard and Hussars.

### C6.c4 `mikhailovsky1844.p194.only-trophy-olenin`

- **source**: `mikhailovsky1844`, p. 194, IIIF k205 (hOCR ordinal 206); images: https://iiif.archive.org/iiif/1805-.-bmk-brz$205/full/1000,/0/default.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Конная гвардія освободила пѣхоту отъ непріятельскихъ всадниковъ. Первые два эскадрона, подъ начальствомъ Полковника Оленина, ударили во флангъ Французамъ, обратили ихъ назадъ, и въ преслѣдованіи наскакали на батальонъ 4-го линейнаго полка, изрубили его и взяли знамя — единственный трофей Союзниковъ въ Аустерлицкомъ сраженіи. [...] Командиръ Кавалергардскаго полка, Депрерадовичъ приказалъ первымъ двумъ эскадронамъ атаковать Французовъ въ правый флангъ
- **translation**: The Horse Guard freed the infantry from the enemy's horsemen. The first two squadrons, under Colonel Olenin, struck the French in the flank, turned them back and, in the pursuit, fell on a battalion of the 4th line regiment, cut it down and took its standard, the Allies' only trophy at the battle of Austerlitz. [...] The commander of the Chevalier Guard regiment, Depreradovich, ordered the first two squadrons to attack the French in the right flank
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: A second Allied count (after Materialien Zusatz 13) calls the 4th Line's standard 'единственный трофей Союзниковъ'; credits the Horse Guard's first two squadrons under Olenin; names Depreradovich commanding the Chevalier Guard's attack. Kologrivov is not named in the charge.
- **does not settle**: The French count of eagles lost; M-D writes 'знамя' (standard), not 'орелъ'.

### C6.c5 `stutterheim1806de.p69.adler-rueckzug`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, p. 69, leaf n72; images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n72_w1200.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): Um der Infanterie Luft zu machen, that das Garde-Regiment zu Pferd des Großfürsten einen Anfall auf die Flanke der Franzosen, hielt selbst die Cavallerie auf, warf sie, und griff die feindliche Infanterie an, welche zur Unterstützung der Cavallerie vorgerückt war. Bey dieser Gelegenheit eroberte das Garde-Regiment zu Pferd einen Französischen Adler von einem Bataillon des vierten Regiments. [...] Hierauf erfolgte der Rückzug der Russischen Garden nach Austerlitz, ohne dabey von den Franzosen beunruhigt zu werden, welche auf den Anhöhen vorwärts von Blasowitz stehen blieben.
- **translation**: To give the infantry air, the Grand Duke's Horse Guard regiment made an attack on the French flank, stopped their cavalry, threw it, and attacked the enemy infantry that had come up to support the cavalry. On this occasion the Horse Guard regiment took a French eagle from a battalion of the fourth regiment. [...] Then followed the retreat of the Russian Guards to Austerlitz, without being troubled by the French, who stayed on the heights in front of Blasowitz.
- **grade**: A. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: The 1806 eyewitness credits the eagle of a battalion of the 4th to the Horse Guard regiment (register §2.5, re-read); the Russian Guard retreated to Austerlitz unmolested: against FORMATIONS.bag.role's 'The only large Allied formation to come off the field in order'.
- **does not settle**: 'Only' (no count here); the hour.
- **bears on**: C6 item 3a; C6 item 3c: FORMATIONS.bag.role; C6 item 4: EVENTS[#blasowitz].
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

### C6.c6 `stutterheim1806de.p56.reserve-blasowitz`

- **source**: `stutterheim1806de`, p. 56, leaf n59; images: https://archive.org/download/11344011bsb/page/n59_w1200.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): mit der Division Rivaud auf seinem linken und der Division Drouet auf seinem rechten Flügel, und nahm seine Richtung nach den Anhöhen bey Blasowitz. [...] Der Großfürst Constantin, welcher mit dem Corps der Garden die Reserve des rechten Flügels zu formiren hatte, verließ zur bestimmten Stunde die Anhöhen vor Austerlitz, um auf die Anhöhen bey Blasowitz und Krug vorzurücken. Kaum war er auf diesem Puncte angelangt, als er sogleich auf die Plänkler von der Division Rivaud und von der leichten Cavallerie des Fürsten Murat unter Commando des General Kellermann stieß, und mit ihnen ins Gefecht verwickelt war. Der Großfürst ließ eilig das Dorf Blasowitz durch das Jäger-Bataillon der Garde besetzen.
- **translation**: [Bernadotte] with Rivaud's division on his left and Drouet's on his right wing, took his direction toward the heights by Blasowitz. [...] The Grand Duke Constantine, who was to form the reserve of the right wing with the Guard corps, left the heights in front of Austerlitz at the appointed hour to advance onto the heights by Blasowitz and Krug. Hardly had he arrived there when he ran into the skirmishers of Rivaud's division and the light cavalry of Prince Murat under General Kellermann, and was engaged with them. The Grand Duke hastily had the village of Blasowitz occupied by the Guard jäger battalion.
- **grade**: A. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: Stutterheim's own words for the Guard as 'die Reserve des rechten Flügels' (3d; register §2.7 re-read); Bernadotte's corps advancing on the Blasowitz heights with Rivaud left and Drouet right; Kellermann's horse and Rivaud's skirmishers engaged the Grand Duke on the height, the jägers then put into the village.
- **does not settle**: Which of Bernadotte's divisions took the village (p. 68 says only 'die Truppen des Corps des Marschall Bernadotte'); that Kellermann attacked the village itself.
- **bears on**: C6 item 3d: FORMATIONS.constantine.role; C6 item 4: EVENTS[#blasowitz].forms (rivaud, drouet, kellermann).
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

### C6.c7 `marbot1891.p259-260.blasiowitz-aigle-clef`

- **source**: `marbot1891`, t. I (27th ed.), pp. 259-260, leaves n278-n279; images: https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugn01marbuoft/page/n278_w1200.jpg, https://archive.org/download/mmoiresdugn01marbuoft/page/n279_w1200.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 259) Nous avons vu que l'Empereur avait montré peu de troupes à sa droite; c'était un piège qu'il tendait aux ennemis, afin qu'ils eussent la possibilité de prendre facilement Telnitz [...] Mais à notre gauche, le maréchal Lannes non seulement / (p. 260) repoussa toutes les attaques des ennemis contre le Santon, mais il les rejeta de l'autre côté de la route d'Olmütz jusqu'à Blasiowitz, où le terrain, devenant plus uni, permit à la cavalerie de Murat d'exécuter plusieurs charges brillantes, dont le résultat fut immense, car les Russes furent menés tambour battant jusqu'au village d'Austerlitz. [...] Le maréchal Soult enlève non seulement le village de Pratzen, mais encore l'immense plateau de ce nom qui était le point culminant de toute la contrée, et par conséquent la clef du champ de bataille. Là s'engagea, sous les yeux de l'Empereur, un combat des plus vifs, dans lequel les Russes furent battus. Mais un bataillon du 4e de ligne, dont le prince Joseph, frère de Napoléon, était colonel, se laissant emporter trop loin à la poursuite des ennemis, fut chargé et enfoncé par les chevaliers-gardes et les cuirassiers du grand-duc Constantin, frère d'Alexandre, qui lui enlevèrent son aigle!...
- **translation**: We have seen that the Emperor had shown few troops on his right; it was a trap he set the enemy, so that they could take Telnitz easily [...] But on our left Marshal Lannes not only repulsed all the enemy's attacks on the Santon, but threw them back across the Olmütz road as far as Blasiowitz, where the ground, becoming more even, let Murat's cavalry make several brilliant charges, with an immense result, for the Russians were driven at the double as far as the village of Austerlitz. [...] Marshal Soult carries not only the village of Pratzen but the immense plateau of that name, the highest point of the whole country and therefore the key of the battlefield. There, under the Emperor's eyes, began one of the liveliest fights, in which the Russians were beaten. But a battalion of the 4th of the line, whose colonel was Prince Joseph, Napoleon's brother, carried too far in pursuit, was charged and broken by the Chevalier Guards and the Grand Duke Constantine's cuirassiers, who took its eagle!...
- **grade**: B. **label**: disputed.
- **settles**: Marbot's versions, attributable: (1) Telnitz left weakly held as a trap 'afin qu'ils eussent la possibilité de prendre facilement Telnitz' (a side for FEATURES[#telnitz].story's 'a flank Napoleon intended to lose slowly' and why[1]); (2) Lannes threw the Russians from the Santon back across the Olmütz road 'jusqu'à Blasiowitz' (a fourth Blasowitz version, linking Bagration's front and Blasowitz as the map's event does); (3) the Pratzen plateau 'la clef du champ de bataille' and 'un combat des plus vifs' (attributable judgement for 1.2/1.3/3e); (4) the eagle credited to the Chevalier Guards and cuirassiers.
- **does not settle**: Any hour; a ranking of Telnitz; Marbot's geography (the Russians attacking the Santon thrown back 'jusqu'à Blasiowitz' places Blasowitz beyond the Olmütz road from the Santon, not checked against the map).
- **bears on**: C6 item 4: EVENTS[#blasowitz]; C6 items 1.2/1.3: PHASES[4].lede, FEATURES[#pratzeberg].story; C6 item 3e: FEATURES[#pratzeberg].why[0]; C6 item 3a; C6 items 1.4/3g: FEATURES[#telnitz].story, why[1].
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

### C6.c8 `schoenhals1873.p164.p166.adler-verlust`

- **source**: `schoenhals1873`, p. 164, leaf n168; p. 166, leaf n170; images: https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n168_w1100.jpg, https://archive.org/download/derkriegindeuts00schgoog/page/n170_w1100.jpg.
- **quote** (read on: page image): (p. 164) Das Garde-Regiment zu Pferd des Grossfürsten Constantin, die Blösse der feindlichen Reiterei benützend, griff sie in der Flanke an, warf sie über den Haufen und stürzte sich nun auf die Infanterie, hieb ein Bataillon des 4. Regiments und ein leichtes des 24. Regiments zusammen und nahm seinen Adler. [...] (p. 166) Sein Verlust, der, sowie der ganze russische, nicht genau bekannt ist, soll gegen 3000 Todte und Verwundete, 2000 Gefangene nebst 27 Kanonen betragen haben.
- **translation**: The Grand Duke Constantine's Horse Guard regiment, using the enemy cavalry's exposed flank, attacked it, overthrew it and fell on the infantry, cut up a battalion of the 4th regiment and a light one of the 24th, and took its eagle. [...] His [Bagration's] loss, which like the whole Russian loss is not exactly known, is said to have been about 3,000 dead and wounded, 2,000 prisoners and 27 guns.
- **grade**: B. **label**: fact.
- **settles**: Schönhals credits the Horse Guard with one eagle ('seinen Adler', singular, after naming two battalions); Bagration's corps lost about 3,000 dead and wounded, 2,000 prisoners and 27 guns ('soll ... betragen haben'): against 'came off the field in order' as an unqualified record.
- **does not settle**: Whose eagle 'seinen' refers to grammatically (the 4th's by the other accounts); the loss figure's source.
- **bears on**: C6 item 3a; C6 item 3c: FORMATIONS.bag.role.
- **status**: found and read by the cluster's source skeptic (a machine reader), 14 October 2026; checked by no other reader. Not read by a person.

**The source skeptic's summary** (C6; its counts and "CRn" numbers are its working list's: the verdicts above are the record): First attempt of this skeptic left no notes or partial file; all 29 readings were re-read here on fresh downloads (work/C6-skeptic/dl*), verdicts appended to results/C6-challenge.partial.md as made. Summary: 23 accepted, 6 accepted with corrections, none rejected. Corrections: (1) Thiers t. VI is dated 1847 on its title page (n8) and deposited 4 January 1847 (n7): key thiers1847 for all three Thiers readings and every proposal citing 'Thiers (1845)'; the brief's lead '(1845; ...)' is wrong for t. VI. (2) materialien1806.p105 overreaches: the disposition orders two retreats (p. 106's worst case on Niemtschan, Hodjegitz and Herspitz). (3) thiers p. 317-318: the map's event matches Thiers's sequence; derivation from Thiers is not shown. (4) misprints: Stutterheim p. 50 'uud'; Wolny p. 237 'Giřikowitz'. Missed by the researcher and returned as counter_readings, all read on page images: Kavalergard 1851 pp. 56-59 (Chevalier Guard under Malyutin/Depreradovich, not Kologrivov; Blasowitz occupied without opposition; Kienmayer in the eighth hour; the Horse Guard took the eagle), Materialien pp. 118-119 (Malyutin's column), M-D 1844 p. 194 (the standard 'единственный трофей'; Olenin; Depreradovich), Stutterheim pp. 56 and 69, Marbot pp. 259-260 (the cluster file asked for it; not read by the researcher), Schönhals pp. 164/166. Code checks: redteam.js 182-226 (patterns), 303 (superlative exempted by LABELLED or ATTRIBUTED in its sentence; VERDICT only by LABELLED); 'as \w+ tells it' fails on 'Thiébault' (no u flag), confirmed; '(this map's reading)' and 'In this map's reading' do not match LABELLED. sim-test.js 50-76: nearest named form only, confirmed. chronology.js 508: cites match by prefix. GEOREF.GT.posthouse map [323.8, 36.0] confirmed. Not verified: the researcher's sim-test distances at 11:15 (needs the generated modules); the Geonames Tuřany point; Davout's report of 26 December, M-D 1844 p. 198 and Marbot as C1/C2 read them (beyond pp. 259-260 read here). Schönhals 1873: the leaves carry the signature 'Österr. militär. Zeitschrift. 1874. (Feldzug 1805.)': the text was printed in the ÖMZ; the registered key is left as registered, for the synthesizer to note.

**The researcher's notes** (C6): 1. The overclaim scan's attribution test (redteam.js ATTRIBUTED) matches 'according to', 'as X tells it', 'accounts/memoirs/the Bulletin say...', but NOT 'in X's account' (the cluster file's suggested form). Also 'as \w+ tells it' needs an unaccented name in JS without the u flag ('Thiebault', as FEATURES[#zuran].story already writes it). 'the finest' (Davout's 'la plus belle') is a SUPERLATIVE hit and needs 'according to' in its own sentence. 2. FORMATIONS.c_iii is III Corps' detachment (Friant, Bourcier); Thiébault's '40 ou 50,000 Russes' (register §4.3) concerns Legrand's few battalions in Telnitz and Sokolnitz (IV Corps). The cluster file calls the item 'Legrand's six times'; the role text belongs to III Corps. 3. Blasowitz: the event's present name, forms and ANALYSIS[#north] follow Thiers's 1845 narrative (C), read here. The map plots no Russian Guard detachment at Blasowitz: rg_inf is 1.8-3 km east at 10:00-12:00 (computed on a read-only copy of the live generated modules in the scratch directory). If the owner wants the defenders drawn, that is a track change (data), not proposed here. sim-test's event check takes the nearest named form, so a forms list with kellermann passes at 0.72 km whatever the others' distances. 4. Thiers p. 317 has the village 'profondément encaissé dans un ravin' and burning; Schönhals p. 164 has Napoleon 'seiner Reserve voraus, bei Blaziowitz' (for C3/C4's items). 5. Davout's report of 26 December (C1/C2's reading) has Friant's troops 'marchèrent d'abord sur Turas': with Thiébault's 'jusqu'à Turas' and Schönhals p. 167 'gegen Turas und Kobelnitz', Turas recurs in the accounts as a western reference point; the disposition's objective is the Turas WOOD, whose place is not read: PLANS.al.cols[1].obj [171,372] remains the map's placement. 6. The Allied report (Materialien Zusatz 13, p. 119) names a 'Rausnitzer Posthaus' at the north end of the French afternoon line; whether it is the Posoritz post house is not stated. Pozořice village itself is about 3.8 km NW of the register's 'Stará pošta' (Wolny: Holubitz and Kruh were in Posoritz parish), which explains the name. 7. FORMATIONS.rg_cav: M-D 1844 p. 145 lists the Guard cavalry at Olmütz as Chevalier Guard 5, Horse 5, Hussar 5, Cossack 2 squadrons; Sokolov p. 199 puts only the Horse Guard and Hussars under Kologrivov on 2 December, the Chevalier Guard with Malyutin (OCR of leaf n199). The map's 3,700 horse (Duffy/Smith) and the 'Chevalier Guard's charge' in rg_cav@6 may need the column split noted (C5's strengths cluster). 8. The disposition (Materialien p. 103) also says 'Da der glückliche Erfolg der Schlacht von dem entschlossenen Angriffe unsers linken Flügels ... abhängt': a source for PLANS.al.assumed[1]'s attributed 'decisive ground' (a permanent LANG_ALLOW entry), should it be cited. 9. Wolny 1836 p. 158 (C, local tradition) puts the Russian Guard's near-destruction 'auf der östl. Anhöhe zwischen Kruh, der Holubitzer Mühle bis Křenowitz' and the Emperors on the height 'Zigan' near Holubitz: unsourced topography lore, recorded only. 10. Process: one read-only 'git log -1' was run by mistake while copying the generated modules (no change to the repository); the generated modules (_world_mod.js, _clock.js, _derived.js, _events.js) and data.js/analysis.js/geo.js were copied to the scratch directory to compute positions; nothing under /home/user/Austerlitz was written. 11. Readings by other clusters relied on in notes only (not as basis unless re-read here): C1 savary1828.p204-207 (the 4e's 'une de ses aigles'), marbot1891.p260.eagle, davout1885.p202-204; C2 md1844.p198.bagration.
